follows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.

In particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in
the graph. The true initial distance is exactly

    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)

No arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.
The specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify
the closest minimizer explicitly.

## 4. Entire unit-curvature repaired-cut family: price exactly zero

This is the main complete redundancy result. It works for any n>=1,
any nonnegative word, and any cut j with0<=j<n and h_j>1. Put

    P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t,
    eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T_j)>0,
    p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for0<=i<=j.

Choose an amplitude epsilon>=0. Define two scalar coordinate arrays:

    xB_i=1+sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, gB_i=1        (i<=j),
    xA_i=gA_i=p_i                            (i<=j),

    R_k=sum_(t=k)^(n-1)h_t                    (k>j),
    xB_k=-eta*(1+R_k), gB_k=-eta              (k>j),
    xA_k=p_j*xB_k, gA_k=-eta*p_j              (k>j).

Empty sums have value zero. At the cut,
xB_(j+1)=1-h_j=-eta*(1+T_j), and the same equality multiplied by p_j
holds for coordinate A. Before the cut the A coordinate follows unit
quadratic GD. After it both coordinates have constant gradients. Hence
the vectors

    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),
    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2                            (11)

obey every original GD equation. Include X_star=G_star=F_star=0.

Here are ALL interpolation pairs. For saturated values(11),

    Q_ik=<G_i-G_k,X_k-G_k>.

For a prefix target k<=j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=(sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t,0).

Every prefix source has residual zero. Every tail source has residual
-(1+eta)sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0. The optimum source has residual
-sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0.

For a tail target k>j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=-eta R_k*(1,sqrt(epsilon)*p_j).

Every tail source has residual zero. A prefix source i<=j has residual

    -eta R_k*[1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)].           (12)

The optimum source has residual
-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2)<=0. Every pair into the optimum is zero.
These cases exhaust all directed finite and optimum pairs.

If T_j>0, legality is EXACTLY the finite set of inequalities

    1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)>=0   for i<=j.        (13)

The necessity follows by using target j+1, where R_(j+1)=T_j>0.
If T_j=0, every tail normal vanishes and no condition(13) is necessary.
Zero p_j, zero epsilon, zero intermediate steps and repeated gradients
cause no exception in these formulas. This specifies the full legal
repair-amplitude family, rather than only its most-used endpoint.

For EVERY legal epsilon, the finite zero-cost arcs include

    n->j,
    j->i for every i<j,
    n->k for every j<k<n,
    i->n for every i<n.                                 (14)

The first and last groups hold because X_j=G_j and X_n=G_n. The second
group holds because both sources and targets are prefix samples. The
third holds because all tail gradients coincide. Thus every original
sample is in the zero-cost component of n, so

    d_i=d_i^*=0 for EVERY i,
    Pflow=0.                                           (15)

Equivalently, all feasible value replacements have z_i=z_n. With the
fixed zero optimum, Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=0 also forces z_n=0. Thus ALL
sampled values in(11) are forced, not merely optimal for this objective.

An explicit attaining ORIGINAL flow sends2sum_(i<=j)h_i from n to j,
sends2h_i from j to i for i<j, and sends2h_k directly from n to k for
j<k<n. Its other entries vanish. The balance is(2), and every used arc
is in(14). It has zero cost. This does not assert its matrix is PSD.

There is one actual static smooth convex witness for every legal
amplitude: use(8) with the zero optimum included. For its prefix samples,
beta_i=-sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t; for its tail samples,
beta_k=-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2). These constants are nonpositive,
and equality in either formula implies that the corresponding normal
is zero. The optimum sample also has constant and normal zero. Thus(9)
gives the UNIQUE minimizer0 for this single conjugate interpolant. Its
TRUE squared initial distance is(1+P_j)^2+epsilon, in every case above.

The projection construction used in the earlier drafts is also valid:
let K=conv({0} union {G_i}) and define

    F(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}.                  (16)

The complete normal list proves that the optimizer at X_i is G_i.
Projection onto K is1-Lipschitz: add the two projection normal
inequalities to obtain ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. The same maximizing
inequalities used in Section3 prove differentiability. Since0 is in K,
F>=0=F(0). This is a real minimizer, and(11) are its actual values.

The saturated-value telescoping identity is

    V=||X_0||^2-||X_n+sG_n||^2.

To check it, use2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2
=||X_i||^2-||X_(i+1)||^2, sum, and substitute F_n.
Here X_n=G_n, so the family gives exactly

    V=(1+P_j)^2+epsilon
         -C^2 eta^2*(1+epsilon*p_j^2).                  (17)

Reoptimizing values adds NO inequality to nonnegativity of(17), for
any legal epsilon, word, cut or zero-step pattern. Consequently it adds
none to an existing collection of necessary conditions that already
includes this ENTIRE family. This is a family-level exact redundancy
claim, not global redundancy for arbitrary queried geometries.

### The actual four-step C83 member and true radius

Take h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0, j=2, and

    C=5+a+b+c+d, Q=3+a+b, eta=c/(2+d), p_2=ab.

The only possibly negative coefficient in(13) is i=1, where p_1=-a.
If b eta<1, the full amplitude interval is

    0<=epsilon<=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta)).                (18)

The other two coefficients have positive p_i. The endpoint in(18)
is exactly the repaired-cut value used in d3abee1b17da52da; there is
no new function-value freedom at ANY amplitude in this interval.

For0<ab<1 and epsilon>0, the two prefix gradients
(1,sqrt(epsilon)) and(1,-a sqrt(epsilon)) have G_2 in the relative
interior of their segment. Since G_3=-eta G_2, zero is in the interior
of their nondegenerate triangle with G_3. Thus the minimizer of(16) is
uniquely0: a nonzero normal at0 would have positive pairing with a
point of an open ball in K. The TRUE squared distance is Q^2+epsilon.
At epsilon=0 the instance is scalar and0 is again the closest minimizer
to the displayed initial point. No benchmark ratio is inferred from V.

## 5. The lifted-cut coupling geometry also has zero value price

This section reconstructs the actual geometry used in draft
2948d4cd3660413f, independently of that draft's quantitative matrix gap.
Let a,b,c,d>0, put u=1+a, C=5+a+b+c+d, H=3+c+d, eta=b/H,
and assume a eta<1. Define

    nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a>0,
    U=(1,0), Z=-eta U+sqrt(nu)*(0,1),
    X=(U+uZ, U, -bU, -(2+d)eta U, -eta U),
    G=(Z, U, -eta U, -eta U, -eta U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (19)

The original four updates follow from b=H eta. Include the zero optimum.
The normals at targets1 and4 vanish. At target0 the normal is U+aZ;
the source1 residual is zero, and each source2,3,4 residual is -a nu.
The optimum-source residual there is -(1-a eta)<0. At target2 the
normal is -(b-eta)U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(b-eta), and tail sources have residual zero. At target3 the
normal is -(1+d)eta U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(1+d)eta, and tail sources have residual zero. Optimum-source
residuals at2,3 are respectively -eta(b-eta) and-(1+d)eta^2.
Every residual into the optimum is zero. This checks every pair.

In particular the zero-cost finite paths are

    4->1->0, 4->2, 4->3, and i->4 for every i<4.        (20)

All value differences are therefore forced, and the optimum pairs fix
their common shift. Both finite-flow and fixed-optimum prices vanish.
The function(16) with K=conv{0,Z,U,-eta U} realizes these samples.
Its minimizer set is{(0,t):t<=0}; the initial second coordinate is
u sqrt(nu)>0, so0 is the closest minimizer. Its true squared initial
distance is ||U+uZ||^2, not an independent calibration variable.

The energy is exactly

    Erow=||U+uZ||^2-C^2 eta^2
        =a+3+1/a-[(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a]eta-C^2 eta^2.     (21)

Value-flow pricing returns only Erow>=0. It cannot recover a strictly
positive lower bound on Erow on this SAME geometry: the auxiliary flow
minimum is attained at zero and the values have no remaining freedom.
The further full-matrix restrictions in2948d4cd3660413f use PSD coupling
with a different quadratic direction and the first row. This result
neither assumes that unverified coupling theorem nor refutes it; it
identifies exactly what the present price can and cannot add to it.

## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant

Here keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let

    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,
    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},
    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).

Use parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate
triangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.
These inequalities imply t>=0. Define

    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),
    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),
    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),
    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),
    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)

Every GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,
K=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).
Its comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,

    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,

all nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal
is(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1
gives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1
residual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient
sources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is
(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2
for source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite
and optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.

The finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.
The least cost into0 is

    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)

The other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no
smaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while
each minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore

    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,
    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)

The complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by

    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)

All incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive
by(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are
unchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including
the optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.

For the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.
Its affine constants beta_i are

    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,
    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,
    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.

When t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its
minimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the
closest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.
If t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same
closest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is

    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.

The base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are

    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,
    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)

In particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are
always rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price
whenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed
actual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.

## 7. Full C82 value-and-parameter minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=1

Keep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as above. Put

    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),
    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,
    v_stationary=a/(1-a),
    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},
    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)

Then R>=1/a>b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because
Htri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For
the second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.

The exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is

    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)
                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)

If ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.
If ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate
legal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.

Here is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches
at z^2=(b+1)t. Below this value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2>=0;
above it the coefficient is1-b^2+2(1+a)>0. Therefore z^2=ell is always
a minimizer. For R<=v<=v_cross the second term in(23) is the minimum,
and(26) becomes

    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)

For v>=v_cross it becomes

    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b
                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)

The two expressions agree at v_cross. J_right has strictly positive
derivative on that half-line. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic
with stationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross]
proves(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.

The original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is

    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)

since its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. At v=R,
Htri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.

Consequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully
optimized C82 condition whenever

    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)

In particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.
The positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the
first-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,
with its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there
by Section4, independently of this comparison.

When ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the
optimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is

    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2
                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)

Equivalently this strict-improvement regime is

    a^2+a>1,
    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)

This proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated
domain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current
necessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is
not a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.

## 8. Comparison, resources, and the precise remaining interface

* C83: Section4 completely resolves the proposed value-price mechanism
  for all legal amplitudes of all unit-curvature repaired cuts. The
  optimum/source flow has an explicit zero-cost realization; the full
  value system is rigid. This includes the actual unit-curvature j=2
  separator in d3abee1b17da52da. Curvatures strictly between0 and1 are
  NOT covered by this rigidity theorem.

* C82: Sections6--7 solve the fixed geometry, construct its optimized
  actual function, and eliminate both geometric parameters in the
  explicitly stated domain. A positive price at a nonoptimal triangle
  cannot be called a new word obstruction when(32) holds.

* 554ec17f7f590bf0 and d3abee1b17da52da: their endpoint core is not
  being declared empty. These findings contain further triangle,
  clipping and endpoint conditions; no claim of their complete
  redundancy is made. On the specified projected prism, the tested
  C82/C83 value re-selections add no new condition beyond those
  already present.

* 2948d4cd3660413f: Section5 gives an exact zero-price result for its
  actual lifted-cut geometry, with every pair reconstructed. A
  transport minimum cannot replace that draft's simultaneous full
  PSD/first-row analysis. Its quantitative gap remains a separate
  unverified joint result.

* 04219554e41d9750: its ORIGINAL twenty-sign, exact-flow, full R5 PSD
  interface remains the relevant sufficient seed condition. Every
  implication here uses precisely that identity. No sparse flow
  constructed for pricing is promoted to a feasible certificate.

Two concrete analytical directions remain for the next authorized
round: decide whether the explicit C82 improvement(28) excludes a
region surviving the OTHER full necessary conditions; or apply the
exact price to a genuinely non-unit-curvature geometry, where zero
paths need not pin the values. Either needs an actual static function
and a comparison on the same word. Neither has been decided here.

Retrieval: Matlas returned unrelated results to the complete-statement
value/transport query. The web fallback recovered the already locally
available Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur1502.05666. Its Theorem4 proof,
Corollary1, and cycle Remark3 were read, as were elementary shortest-path
duality notes. No external theorem is a proof input: the accepted
interpolation fact and the direct path argument above suffice.

The complete draft is one joint argument; its sections are not accepted
building blocks. No fact_submit call is authorized or was made. This
round does not use or renew any numerical allocation. It does not close
the original project or select a final answer.

# Incompatible mode orderings: a strict joint price and a complete redundancy theorem

Status: UNVERIFIED complete joint proof draft, max2 round25, under
TASK.md and master_guidance6e0a18d700dcd62f. No numerical/model evaluation,
solver, replay, installation, subordinate agent, or fact_submit was used.
The verifier gate remains closed. This document is not a fact.

Accepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the original primitive
identity on the full formal gradient space), and1252b8823bb5a290,
proof paragraphs1--2 (the interpolation convention and its constructive
sufficiency). All other mathematics used here is proved jointly below.
Previous max2 value-price and monotone drafts are awareness, not premises.

The result addresses an actually incompatible-order family. One matched
quadratic mode reverses at the first step and is annihilated at the second;
any number of slower matched modes remain nonnegative and monotone.
The exact joint price is STRICTLY larger than the sum of the separate
mode prices in the nondegenerate case. Nevertheless the complete
parameter-and-value family supplies NO additional primitive obstruction
when the first step is at most2 and the old prefix-step bounds hold.
In particular there is no need to mistake its positive coupling price
for information beyond the separate scalar tests.

Section8 extends the complete comparison to a reversal at ANY index j,
provided the fast mode is nonnegative before j and is annihilated at j+1.
For arbitrary1<=b=h_(j+1)<=r=h_j, joint pricing is exactly redundant
relative to the separate fast-mode test and the old prefix-step bounds.
If b<=2, that separate fast test is itself already nonnegative under
the old prefix-step bounds. This extension includes middle reversals;
the initial-reversal theorem is not the final scope of the result.

The arbitrary four-step full cone, modes that continue oscillating after
their first sign reversal, delayed annihilation chronologies, planar fractional
repairs, the complementary C82 comparison, and the original all-schedule
exact-profile target remain open. No global no-seed assertion is made.

## 1. Precise family and the claimed all-value minimum

Let n>=2 and let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word with

    h_0=r, h_1=b, 1<=b<=r<=2,
    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_n, C=1+s,
    h_i<=P_i+2 for every i<n.                         (1)

There is no capacity cap and no bound on the number or total mass of tail
steps. The result applies at n=4 with all five original gradient indices.
Set beta=1/b and m=r/b-1>=0. Choose an integer d>=1, amplitudes
u_1,...,u_d,v>=0, and fixed curvatures satisfying

    0<alpha_q<beta, h_i*alpha_q<=1 for every i,q.

Define p_(0,q)=1, p_(i,q)=product_(t<i)(1-alpha_q*h_t), and the fast
position factors z_0=1, z_1=-m, z_i=0 for2<=i<=n. The actual points and
gradients in R^(d+1) are

    x_i=(u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, v*z_i),
    g_i=(alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, beta*v*z_i). (2)

They obey every GD update, including the second update because b*beta=1.
They are initially the samples of the ONE quadratic

    F_base(x)=sum_q alpha_q*x_q^2/2+beta*x_(d+1)^2/2.

No time-dependent Hessian is used. Each slow coordinate is nonnegative
and nonincreasing. If b<r and v>0, the fast sampled gradients really
change sign: positive at0, negative at1, zero from2 onwards.

Put

    U_q=alpha_q*(1-alpha_q)*u_q^2/2,
    B=beta*(1-beta)*v^2/2,
    A=sum_q U_q*(p_(1,q)-p_(2,q))*(1-p_(1,q)),
    X25=4r*A, Y25=4r*B*m*(1+m).                         (3)

For slow gradients a_(i,q)=alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q), write
t_(i,q)=a_(i,q)-a_(i+1,q). Define

    L_slow=sum_(q,i<n) P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)
               *[2(1+P_i)*a_(i,q)-P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)],
    H_fast=beta^2*v^2*[r(2-r)+b(2-b)*m^2].           (4)

Among ALL scalar values on the fixed geometry(2) satisfying ALL directed
finite interpolation inequalities, the exact minimum primitive energy is

    V_min=L_slow+H_fast-min(X25,Y25).                    (5)

The minimum is attained by a single everywhere-defined convex
differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient and actual minimizers.
Requiring the actual optimum jet(0,0,0) does not change this minimum.
The explicit values, static function, and true distance are in Section6.

The decisive full-family comparison is

    V_min >= r*(2-r)*[beta^2*v^2
                            +r*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2] >=0. (6)

In fact it is strictly positive whenever r<2 and the initial vector is nonzero.
The bound is uniform over every curvature and amplitude allowed above,
all tail steps satisfying(1), and all legal values, including values of
nonseparable functions. Thus minimization over the ENTIRE stated family
cannot reject any word passing(1). It is not just a comparison at one
chosen amplitude or at unoptimized values.

For an interior two-mode instance with1<b<r, v,u_1>0, and
0<alpha_1<1/max_i h_i, both X25 and Y25 are positive. The exact joint
price gain min(X25,Y25)>0 is then a concrete strict failure of additivity.
Sections4--5 prove why that failure still gives no new obstruction here.

## 2. Original primitive orientation and all-value optimization

Retain every index I={0,...,n}. For any values f_i define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i*(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n)h_i*(1-h_i)*||g_i||^2-sC*||g_n||^2.

For a nonnegative original finite multiplier array Lambda with zero
diagonal put e=(1,...,1)^T, L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda, and

    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    D=diag(h_i*(2-h_i) for i<n, -s*(s+2)).

The original conditions are

    L^T*e=omega, M=D+(L*T+T^T*L^T)/2 PSD on R^(n+1).

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the identity

    V(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij*Q_ij
        =<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.                      (7)

Here omega is OUTGOING minus incoming flow. The terminal index n is the
source of mass2s; each i<n is a sink of mass2h_i. All ordered pairs and
the full formal gradient space remain in this interface. No equality of
formal coordinates at a zero step is imposed on the certificate.

The quadratic base values f_i^0=F_base(x_i) have symmetric nonnegative
slack costs

    w_ij=-Q_ij^0
         =sum_q U_q*(p_(i,q)-p_(j,q))^2+B*(z_i-z_j)^2. (8)

Let d_i be the shortest cost of a directed path n->i in the complete
finite graph with costs(8). Nonnegative costs imply that a shortest
simple path exists, even with zero costs and repeated points.

Replacement values f_i^0+delta_i are legal exactly when
delta_i-delta_j<=w_ij. Every n->i path implies
delta_i-delta_n>=-d_i. Since h_i>=0,

    min_f V(f)=V(f^0)-2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.              (9)

The choice delta_i=-d_i attains all these bounds at once: shortest
distances obey d_j<=d_i+w_ij, which is precisely its feasibility.
This proves EVERY directed inequality, not only an adjacent subset.

The same improvement is the exact minimum ORIGINAL slack-flow price.
For any original prescribed flow, w_ij>=d_j-d_i gives

    sum Lambda_ij*w_ij >= -sum_i omega_i*d_i
                           =2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.

Sending2h_i along one shortest n->i path for each positive sink h_i
attains equality and satisfies the exact original flow equation.
These auxiliary optimizing flows are not claimed to make M PSD.

## 3. All shortest paths in the incompatible-order geometry

For a scalar ordered list p_0>=...>=p_n and edge costs U*(p_i-p_j)^2,
the distance from n to i is

    U*sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(p_k-p_(k+1))^2.                 (10)

For a proof, an edge crossing several intervening ordered gaps has
cost U*(sum gaps)^2 >= U*sum gaps^2. Every path from p_n to p_i must
cross each gap between them at least once. Summing these lower bounds
over its edges proves(10); the adjacent ordered path attains it. Ties
have zero gap and do not change the argument.

In the isolated fast scalar coordinate, the source position is0, and
the only other positions are1 and-m. The shortest distances are B to0,
B*m^2 to1, and0 to indices>=2: a detour from0 through the opposite-sign
position only adds a nonnegative cost. Define the summed separate-mode
distances

    d_i^sep=sum_q U_q*sum_(k=i)^(n-1)
                          (p_(k,q)-p_(k+1,q))^2
             +B*1_(i=0)+B*m^2*1_(i=1).             (11)

Any joint path has cost at least the sum of these separate minima.
For every i>=1 the chronological path n,n-1,...,i attains(11), including
the fast coordinate. Consequently

    d_i=d_i^sep for1<=i<=n.                          (12)

To reach0, a shortest simple path has a last predecessor j>=1. For j>=2
the best such choice is j=2. Indeed the high coordinate is0 at all these
indices; in each slow coordinate, inserting the intermediate decreasing
points j-1,...,2 into the last edge j->0 weakly decreases squared cost.
For a direct algebraic check, if Delta_k=p_k-p_(k+1)>=0, then

    (1-p_j)^2-(1-p_2)^2
      =2(1-p_2)*sum_(k=2)^(j-1)Delta_k
           +(sum_(k=2)^(j-1)Delta_k)^2
      >=sum_(k=2)^(j-1)Delta_k^2.

Thus it remains to compare exactly two paths: n,...,2,0 and n,...,2,1,0.
Their costs are, respectively,

    d_0^sep+2A,  d_0^sep+2B*m*(1+m).

The first expression uses
(1-p_2)^2-(1-p_1)^2-(p_1-p_2)^2
=2(1-p_1)*(p_1-p_2). The second uses
m^2+(1+m)^2-1=2m(1+m). Therefore

    d_0=d_0^sep+2min(A,B*m*(1+m)).                   (13)

This checks every possible path, without assuming that arbitrary
modewise optimal paths can be combined. Equations(9),(12),(13) show
that the joint price exceeds the sum of the separate prices by EXACTLY

    4r*min(A,B*m*(1+m))=min(X25,Y25).                    (14)

An explicit attaining original flow is also available. If the path
through1 is cheaper, take Lambda_(k+1,k)=2P_(k+1) for0<=k<n and all
other entries zero. If the bypass is cheaper, take

    Lambda_20=2r, Lambda_21=2b, Lambda_10=0,
    Lambda_(k+1,k)=2P_(k+1) for2<=k<n,

with every other entry zero. At equality either flow, or any convex
combination, attains the minimum. Both are nonnegative and have exact
terminal-source flow. They are auxiliary flows, not full PSD certificates.

## 4. The exact separate energies and their sum

Consider one slow coordinate and abbreviate its gradients by a_i and
decrements by t_i=a_i-a_(i+1). Its ordered shortest paths make every
reverse adjacent inequality tight at the optimized values:

    f_i-f_(i+1)=h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)+t_i^2/2.

Substitution into V gives the corresponding summand L_q in(4).
Here is a telescoping audit independent of any previous monotone draft.
The value term becomes sum_i[2P_(i+1)*h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)
+P_(i+1)*t_i^2]. After writing a_(i+1)=a_i-t_i, the coefficient of a_i^2
is h_i*(1+2P_i+h_i), which equals
P_(i+1)*(P_(i+1)+1)-P_i*(P_i+1). Subtracting sC*a_n^2 telescopes it
to sum_i P_(i+1)*(P_(i+1)+1)*(2a_i*t_i-t_i^2).
The remaining terms reduce exactly to

    L_q=sum_i P_(i+1)*t_i*
                 [2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i].        (15)

This derivation proves the exact minimum for a scalar slow mode, because
the distance potentials already satisfy all its nonadjacent inequalities.

For the fast coordinate, the optimized values, with its zero terminal
value, are beta^2*v^2/2 at0 and beta^2*v^2*m^2/2 at1, zero thereafter.
Indeed they are the quadratic base values minus B and B*m^2. Their
exact primitive energy is H_fast in(4).

They are also attained by the explicit one-dimensional function

    F_fast(t)=dist(t,J)^2/2,
    J=[-(1-beta)*v*m, (1-beta)*v].                   (16)

The derivative is t minus its projection onto the interval J; it is
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. At v,-v*m,0 it has exactly the stated
gradients and values, and J is its actual full minimizer set.

The base primitive energy is a sum of coordinate energies. Applying
the exact improvement(14) to their separate all-value minima proves(5).
The minimizing JOINT values need not be values of the separable sum of
the coordinate interpolants. Section6 constructs their actual joint
function; no incompatible scalar value assignments are silently glued.

## 5. Full parameter comparison: the coupling gain is absorbed

The prefix bounds in(1) imply nonnegativity of EVERY slow summand in(15).
Indeed 0<=t_i<=a_i and

    2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i
        >=(P_i+2-h_i)*a_i>=0.                       (17)

The FIRST TWO summands cover the entire possible coupling charge. Since
p_1=1-r*alpha and p_2=(1-b*alpha)*p_1, the coordinate contribution X25_q to X25 is

    X25_q=2r^2*b*alpha^3*(1-alpha)*(1-r*alpha)*u^2.

Let l=1-r*alpha in[0,1] and set

    D_b=b*(r+b)/r, A_b=2+2r-D_b.

Since b<=r, D_b<=2r and A_b>=2. The first two summands in L_q,
after dividing by alpha^3*u^2 when u>0, are

    r^2*(2-r^2*alpha)
      +b*(r+b)*l^2*[2(1+r)-b*(r+b)*alpha].

Subtract X25_q/(alpha^3*u^2)=2r^2*b*(1-alpha)*l and use
alpha=(1-l)/r. The resulting EXACT polynomial is

    Delt_q(l)=r^2*(2-r)
       +r*[r^2-2b*(r-1)]*l
       +[b*(r+b)*A_b-2r*b]*l^2
       +[b^2*(r+b)^2/r]*l^3.                       (18)

Every coefficient is nonnegative. The linear coefficient is at least
r^2*(2-r), since b<=r and r>=1. The quadratic coefficient is at least
2b^2, since A_b>=2. The cubic coefficient is positive. In particular

    Delt_q(l)>=r^2*(2-r)*(1+l)+2b^2*l^2>=r^2*(2-r).

If u=0, the same comparison holds after multiplying through by
alpha^3*u^2, without division. All later summands of L_q are
nonnegative by(17). Summing over the slow modes gives

    L_slow>=X25+r^2*(2-r)*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2.      (19)

Meanwhile1<=b<=r<=2 gives

    H_fast>=beta^2*v^2*r*(2-r)>=0.                  (20)

Finally min(X25,Y25)<=X25. Inserting(19)--(20) into the EXACT formula(5)
proves(6). The slow modes alone cover the entire potential coupling
gain; the fast mode contributes a nonnegative remaining energy.
Neither the amplitudes nor the curvatures nor the values remain to be
optimized to decide the sign. The comparison works on the WHOLE range
r<=2 supplied by the first prefix-step bound, not only near one word.

## 6. One static attaining function, actual optimum and true radius

Append the optimum index star with x_star=g_star=f_star=0 to the graph.
Its direct distance from n is

    d_star=D_star=sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)^2.               (22)

This is a shortest path: in every slow coordinate, all finite positions
are at least the terminal position, and there is no finite position
strictly between zero and that terminal position. Formula(10), with
zero inserted at the end, gives a lower bound U_q*p_(n,q)^2 in that
coordinate. The fast coordinate is zero at both endpoints. The direct
arc attains the sum of these lower bounds.

Adding star cannot shorten a finite distance. For every finite j,

    w_(n,j)=D_star+w_(star,j)
                    -2sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)*p_(j,q)
             <=D_star+w_(star,j).

In a path passing through star, its prefix to star has cost at least
D_star. Replace that prefix and its following arc star->j by n->j.
The displayed inequality never increases the cost. Thus the finite
distances remain exactly(12)--(13), and the fixed-optimum price equals
the original finite-flow price, without adding optimum multipliers to(7).

The actual attaining values are now explicit:

    f_i=f_i^0+D_star-d_i, f_star=0.                  (23)

For every ordered pair among the finite and optimum indices, the change
of values is a distance potential. Hence all Q_ij<=0 follow from the
distance inequalities as in Section2, including both directions and
every coincident-point pair.

Let y_i=x_i-g_i, eta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f_i, including y_star=eta_star=0.
Define on R^(d+1)

    H(w)=||w||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,w>+eta_i),
    F(x)=sup_w(<x,w>-H(w)).                         (24)

This is the constructive interpolation form of accepted1252b8823bb5a290.
For completeness, H is finite, continuous,1-strongly convex and has
quadratic growth. There is a unique maximizing w(x) for every x.
The two strong subgradient inequalities imply
<x-x',w(x)-w(x')>>=||w(x)-w(x')||^2. The two maximizing inequalities
then give a differentiability remainder bounded by||x-x'||^2, proving
that F is convex differentiable with gradient w(x) and1-Lipschitz
gradient. The Q inequalities make the i-th branch of H active at g_i,
with subgradient x_i. Thus grad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. This is ONE
fixed global F realizing every step of(2), not a sequence of local jets.

The exact minimizer set is

    K_min=conv{y_i:eta_i=0}, min F=0.                (25)

Indeed eta_i<=0 by the optimum inequalities, and eta_star=0. A point x
minimizes F exactly when grad F(x)=0, equivalently x belongs to the
subgradient set of H at0. This set is exactly the convex hull of the
active slopes y_i. To verify the latter equality, a convex combination
of active slopes gives the subgradient inequality directly. For a point
outside that hull, a separating direction and a sufficiently small
positive displacement keep inactive affine branches inactive and
contradict the subgradient inequality. This proves both inclusions.

In particular a real optimum always exists and the TRUE radius is

    R_true=dist(x_0,K_min),                          (26)

with the displayed finite convex hull, not an assumed normalization.
The point x_0 has nonzero gradient whenever some amplitude is positive,
so it is outside K_min and this radius is positive.

There is a simpler exact radius in the generic sign-changing family.
The direct arc bound d_i<=w_(n,i) gives

    eta_i<=-2sum_q U_q*p_(i,q)*p_(n,q).

If any active slow mode has p_(n,q)>0, this is STRICTLY negative at
every finite sample. Therefore K_min={0} and

    R_true^2=sum_q u_q^2+v^2.                       (27)

The entire open family with strict h_i*alpha_q<1 has this property.

For an explicit terminal-zero boundary, take one active slow mode,
u>0, alpha*r<1, and let k>=3 be its first zero index. Put j=k-1.
Then the slow positions are positive through j and zero afterwards;
the fast coordinate has already vanished. The active slopes in(25)
are0 and ((1-alpha)*u*p_j,0), including any repeated copies caused by
zero steps before the killing step. Earlier distinct positions have
strictly smaller eta because their monotone distance path has at least
one intermediate positive position; in particular the path via j
strictly improves their direct squared cost. Hence

    K_min=[0,(1-alpha)*u*p_j] x {0},
    R_true^2=(u-(1-alpha)*u*p_j)^2+v^2.              (28)

For other degenerate amplitude patterns, (25)--(26) already specify
the complete minimizer set and exact true distance without a false
uniqueness assertion. In particular, if every slow amplitude vanishes,
(16) supplies the full fast minimizer interval directly, with closest
distance beta*v from its initial point v.

## 7. Boundaries, scalar-cut comparison, and initial-reversal scope

All formulas allow b=1 (unit fast curvature, B=0), r=b (no fast
reversal, m=0), vanishing amplitudes, zero tail steps, and slow products
that first hit zero. Then the corresponding price increment is zero
when dictated by(3); no positive-price claim is retained at a collapsed
boundary. Repeated positions have identical gradients and values by
(2),(8),(23). The optimum graph retains them as separate indices.

Arbitrary additional zero steps can also be inserted anywhere into the
physical word, with all duplicate samples retained. Compression here
is only a description of this actual witness: its positions, gradients,
values, sums, energy, and price do not change. Lift each displayed path
through the zero-cost duplicates, keeping sinks of weight zero if
needed. No compression, quotient, or equality is imposed on the formal
M in(7). At n=4 its ambient space is still all of R^5.

The prefix-step hypothesis in(1) is already weaker than the old actual
single-cut necessary conditions. Here is the complete relevant
reconstruction. For any cut j with h_j>1, put
P=P_j, T=sum_(i>j)h_i, eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T). Use the scalar function

    F_cut(x)=max_(g in[-eta,1]) (x*g-g^2/2)

and x_0=1+P. Before the cut, the gradient is1 and x_j=1. Immediately
afterwards x_(j+1)=-(1+T)*eta; throughout the tail the gradient is-eta,
with x_n=-eta. The derivative is projection onto[-eta,1], so the
function is convex differentiable and1-smooth. Its unique minimizer
is0: the interval contains both a positive and a negative number,
and testing a sufficiently small number of the sign of x makes F_cut(x)>0
for x!=0. Direct insertion of F_cut(x)=x*g-g^2/2 and these two constant
gradient blocks into V yields

    V_cut=(1+P)^2-C^2*((h_j-1)/(1+T))^2.

Thus a full certificate in(7) requires

    C*(h_j-1)<=(1+P)*(1+T).                         (29)

Writing U=1+P and R=1+T gives C=U+R+(h_j-1). If h_j>=P+2, then
h_j-1>=U and C*(h_j-1)>=C*U>R*U, contrary to(29).
Hence every positive step passing(29) satisfies h_j<P_j+2; a step at
most1 satisfies that inequality directly. This proves the comparison
to previously available conditions, without importing an unverified
monotone or cut draft.

For four-step words with 1<=h_1<=h_0<=2, the theorem covers every
fast mode beta=1/h_1 and every collection of slow eigenmodes satisfying
alpha_q*h_i<=1. It is independent of C and so remains valid throughout
the threshold C>=193/25. The old first-cut condition itself forces
h_0<2, so no stronger compact-domain bound or auxiliary draft is needed.
The family still imposes h_1<=h_0 and annihilation at the second step. No claim is
made for the other initial ordering or a fast mode that survives.

The exact information gain is therefore a SCOPED CLOSURE: even genuinely
incompatible spatial orderings and a strictly positive all-value joint
price do not strengthen the old conditions in this entire stated family.
It does not exclude a new remaining h-region, construct any full Lambda,
or establish feasibility of any full PSD certificate. Excluding a witness
family is not a seed, and even excluding every four-step seed would only
close the sufficient negative route of04219554e41d9750, not settle the
original affirmative minimax target.

Literature grounding: Matlas returned1910.01845 Theorem5, the standard
smooth-convex interpolation equivalence, rather than a result about
competing mode orders. Web search recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
arXiv1502.05666. The exact primary PDF was downloaded locally and its
Theorem4 proof, constructive Remark2, Corollary1 and cycle Remark3 were
read. These concern all directed interpolation inequalities; they do not
assert separability or common modewise shortest paths. No new external
result is imported as a premise beyond accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

## 8. General single reversal followed immediately by annihilation

This section extends the complete theorem rather than importing another
draft. Keep an arbitrary nonnegative word and all old prefix bounds
h_i<=P_i+2. Choose0<=j<=n-2 and write

    P=P_j, r=h_j, b=h_(j+1),
    1<=b<=r, H_j=P+r, beta=1/b,
    h_i<=b for i<j.                                (30)

The last hypothesis makes the fast quadratic trajectory nonnegative up
to j. Its position factors are

    t_i=product_(k<i)(1-beta*h_k) for i<=j,
    t_(j+1)=-m*t_j, m=r/b-1,
    t_i=0 for i>=j+2.

Use any number of slower modes with0<alpha_q<beta and
alpha_q*h_i<=1 for every i. Their positions and gradients are still
u_q*p_(i,q) and alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q). The fast position and gradient
are v*t_i and beta*v*t_i. These are samples of ONE fixed diagonal
quadratic before values are re-selected. A genuine sign reversal
requires r>b, v>0, t_j>0; every degenerate boundary remains allowed.

Set U_q and B as in(3), and now define

    A_j=sum_q U_q*(p_(j,q)-p_(j+1,q))
                         *(p_(j+1,q)-p_(j+2,q)),
    X25_j=4H_j*A_j,
    Y25_j=4H_j*B*t_j^2*m*(1+m).                     (31)

Let a_i^f=beta*v*t_i for i<=j and let

    E_pref=sum_(i<j) P_(i+1)*(a_i^f-a_(i+1)^f)
              *[2(1+P_i)*a_i^f
                          -P_(i+1)*(a_i^f-a_(i+1)^f)],
    K_j=(1+P)^2-(r-1)^2+b*(2-b)*m^2,
    H_fast,j=E_pref+K_j*(a_j^f)^2.                  (32)

Then the exact all-value minimum is

    V_min=L_slow+H_fast,j-min(X25_j,Y25_j),          (33)

and the COMPLETE coupling comparison is

    L_slow>=X25_j,
    V_min>=H_fast,j.                               (34)

Therefore, for this fixed word and fast curvature, nonnegativity over
ALL the displayed slow curvatures, amplitudes and legal joint values
is EQUIVALENT to nonnegativity of the separate fast scalar minimum
H_fast,j. One implication is(34); for the other set every slow amplitude
to zero. This is an exact redundancy theorem, not a bare new condition
with an unresolved joint sign. The scalar fast test can still be useful;
adding monotone slow modes and all their joint value freedom cannot
strengthen it in this entire family.

If additionally b<=2, then

    H_fast,j>=0                                    (35)

already follows from the old prefix bounds. Thus the WHOLE family,
including its fast scalar part, is redundant under the old cuts in
this subcase. No condition r<=2 is needed at a later reversal: the old
bound here is r<=P+2.

### 8.1 Exact shortest distances and the attainable price

The isolated slow distances are still(10). The isolated fast distances
from the zero terminal position are

    d_i^f=B*[t_j^2+sum_(k=i)^(j-1)(t_k-t_(k+1))^2] for i<=j,
    d_(j+1)^f=B*m^2*t_j^2,
    d_i^f=0 for i>=j+2.                             (36)

To prove these expressions, insert the source0 into the sorted scalar
list. The positive prefix t_0>=...>=t_j>=0 is on one side of0; the
single negative value-m*t_j is on the other side. The ordered-gap proof
of(10) applies on each side, and detouring through the opposite side
adds a nonnegative cost. This includes zero products and ties.

Write d_i^sep for the sum of(36) and all slow scalar distances. For
i>=j+1 the chronological path n,...,i attains this sum, so the joint
distance equals d_i^sep. For each i<=j, there are exactly two relevant
paths: the chronological path, and the chronological path that bypasses
the single vertex j+1. Their costs are

    d_i^sep+2B*t_j^2*m*(1+m),
    d_i^sep+2A_j.                                  (37)

Here is an all-directed-pair proof that no omitted path can be shorter.
Set delta=2min(A_j,B*t_j^2*m*(1+m)). Propose distances d_i^sep+delta
on i<=j, and d_i^sep on i>=j+1. Sums of the separate scalar distance
potentials always obey d_i^sep-d_k^sep<=w_(k,i), since each scalar
potential obeys its own distance inequality and the edge costs add.

Within either index group, the proposed constant shift cancels. From
a prefix index to a later index, it makes the distance inequality
weaker. Only inequalities bounding a prefix distance by a later
distance plus an edge need checking. For i<=j and k>=j+1, the scaled
position of k is coordinatewise at most that of j, and the positions
of j,j-1,...,i are coordinatewise nondecreasing. Expanding the sums of
nonnegative coordinate gaps gives

    w_(k,i)>=w_(k,j)+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l),
    d_i^sep=d_j^sep+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l).

Thus it suffices to check the target i=j. For k=j+1 the exact slack
is d_(j+1)^sep+w_(j+1,j)-d_j^sep=2B*t_j^2*m*(1+m).
For k>=j+2, insert the coordinatewise ordered tail indices k-1,...,j+2
into the edge k->j. The gap-square inequality in Section3 gives

    d_k^sep+w_(k,j)>=d_(j+2)^sep+w_(j+2,j)
                         =d_j^sep+2A_j.

Both lower bounds are at least delta, by its definition. Every directed
distance inequality is now checked, including those that could have
used an extra tail vertex after the negative sample. Summing these
inequalities along any path from n proves that its cost is at least
the proposed terminal-to-target distance. The paths(37) attain that
distance, proving exactly

    d_i=d_i^sep+2min(A_j,B*t_j^2*m*(1+m)) for i<=j. (38)

Multiplying this common increment by2sum_(i<=j)h_i=2H_j yields the
exact original-flow price gain min(X25_j,Y25_j). An attaining flow
sends each prefix sink along its cheaper path in(37), and each later
sink along its chronological path. Both complete choices have the
original terminal-source flow, all nonnegative entries, and all
zero-weight vertices retained. Again no PSD property is asserted for
these auxiliary flows.

### 8.2 Exact separate fast energy

The all-value fast minimum has terminal value0, value(a_j^f)^2/2 at j,
value(m*a_j^f)^2/2 at j+1, and zero values afterwards. For i<j its
successive value differences satisfy the tight ordered relation

    f_i^f-f_(i+1)^f
      =h_i*a_i^f*a_(i+1)^f+(a_i^f-a_(i+1)^f)^2/2.

These are precisely the quadratic base values minus the distances(36),
so they satisfy EVERY scalar pair. The first j steps have primitive
energy E_pref by the complete derivation(15). Expanding the remaining
two steps, while retaining the weights on f_j from all earlier steps,
adds

    [P(P+1)+(P+r)+r(1-r)]*(a_j^f)^2
                       +b(2-b)*m^2*(a_j^f)^2
      =[(1+P)^2-(r-1)^2+b(2-b)*m^2]*(a_j^f)^2.

This proves(32) as an exact all-value scalar minimum. Subtracting the
joint price gain just computed proves(33).

### 8.3 The complete slow-mode budget at an arbitrary reversal

Fix one slow mode alpha,u and set l=1-r*alpha in[0,1]. All its energy
terms outside j,j+1 are nonnegative by(17). With H=H_j=P+r and

    A_jb=2(1+P+r)-b*(H+b)/r,

the two terms at j,j+1 minus their contribution to X25_j, after
removing the nonnegative factor alpha^3*u^2*p_j^2, equal

    H*r*(P+2-r)
      +H*[r*H-2b*(r-1)]*l
      +[b*(H+b)*A_jb-2H*b]*l^2
      +[b^2*(H+b)^2/r]*l^3.                       (39)

This identity follows by substituting alpha=(1-l)/r into

    H*r*[2(1+P)-H*r*alpha]
      +(H+b)*b*l^2*[2(1+P+r)-(H+b)*b*alpha]
      -2H*r*b*(1-alpha)*l.

It remains valid when the removed factor is zero by multiplying back;
no division by a vanished trajectory value is used in the conclusion.
All coefficients in(39) are nonnegative:

* The constant is nonnegative because r<=P+2.
* The linear coefficient is at least H*r*(P+2-r), because1<=b<=r.
* Since b<=r, A_jb>=2+P+r-b>=P+2. The quadratic coefficient is at
  least b*[P*H+b*(P+2)]>=0.
* The cubic coefficient is positive.

Thus these two slow terms alone cover the whole possible bypass price
X25_j. Summation proves the first assertion in(34), and (33) gives the
second. This is the full comparison, including arbitrary slow amplitudes
and any number of slow modes. It does not rely on a shared scalar
ordering at the exceptional negative vertex.

Finally E_pref>=0 by(17), applied to the nonnegative fast prefix, using
beta*h_i<=1 for i<j. If b<=2, b(2-b)>=0; and r<=P+2 with r>=1 implies
(r-1)^2<=(P+1)^2. Every term in(32) is then nonnegative, proving(35).

### 8.4 Attainment, boundaries and the four-step consequence

Use the distances(36)--(38) and the quadratic base on the generalized
geometry. The fixed optimum0 still cannot shorten a finite distance:
all slow positions are at least their terminal positions, the fast
terminal coordinate is0, and the exact argument(22)--(23) is unchanged.
Consequently the values(23), with these new distances, satisfy all
finite and optimum directed inequalities and attain(33).

The single static conjugate function(24), its COMPLETE actual minimizer
set(25), and its TRUE radius(26) apply verbatim with the new jets. If
at least one active slow mode survives to the terminal, (27) still
gives the unique optimum0 and true squared distance sum u_q^2+v^2.
Otherwise (25)--(26) retain every active slope and do not assert a
false unique minimizer or transfer the special segment formula(28)
outside its stated initial-reversal setting.

All coincident points, zero step indices, zero products, b=1, r=b,
and vanishing amplitudes are retained as in Section7. Strict price
gain occurs whenever the two quantities in(31) are both positive;
that positivity does not imply a new primitive obstruction, by(34).

For a four-step word this covers j=0,1,2 whenever the explicit prefix
order h_i<=h_(j+1) for i<j holds and h_j>=h_(j+1)>=1. The fast
curvature is the actual reciprocal1/h_(j+1), not a fitted sequence of
Hessians. Under the old cuts, every killing step at most2 gives the
complete redundancy(35). If the killing step exceeds2, the possible
new information is exactly the ONE separate fast scalar test(32);
coupling it with all allowed monotone modes and optimizing ALL values
does not strengthen it. This conclusion is uniform in C, including
the remaining threshold C>=193/25, with all original h,Lambda,flow and
ambient R^5 PSD coordinates preserved.

The general case of a fast mode which remains nonzero after its first
sign reversal, or has multiple sign reversals, is not covered. The
original target remains open. All new conclusions in this whole
document remain UNVERIFIED joint drafts.

# Terminal sum-guard equality is equivalent to intrinsic primitivity

Status: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No additional fact_submit is allocated.
The accepted predecessors are1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d. The
known primitive-energy functional is already used in max4's shared research;
the new point here is NECESSITY from sharpness of the terminal sum guard.
No unverified terminal minimax or guard candidate is a proof premise.

## 1. Definitions and the equivalence

Fix k>=0 and a finite nonnegative prefix h_pre=(h_0,...,h_(k-1)). Put
S=sum h_pre and define

    t=(-1+sqrt(8S+9))/4,
    b_S=1+t,
    r=1/(2t+1),
    q_S=r^2=1/[1+2(S+b_S)].                            (1)

Thus t(2t+1)=S+1, t=r(S+1), and 1-r=2r^2(S+1).
Let rho_last=inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b), in the original project
normalization. It is always at least q_S.

For a smoothness-one trajectory of the prefix, with arbitrary initial
point and any minimizer, define the translation-invariant intrinsic energy

    V_prim(h_pre)=2sum_(i<k) h_i(f_i-f_k)
       +sum_(i<k)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
       -S(S+1)||g_k||^2.                              (2)

Here f_i are function values, g_i are gradients, and the GD recursion is
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. In arbitrary smoothness L, use f_i/L and g_i=grad f(x_i)/L.
The value in(2) is unchanged by a common additive shift of all function values.

The following four statements are equivalent:

(A) rho_last(h_pre)=q_S.

(B) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b_S)=q_S.

(C) V_prim(h_pre)>=0 on EVERY finite-dimensional legal convex 1-smooth
    trajectory with a minimizer.

(D) There are nonnegative coefficients eta_ij on distinct finite-prefix
    pairs i,j in {0,...,k} such that

      V_prim(h_pre)+sum_(i!=j)eta_ij Q_ij                (3)

    is PSD on the FULL formal space of (g_0,...,g_k), after substituting
    the prefix GD positions. All value coefficients cancel. Equivalently,
    the finite-node flow of eta is

      out_i-in_i=-2h_i,       i<k,
      out_k-in_k=2S.

When (A) holds, b_S is the unique optimal final step. Leading, interior,
trailing and all-zero prefixes are included in the equivalence.

## 2. The universal lower guard and its equality location

Accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d gives, for every b>=0,

    W_(k+1)(h_pre,b)
       >=max{1/[1+2(S+b)], (b-1)_+^2/(S+1)^2}.         (4)

The first term decreases strictly; the second increases strictly for b>1.
They meet at b=b_S and value q_S, by(1). Thus rho_last>=q_S, and (B)
implies (A) with the asserted unique step.

To justify (A) implies (B) without using the unverified minimax result,
take a sequence b_j with W(h_pre,b_j)->q_S. The continuous lower envelope
in(4) has unique minimizer b_S and tends to infinity as b tends to infinity,
so b_j->b_S. The function W(h_pre,b) is lower semicontinuous: for every
fixed actual prefix instance its last objective is a continuous function
of b, and W is the supremum of these functions in the same normalization.
Therefore W(h_pre,b_S)<=liminf_j W(h_pre,b_j)=q_S. The reverse follows
from(4), proving (B). This argument needs no prior existence theorem for
an optimal final step.

## 3. Sufficiency of the intrinsic energy: an explicit identity

Normalize the minimizer to zero. Append b_S, write n=k+1, and put

    V=r[x_k-(S+2)g_k].

The following algebraic identity holds on every formal trajectory:

    q_S||x_0||^2-2f_n
      +2q_S sum_(i<k)h_i Q_(i,star)
      +2q_S(S+2) Q_(k,star)
      +2r Q_(n,star)+2(1-r)Q_(n,k)
          =||g_n-V||^2+q_S V_prim(h_pre).             (5)

One derivation is to apply the terminal-square expansion in accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290 with alpha_star=r, alpha_k=1-r and every other
terminal weight zero. It gives V=x_n-(1-r)(x_k-g_k), which equals the
displayed V because b_S-1+r=t+r=r(S+2). After expanding its remaining
prefix terms and using

    ||x_0||^2-||x_k||^2
       =sum_(i<k)[2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2],

the star terms cancel every <x_i,g_i>, and the remaining expression is
exactly q_S times(2). This checks (5) without any assumption that its
prefix remainder is PSD on unrestricted data.

If (C) holds, the RHS of(5) is nonnegative on every actual trajectory.
All Q terms on the LHS are nonpositive with nonnegative coefficients.
Thus 2f_n<=q_S||x_0||^2 and W(h_pre,b_S)<=q_S. Equation(4) gives equality.
This proves (C)=>(B) directly, without needing a finite multiplier
representation in advance. The implication (D)=>(C) follows from(3)
because all finite Q are nonpositive on actual trajectories.

## 4. Necessity: two explicit equality instances force the terminal support

First assume all k prefix steps are positive, allowing k=0, and assume (B).
By accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, select a tight full nonnegative-flow PSD
certificate at level q_S with no incoming terminal multipliers. Write its
outgoing probabilities as alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 for j in {star,0,...,k}.

Consider the scalar Huber function with derivative clamp(x,[-q_S,q_S]),
started at x_0=1. If P_i=sum_(u<i)h_u, its prefix points and gradients are

    x_i=1-q_S P_i,    g_i=q_S.

The final point is 1-q_S(S+b_S), still strictly in its positive linear
region, and its normalized error is q_S. Moreover, at EVERY prefix sample,

    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2
            =q_S[1-q_S(P_i+1)]>0.                    (6)

The inequality uses P_i<=S and q_S(S+1)=r t=(1-r)/2<1.
Every star-outgoing prefix residual Q_(star,i)=-delta_i is strictly negative.
Complementarity in the accepted full certificate therefore forces

    lambda_(star,i)=0,       0<=i<=k.                  (7)

Next use the scalar capped quadratic with derivative min(c,x), c=1/(S+1),
also started at1. Its whole prefix remains on the right affine branch:

    x_i=1-cP_i,   g_i=c,   x_k=c.

The appended step sends it to x_n=-ct and gradient g_n=-ct. Its normalized
error is c^2t^2=q_S. For i<k the outgoing terminal residual is exactly

    Q_(n,i)=-b_S c^2(S-P_i)<0,                        (8)

while Q_(n,k)=Q_(n,star)=0. To check(8), start from Q_(n,k)=0, then increase
the old point from c to x_i=c+c(S-P_i). Its old value increases by c times
that displacement while its pairing with g_n changes by -ct times that
displacement. The resulting difference is -(1+t)c^2(S-P_i).
Strict positivity of the prefix steps makes S-P_i>0 for every i<k.
Complementarity now forces alpha_i=0 for every i<k.

Thus only alpha_star and alpha_k=1-alpha_star remain. Evaluate the terminal
square on the Huber equality trajectory. The accepted fact gives g_n=V_alpha,
so

    q_S=alpha_star(1-Sq_S)-(t+alpha_star)q_S.

Equivalently,

    alpha_star[1-(S+1)q_S]=(t+1)q_S.

The bracket is positive, and(1) gives its unique solution

    alpha_star=r,   alpha_k=1-r.                      (9)

All these restrictions hold for every selected optimal certificate of
the stated no-incoming-terminal type. They were deduced from actual scalar
equality functions, not from a rank guess or an assumed sparse prefix.

## 5. Full PSD forces the remaining optimum multipliers

The accepted terminal remainder is

    R_alpha=q_S||x_0||^2-2(1-r)delta_k
             -r^2||x_k-(S+2)g_k||^2
             +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,k})lambda_ij Q_ij.

It is PSD on the FULL original prefix formal space. By(7), no multiplier
points out of star. Let mu_i=lambda_(i,star). Its x_0-square coefficient
is q_S-r^2=0. A PSD matrix with a zero diagonal entry has a zero row:
on any two-coordinate restriction, a nonzero off-diagonal entry would make
the quadratic negative for one sufficiently small signed second coordinate.
Therefore every x_0--gradient cross coefficient in R_alpha vanishes.

Finite-pair Q_ij have no x_0 cross term after the GD substitution, because
x_j-x_i does not contain x_0. The star terms contribute -mu_i<x_0,g_i>.
Expansion of the negative square then forces

    mu_i=2q_S h_i,       i<k,
    mu_k=2q_S(S+2).                                  (10)

No interpolation multiplier on a finite queried-prefix pair is discarded.
Substitute(10), divide by q_S, and use the telescoping calculation in(5).
The result is exactly

    R_alpha/q_S=V_prim(h_pre)
           +sum_(i!=j in {0,...,k})(lambda_ij/q_S)Q_ij.

This is PSD on the full gradient formal space, and each eta_ij=lambda_ij/q_S
is nonnegative. All value coefficients cancel because they already cancel
in R_alpha. Their coefficients give precisely the finite flows in(D).
Thus (B)=>(D), completing the equivalence for positive prefixes.

## 6. Zero-step lifting and the empty prefix

Delete zero prefix steps and select as representatives each original
positive-step query index, with original index k representing the last
prefix query. Actual coincident queries have identical gradients and values:
adding their two Q inequalities gives ||g_i-g_j||^2<=0, and their values
then agree. The original and compressed schedules therefore have identical
S, b_S, q_S, W and rho_last.

The energy(2) is also unchanged by this compression. Terms at zero steps
have coefficient zero, while the final gradient is the selected original
g_k. Hence condition(C) is equivalent on the two schedules.

If (B) holds, apply the positive-prefix proof to the compressed schedule
and lift its finite eta multipliers by the representative-index map.
All other eta vanish. Every mapped finite Q is the corresponding compressed
Q as an identity in independent original formal vectors, because the
omitted h coefficients are zero. The lifted quadratic(3) is the congruence
of the compressed PSD matrix with the coordinate-selection map. Unused
gradient columns are zero; no equality between duplicated formal gradients
is needed for PSD. This proves(D) in the full ORIGINAL formal space.

For an empty or all-zero prefix, S=0, V_prim=0, b_S=3/2, q_S=1/4, and
eta=0 is a certificate in(D). The sufficiency identity(5) proves the upper
value, and(4) proves the lower value and uniqueness. These cases therefore
require no strictness or Slater assumption.

## 7. What this does and does not close

The result characterizes exactly WHEN the sum-based terminal lower guard
is sharp: the complete prefix must satisfy the full intrinsic primitive
energy inequality, equivalently its full finite-pair PSD certificate.
It supplies a necessity statement for the single terminal ConPD bridge
psi(S,0), not merely the familiar sufficiency of composing a primitive
prefix with an empty terminal block.

The primitive energy itself is not new; it is already present in shared
findings504a69a1e000d6aa and2973c2322cdee545. Their numerical probes and
unverified composition statements are not premises here. The accepted
terminal reduction is what forces(7)-(10) and the complete finite certificate.

An arbitrary globally optimal longer schedule need not attain this particular
sum-only guard. A dominant terminal block can have several steps, and its
earlier prefix is not thereby primitive. Nor does this equivalence bound
the largest sum of a primitive prefix of a fixed length. Those are distinct
unresolved bridges to the original asymptotic target.
