# Geometry-adaptive single-anchor terminal mixtures

JOINT UNVERIFIED — max4 invocation83, project gd-exact-profile-20260926.
Current steering ab8fdd0dc7b752a2; plan14ef9bbc976ba162.
This is one joint mathematical draft. No formal submission was authorized
or made, no new fact_id exists, and its new assertions are not truth-store
building blocks. The original target and the full W threshold remain OPEN.

## 1. Verdict and exact scope

The assigned selector has NOT been refuted, and its uniform all-function
inequality has NOT been proved. Only Mechanism1 was opened. Its required
legal all-depth first-law failure was not obtained, so Mechanism2
(optimum plus two selected anchors) remains unopened. There is no third law.

The following analytical screens are completed jointly in this draft:

* The known root-sign-reversing quadratic passes. On that precise prefix,
  the last anchor is proved to be the unique optimal single anchor AND
  to attain the TRUE full-prefix functional. The functional is below1/(4D).
* The canonical Huber contact passes with equality1/D. EVERY original
  anchor attains this value, so the deterministic index tie rule chooses0,
  not the last anchor.
* A genuinely multidirectional, separable Huber/quadratic family passes
  uniformly for all initial coordinate amplitudes and every allowed m.
  Its actual gradient vectors span two dimensions when both amplitudes
  are nonzero; no equality of formal gradient variables is imposed.
* Every legal prefix with ALL heights delta_i=0 passes with the stronger
  bound below1/D, in arbitrary dimension. The original rows force these
  anchors to be projections onto ONE convex set. This closes the whole
  zero-height case, not just a preselected cloud of points or a scalar
  projection example.
* The same selector passes for EVERY legal prefix with
  max_i delta_i<=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)), by an explicit bound on
  the sum of all physical suffix products. This is a quantitative
  positive-height extension, not a full all-prefix theorem.

An exact finite correlation-cover inequality states the remaining
interface. It has not been proved for arbitrary positive-height prefixes.
The zero-height argument does not transfer merely by dropping height
terms; those terms change the normal-cone inequalities themselves.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the full original interpolation model
and maximal prefix extension. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the
same-enclosing-m scalar benchmark comparison. No unverified prior
finding or external theorem is used as a mathematical premise. In
particular the known quadratic is rederived here as part of this joint
argument, rather than importing its previous unverified proof.

## 2. Original model, hierarchy, and normalization

Normalize L=1, an actual selected minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0.
All norms and inner products are Euclidean. The original updates are

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i,     g_i=grad f(x_i),     f_i=f(x_i).

For all original ordered pairs, including the optimum, retain

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

This orientation agrees with accepted1252b8823bb5a290. All statements
about a full certificate would have to retain independent physical
formal gradients; no new PSD certificate is asserted in this report.
The score is W=2R, namely2f_n/||x_0||^2 about the specified minimizer.

Fix an integer m>=1536 ONCE. Throughout every child uses

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).

Let h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, D=D_m=1+2S, R=n-1, and

    A=h_R=1+a, J=2^(m-1)-1,
    t=a*c^(m-1), T=h_J=1+t.

Here T is the actual whole-root step; A is the actual last leaf.
The complete prefix set is I={star,0,...,R}; the actual terminal is n.
No tail, leaf, child, query, or original row is removed.

We will use only the following deliberately loose parameter bounds:

    12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2, T>18,
    D>36, S/T<15/2, D/T<16, D<2*c^m.

Here are their symbolic justifications. Since sqrt(2)>7/5 and
sqrt(2)<10/7, and epsilon<=1/6144,
12/5<c<(17/7)*(6145/6144)<5/2. Also beta>99/100, so
a>(99/100)/(5/2)>1/3, while a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.
For v=a/(c-2), one has0<v<1 because
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Literal concatenation gives

    1+S_j=(1-v)*2^j+v*c^j.                             (2.1)

This identity follows by substituting into
1+S_(j+1)=2*(1+S_j)+a*c^j and checking j=0; no recurrence
was executed. For j>=1, (2.1) is strictly less than c^j.
Consequently D=2*(1+S_m)-1<2*c^m, and
S/T<c^m/(a*c^(m-1))=c/a<15/2. Moreover
T>1+c^(m-1)/3>18, and D/T=1/T+2S/T<16.
Every step exceeds1, so S>n and D>2^(m+1)-1>36.
These bounds apply to EVERY declared m with the SAME parameters.

## 3. Exact complete-prefix functional and the explicit selector

Put

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    y_star=delta_star=0.

The original rows Q_(star,i)<=0 imply delta_i>=0.
For a probability alpha on I define

    Psi(alpha)=2*sum_i alpha_i*delta_i
                         +||x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i||^2.

Accepted1252 constructs the maximal legal prefix extension F from

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),
    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).

We jointly derive the finite variational step used here:

    2F(x_n)=min_(alpha probability on ALL I) Psi(alpha). (3.1)

For any alpha, H dominates its alpha-average quadratic; completing
the square in v gives2F(x_n)<=Psi(alpha). A minimizing alpha exists
by compactness. Put V=x_n-sum alpha_i*y_i at this minimum.
The partial derivatives of Psi/2 are delta_i-<V,y_i>. Probability
exchanges show they have a common value d on positive coordinates
and are at least d elsewhere. Thus
max_i(<y_i,V>-delta_i)=-d equals its alpha-average. Evaluating
<x_n,V>-H(V) now gives Psi(alpha)/2. This proves the reverse
inequality and (3.1), with the full vector geometry retained.

For each actual finite anchor define

    d_i=||y_i||^2, b_i=<x_n,y_i>-delta_i.

If d_i>0, set theta_i=clip_[0,1](b_i/d_i); if d_i=0 set theta_i=0.
Put

    Psi_i=||x_n-theta_i*y_i||^2+2theta_i*delta_i,
    M_1=min_(0<=i<=R) Psi_i.

The selector is the least original index attaining this finite minimum.
It is exactly the stated rule; choosing a particular anchor later is
only a valid upper bound on M_1 unless optimality is actually proved.
If d_i=0, b_i=-delta_i<=0, so the zero convention is minimizing.
For d>0 define the scalar gain

    G(b,d)=0                  if b<=0,
           b^2/d              if0<b<d,
           2b-d               if b>=d.

At b=0,d the adjacent formulas agree. Set G(b,0)=0 for the only
legal case b<=0. Completing the scalar square yields exactly

    M_1=||x_n||^2-max_i G(b_i,d_i).                    (3.2)

There is no unevaluated one-variable optimization left. The chain
for every legal actual terminal is

    2f_n <=2F(x_n) <=M_1.                             (3.3)

The assigned quantitative assertion is

    M_1 <=17||x_0||^2/(16D)                           (3.4)

for EVERY legal original H_m prefix in EVERY finite dimension.
A family passing (3.4) is not a proof of this assertion.

## 4. Exact root-sign-reversing quadratic screen

Choose the ONE global scalar function

    kappa=2/T, f(x)=kappa*x^2/2, x_0=1.

It is convex differentiable and1-smooth because0<kappa<1; its
unique minimizer is0 and the true initial radius is1. All physical
updates are exactly

    x_i=product_(s<i)(1-kappa*h_s), g_i=kappa*x_i,
    x_n=P=product_(s<n)(1-kappa*h_s).

Products here are symbolic identities, not computed trajectories.
For every ordered pair, including the terminal and optimum,

    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.           (4.1)

The root multiplier is -1. Every other step is at most1+t/c;
with z_i=kappa*h_i,

    0<z_i<=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)<9/10      (i!=J).        (4.2)

For the last comparison use c>12/5 and t>17: after multiplying
positive denominators it is enough that
2+(5/6)t<(9/10)(1+t), which holds for t>33/2.
Every non-root multiplier is therefore in(0,1). All x_i before
and at the root query are positive. All subsequent x_i are negative
and strictly increase toward0. In particular P<0, and x_R is the
negative prefix value closest to0.

Both physical immediate-child roots have
z_i=2*(1+t/c)/(1+t)>2/c>4/5. Their multipliers are each less
than1/5; all other non-root factors have magnitude less than1.
Consequently

    P^2<1/625, D*kappa*P^2<32/625<1/16.             (4.3)

Thus this function's ACTUAL score is below1/(16D).

Let b=1-kappa*A. The bounds above give1/2<b<1 and
x_R=P/b. Every prefix anchor has

    y_i=(1-kappa)*x_i,
    delta_i=kappa*(1-kappa)*x_i^2/2.

For R the optimal scalar weight is

    theta_R=(b-kappa/2)/(1-kappa)
            =[1-kappa*(A+1/2)]/(1-kappa),
    0<theta_R<1.                                    (4.4)

Its positivity follows from kappa*(A+1/2)<4/T<1; the upper
bound uses A+1/2>1. Direct substitution gives

    Psi_R=P^2-[P-(kappa/2)*x_R]^2
         =[kappa*b-kappa^2/4]*x_R^2
         <kappa*x_R^2<4*kappa*P^2<128/(625D)<1/(4D).
                                                               (4.5)

We now evaluate the TRUE full-prefix functional, rather than assuming
this anchor is best. Set v_*=kappa*x_R/2<0. At every original anchor,

    <y_i,v_*>-delta_i
      =[kappa*(1-kappa)/2]*x_i*(x_R-x_i)<=0.          (4.6)

For positive x_i, the second factor is negative. For every negative
prefix value, x_i<=x_R, the second factor is nonnegative. Equality
in (4.6) occurs only at i=R; star also has value0. Thus the maximum
in H(v_*) is0. Furthermore x_n=v_*+theta_R*y_R by (4.4), and
0<theta_R<1. Evaluation of the defining conjugate at v_* gives

    2F(x_n)>=2P*v_*-v_*^2=Psi_R.

Together with (3.1) and the valid star/R probability this proves

    2F(x_n)=M_1=Psi_R<1/(4D).                        (4.7)

The last anchor is also the UNIQUE best finite anchor here. Indeed
the gap between any alpha-average and the maximum at v_* is a sum
of nonnegative terms, strictly positive for positive mass at any
finite i!=R by (4.6). Equality in the square-completion bound also
forces residual v_*. A star-only choice would have residual P!=v_*.
Hence any exact minimizing probability has positive mass at R and
no other finite node. In particular no other single-anchor law ties R.
This argument is specific to this legal quadratic; it is not a general
last-anchor theorem.

For comparison to the previous round, the physical occupation average
has y_bar=(1-kappa)*(1-P)/(kappa*S)>0 by summing the actual GD
updates, whereas x_n=P<0. Its optimum scalar mix with star is zero,
giving surrogate P^2. Equation(4.7) displays exactly why the present
adaptive support repairs that earlier failure. No conclusion about
all other prefixes follows.

## 5. Exact Huber contact and its deterministic selector

Put k=1/D and define the ONE global scalar Huber function

    f_H(x)=x^2/2                       if |x|<=k,
           k*|x|-k^2/2                 if |x|>=k,
    x_0=1.

Its derivative clip(x,-k,k) is continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz.
The unique minimizer is0. At original cumulative mass
s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, all original prefix and terminal points satisfy

    x_i=1-k*s_i>k, g_i=k,
    f_i=k*x_i-k^2/2,
    y_i=x_i-k>0, delta_i=k*y_i.

Indeed s_i<=S and1-k*S=(S+1)/D>k. All updates use the original
steps; finite-to-finite Q rows vanish. Rows to and from star are
Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-k*y_i<=0. The terminal obeys the same
identities. The closest initial radius is1.

Let z=x_n-k=S/D>0. For every actual prefix index i,

    y_i>=y_R=x_n+a*k>z,
    theta_i=z/y_i in(0,1),
    Psi_i=2k*x_n-k^2=1/D.                            (5.1)

The equality follows from the scalar minimizer x_n-theta_i*y_i=k.
For the full probability simplex, delta_i=k*y_i including star, and
its barycenter y ranges over[0,y_0]. Thus (3.1) minimizes
2k*y+(x_n-y)^2 over that interval; its minimum is at y=z and equals
1/D. Equivalently the actual Huber value already attains this upper
bound. Hence

    2f_H(x_n)=2F(x_n)=M_1=1/D.                        (5.2)

EVERY prefix anchor is best. Under the stipulated least-index tie
break, the selected index is0. This is an explicit reason not to
identify the rule with a fixed last-anchor law.

## 6. A genuine two-dimensional positive-height screen

Take arbitrary u>0 and v!=0. Define a scalar Huber function with
threshold k_u=u/D by the formula of Section5, replacing k by k_u.
On R^2 use the ONE static function

    f(z,w)=f_(H,u)(z)+(kappa/2)*w^2,
    x_0=(u,v), kappa=2/T.

Its Hessian bounds, or directly its coordinate gradients, show convexity
and1-Lipschitz gradient. Its unique minimizer is(0,0), so the exact
initial radius squared is u^2+v^2. Every original iterate is

    z_i=u*(1-s_i/D),
    w_i=v*product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t),
    g_i=(u/D,kappa*w_i).

The first coordinate remains on the positive affine Huber ray through
the actual terminal. The second has exactly the one root sign change
from Section4. All original updates hold coordinatewise. Every Q row
is the sum of the Huber row calculated in Section5 and the nonpositive
quadratic row in(4.1), including optimum and actual terminal rows.
This proves legality of the entire original transcript, not only of
its collapsed endpoint data.

It is genuinely multidirectional. The first two gradients have
nonzero determinant

    det(g_0,g_1)=-(u/D)*kappa^2*A*v !=0,

because the first physical step is A. Therefore the trajectory is
not a scalar common-gradient embedding.

Evaluate the allowed R segment at theta=1, without claiming its
optimum is attained there and without choosing different coordinate
weights. Its Huber contribution is exactly

    u^2/D+A^2*u^2/D^2.

To check, at R the first-coordinate y is z_n+a*k_u and
x_n-y=-a*k_u; hence2delta_R+|x_n-y|^2 differs from the true
Huber score u^2/D by (a+1)^2*k_u^2=A^2*u^2/D^2.
The quadratic contribution is

    [kappa*(1-kappa)+a^2*kappa^2]*v^2*(P/b)^2
       <kappa*v^2*(P/b)^2
       <128*v^2/(625D),                              (6.1)

using a<1 and (4.3). Because A^2<9/4 and D>36,

    M_1 <=Psi_R(theta=1)
        <(17/16)*u^2/D+(128/625)*v^2/D
        <17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                         (6.2)

The same upper bound includes the zero-amplitude endpoints by the
already treated scalar cases. No optimizer or executed product is
needed. The TRUE full-prefix functional obeys, on this same prefix,

    u^2/D+kappa*v^2*P^2 <=2F(x_n)
       <=M_1<17*(u^2+v^2)/(16D).                     (6.3)

The lower inequality uses this actual globally defined function;
the upper inequality uses(3.3). We do not assert exact equality for
the two-dimensional full functional. These bounds suffice to show
that this rotating-gradient test is not a selector failure or full-W
failure. They do not cover arbitrary two-dimensional functions.

## 7. The entire zero-height legal-prefix class

Here start from ANY legal original prefix with delta_i=0 for EVERY
finite i. Do not assume that a given cloud of convex-hull points is
a GD trajectory. Retain all original ordered rows. Define the ONE
compact convex set

    K=conv{0,y_0,...,y_R}.

Since f_i=||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i, direct cancellation gives

    Q_ij=<g_i,y_j-y_i>                               (7.1)

for every ordered pair, including star. Thus all original rows imply
<g_i,z-y_i><=0 for all z in K. As y_i belongs to K and x_i=y_i+g_i,
for every z in K one has

    ||x_i-z||^2
      =||g_i||^2+||z-y_i||^2-2<g_i,z-y_i>
      >=||g_i||^2.

The unique nearest point in K to x_i is therefore y_i. Consequently

    y_i=P_K(x_i), g_i=x_i-P_K(x_i)                   (7.2)

at EVERY original queried index, with no free reassignment of anchors.

For clarity this also constructs a legal static global function:

    f_K(x)=dist(x,K)^2/2.

A nearest point exists by compactness, and uniqueness follows from
strict convexity of squared norm along a segment of K. Projection
optimality gives <x-P_K(x),z-P_K(x)><=0 for z in K. Applying this
at x and x' and adding proves

    <P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'> >=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2.

Hence g_K(x)=x-P_K(x) is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the distance values
using P_K(x) and P_K(x') as trial points shows
f_K(x+d)<=f_K(x)+<g_K(x),d>+||d||^2/2 and, by reversing the
comparison, f_K(x+d)>=f_K(x)+<g_K(x+d),d>-||d||^2/2.
Continuity of g_K proves differentiability with gradient g_K.
For convexity, a convex combination of the projections is a valid
point in K; convexity of squared norm then proves the convexity
inequality for f_K. Thus f_K is finite convex differentiable and
1-smooth, with its complete minimum set exactly K. It interpolates
all queried points, gradients, and values by(7.2), and follows the
original GD trajectory. The designated minimum0 belongs to K and
is explicitly valid. If desired choose the closest point P_K(x_0)
as the reference minimum; the bound below holds for that minimum too.

In this case(3.1) additionally evaluates the TRUE maximal prefix value:

    2F(x_n)=min_(y in K)||x_n-y||^2=dist(x_n,K)^2.    (7.3)

For each original step, its already computed projection y_i is a
valid trial point for the NEXT distance. Therefore

    dist(x_(i+1),K)
      <=||x_i-h_i*g_i-y_i||
      =(h_i-1)*dist(x_i,K),                         (7.4)

because every h_i>1. Only the original geometry yields this bound.
At the actual last anchor, theta=1 gives

    M_1<=||x_n-y_R||^2
       =a^2*dist(x_R,K)^2
       <=[product_(i<n)(h_i-1)]^2*dist(x_0,K)^2.     (7.5)

The product has a closed symbolic value for the physical hierarchy.
A level ell step1+a*c^ell occurs2^(m-1-ell) times. The multiplicity
sum is n and the level-weighted sum is n-m (both follow by the
literal binary concatenation, or induction on these finite sums).
Consequently

    product_(i<n)(h_i-1)
       =a^n*c^(n-m)=beta^n*c^(-m).                  (7.6)

No trajectory, recurrence, or product was numerically evaluated.
Using D<2*c^m and0<beta<1,

    D*beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)<2*c^(-m)<1.

Thus for every such prefix and every specified minimum0,

    M_1<=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*dist(x_0,K)^2
        <=beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)*||x_0||^2
        <||x_0||^2/D                                (7.7)

when x_0!=0; if the distance is zero the non-strict zero bound
already suffices. Since any supplied legal prefix interpolant has
terminal value at most F(x_n), this proves the assigned inequality
for the ENTIRE zero-height legal-prefix class, including all of its
multidirectional configurations. A nonzero initial radius for f_K
is dist(x_0,K); if x_0 belongs to K, all queried gradients and the
actual terminal error are zero, a harmless degenerate case.

An unconstrained convex-hull configuration can have a large gap
between distance to its hull and distance to all rays[0,y_i]. It
cannot refute the assigned selector through delta_i=0: the GD and
normal conditions(7.1)--(7.4) force the last ray already to be close
enough at the required quantitative scale. This distinction uses
all the original rows and the actual last leaf.

## 7b. A quantitative small-positive-height extension

The preceding geometry need not be assumed when the heights are small.
For ANY legal original prefix put

    Delta=max_(0<=i<=R) delta_i, r_i=h_i-1>0.

Direct expansion of the original row, using
x_(i+1)=y_i-r_i*g_i and y_(i+1)=x_(i+1)-g_(i+1),

    Q_(i+1,i)=delta_(i+1)-delta_i
                +r_i*<g_i,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2<=0.

Consequently

    r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2delta_i-2delta_(i+1)-||g_(i+1)||^2
       =||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2-2Q_(i+1,i)>=0,

and hence

    ||g_(i+1)||^2<=r_i^2*||g_i||^2+2Delta          (i<R).
                                                               (7b.1)

Both optimum rows at0 imply||g_0||^2<=<g_0,x_0>, so
||g_0||<=||x_0||. Applying(7b.1) successively, by finite induction
as an inequality rather than an executed recurrence, and evaluating
the same R segment at theta=1, gives

    M_1 <=2delta_R+a^2||g_R||^2
        <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,                 (7b.2)

where the two explicit physical word functionals are

    p_j=product_(i in H_j)(h_i-1)^2
       =beta^(2(2^j-1))*c^(-2j),
    e_j=sum_(i in H_j) product_(t in H_j,t>i)(h_t-1)^2,
    p_0=1, e_0=0.

Here indices inside a child are its original chronological order;
the SAME enclosing m parameters are used. We now bound e_m
quantitatively, rather than leaving its size to a recurrence oracle.
Concatenation as left child, root, right child gives the identity

    e_(j+1)=M_j*e_j+p_j,
    M_j=1+a^2*c^(2j)*p_j
       =1+a^2*beta^(2(2^j-1)).                     (7b.3)

The left-child suffix factors cross the actual root and whole right
child; the root contribution is p_j; the right-child contribution
is e_j. This proves(7b.3) with every physical suffix retained.
As all M_j>=1, expansion of this identity as a finite sum yields

    e_m <=[product_(j=0)^(m-1) M_j]*sum_(j=0)^(m-1)p_j.
                                                               (7b.4)

Let q=ceil(log_2(4m)); q is only an analytic splitting index, not a
new schedule parameter. Then4m<=2^q<8m. For j<q,
M_j<5/4<sqrt(2), and hence the product of at most q such factors
is less than2^(q/2)<3sqrt(m).
For j=q+r with r>=0, use log(beta)<=-1/(4m) and
2^j-1>=2^(j-1) to obtain

    beta^(2(2^j-1))<=exp(-2^j/(4m))<=exp(-2^r).

Since2^r>=r+1, the sum over r>=0 is at most
sum_(r>=0)exp(-(r+1))=1/(exp(1)-1)<1.
The logarithm of the product of all remaining M_j is thus less
than a^2<1/4, using log(1+s)<=s for s>=0. This product is less
than exp(1/4)<4/3. The last inequality follows by comparing the
exponential series term by term with the geometric series at1/4.
These bounds also apply if the finite tail is empty.
Consequently product M_j<4sqrt(m). Furthermore
p_j<=c^(-2j)<=4^(-j), so sum p_j<4/3. Equation(7b.4) gives

    e_m<(16/3)*sqrt(m)<6sqrt(m).                    (7b.5)

The constants are valid for EVERY m>=1536. No arithmetic, product,
or recurrence was evaluated by a program. The product decay already
proved above gives D*p_m<2c^(-m)<1/16 for these m. Therefore

    M_1 <=p_m*||x_0||^2+2Delta*e_m,
    M_1 <||x_0||^2/(16D)+12sqrt(m)*Delta            if Delta>0.
                                                               (7b.6)

For Delta=0 the first inequality directly gives the stronger bound.
In particular the assigned target inequality holds for EVERY legal
original prefix satisfying the explicit positive-height condition

    max_i delta_i <=||x_0||^2/(12D*sqrt(m)).         (7b.7)

This is a uniform quantitative sufficient condition, not a conjecture
about all prefixes. It uses an available point on the SAME single-
anchor segment and does not introduce any new terminal probability
law. It does not claim that a failed (7b.7) forces selector failure;
Huber is an example with larger heights which passes by Section5.
No new minimizer or function is needed: (7b.1)--(7b.7) apply to the
supplied legal static prefix and its original chosen minimizer.


## 8. Exact remaining positive-height interface and failed transfers

Let chi=17/(16D) and E=||x_n||^2-chi*||x_0||^2.
If E<=0 the optimum-only choice theta=0 proves(3.4). If E>0,
(3.2) proves that(3.4) is equivalent to the existence of an actual
finite anchor with d_i>0 such that

    b_i>=sqrt(E*d_i)              if d_i>=E,
    b_i>=(E+d_i)/2                if0<d_i<E.         (8.1)

To verify the cases, solve G(b_i,d_i)>=E on its two increasing
positive branches; at d_i=E they both require b_i>=E.
The threshold requires b_i>0, so the zero-gain branch cannot help.
Equation(8.1) is an exact finite geometry condition, not its proof.
Its unproved quantifier is: every positive-height legal original
H_m prefix satisfies one of these original-anchor inequalities.

Two direct proof routes were screened within Mechanism1.

First, a stronger sufficient last-anchor estimate would be

    2f_R-(1-a^2)||g_R||^2 <=chi*||x_0||^2.           (8.2)

Its left side is exactly Psi_R(theta=1), so(8.2) would imply(3.4).
No bound(8.2) for arbitrary legal prefixes is established here.
Failure of the previous backward-only multiplier support does not
refute(8.2): its negative direction was not interpolable. Conversely,
the family calculations above cannot establish(8.2) outside their
hypotheses. We retain (8.1), rather than replacing the selector by R.

The original optimum rows give a useful exact last-anchor formula.
Write y=y_R, g=g_R, delta=delta_R, d=||y||^2, and

    t_R=a*<g,y>+delta.

From Q_(R,star)<=0 one has <g,y>>=delta>=0, so t_R>=0.
Since x_n=y-a*g, the segment numerator is b=d-t_R. Thus if d>0,

    theta_R=max(0,1-t_R/d),
    Psi_R=2delta+a^2||g||^2-t_R^2/d    when0<=t_R<d,
    Psi_R=||x_n||^2                   when t_R>=d.  (8.3)

At equality the formulas agree. If d=0, the optimum rows imply
delta=0 and the stated zero-anchor convention applies. These are
exact scalar evaluations; bounding their minimum together with
all OTHER anchors remains necessary. They do not leave an unknown
one-dimensional optimizer and do not supply the missing global rate.

Second, the full normal-cone contraction closes the zero-height
case but does not extend unchanged. For general heights, expanding
ALL the original rows gives instead

    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0,
    <g_i,y_j-y_i><=delta_j-delta_i.                  (8.4)

The right side is not zero and has no fixed sign across all pairs.
Therefore y_i need not be the projection of x_i onto the convex
hull K. Discarding delta_i>=0 from Psi while continuing to use
(7.2) or(7.4) is invalid. As a concrete legal check, in the Huber
contact all finite y_i are positive and K=[0,y_0], whereas for a
later query with x_i in[0,y_0] the projection is x_i itself and the
actual y_i=x_i-k differs. The height differences in(8.4) account
for this. Such an i exists, for example i=R: s_R=S-A>1 gives
0<x_R<y_0. All values here use the original Huber trajectory.

Thus no counterexample to the selector has emerged, but the attempted
projection transfer has a concrete failed hypothesis. The unresolved
case requires controlling positive heights and the changed geometry
jointly at scale1/D. It is not a support-representation issue or an
unevaluated scalar minimization.

## 9. Literature, memory, and resource record

Query-memory recovered TASK.md, current guidanceab8fdd0dc7b752a2,
previous quadratic finding1b246cab08578ca1 and its precise failure
scope, and the relevant accepted facts. The first broad combined
retrieval was truncated; the needed local report sections and both
accepted proofs were subsequently read in bounded passages. No

The procedures used were query-memory, direct-proving, and

One Matlas query about common-Hessian conjugate maxima returned

https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666.
The existing own extracted paper text at
`downloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt`
was checked for its smoothness/interpolation definitions. No external

One administrative report-formatting patch returned "Failed to find

Counts: mathematical programs0; numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/

## 10. Cap1 handoff and original target

# A supercritical whole-word obstruction from ruler products

Status: complete joint proof candidate, **UNVERIFIED**. Ninth max4 launch,
second and last allocated round (cumulative21). This work uses no numerical
evaluation, parameter sweep, installation, optimizer, or fact submission.
The only correctness predecessors used below are accepted facts
`1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation and maximal terminal extension)
and `b98bb807d514e80d` (exact benchmark and its elementary growth bound).
Earlier unverified drafts are not proof premises: the movement, return,
hierarchy and terminal arguments are all supplied here.

The new point is a whole-word estimate that permits strictly expanding
high returns. Their gains are interleaved with actual level2 contractions.
Both the total multiplier and the accumulated additive perturbations are
controlled. Thus the zero of the earlier four-ray return coefficient is
not a boundary of this obstruction for the full physical word.

## 1. Definitions and quantitative claim

Fix an integer m>=1024. All subwords below use these SAME enclosing-m
parameters:

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),
    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,
    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2, l=1+a_chr, k=1+delta.

The original word is H_chr,0=() and

    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.

Equivalently h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)) for0<=i<n, where nu_2
is the exponent of2 dividing a positive integer. In particular a levelq
bridge has excess u=a_chr*c_chr^q. No reindexing or parameter substitution
is made inside a subword.

For s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in{0,1}^m, define

    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),
    B(s)=(-1)^s_0, C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m,

where i_j are the binary digits of the original indexi. The map froms to
(B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is a bijection. Set gamma=9/10 and choose beta in(0,1).
For b in{1,-1} write mu_1=-beta,mu_(-1)=gamma, and define probability laws

    A_b(c)=(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)
                +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),
    P_b(c)=product_(j=1)^(m-1) (1+mu_b c_j)/2,
    w_s=(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2
          *[(1-eta)A_(B(s))(C(s))+eta P_(B(s))(C(s))].          (1)

Every w_s is positive and their sum is1. Prescribe only queried gradients

    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<n,                (2)

with arbitrary positive amplitudes Gamma_i. All isometric realizations of
their Gram matrix are included.

Here are exact finite-m quantities, before beta is selected:

    rho=1-epsilon/2, p=1-epsilon, d_eps=gamma epsilon/2,
    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2,
    L_leaf=l/r_leaf, tau_ang=2^-12,
    beta_S=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p+tau_ang)
                                     +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]
                         /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                   (3)

The claim is uniform over

    7/10 <= beta <= beta_S.                                 (4)

Suppose a finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth function F
with a minimizer realizes(2) on its actual GD trajectory
X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i. Its initial anchor, all values, all positive queried
amplitudes, and its unqueried terminal gradient are unrestricted. Let
X_star be a closest minimizer to X_0 and set

    R=||X_0-X_star||, F_star=min F,
    T_path=sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i,
    V_chr=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2.

Then the candidate bounds are

    R>0, T_path<8192R, ||X_i-X_star||<512R for0<=i<=n,         (5)
    ||grad F(X_n)||<=R[2(3/4)^(N/8)+2^26*m/N^2],             (6)
    V_chr<=2048(3/4)^(N/8)+2^36*m/N^2.                      (7)

Let beta_R denote(3) with tau_ang replaced by0. We will prove

    4/5<beta_R<beta_S<6/7,
    beta_S -> sqrt((1+sqrt(2)+tau_ang)/(2+sqrt(2)))
                  >2^(-1/4).                               (8)

Thus(7) extends by a fixed positive angular interval past beta_R; it is
not just a shrinking finite-epsilon correction. At beta=beta_S the exact
return coefficient defined below equals -tau_ang, strictly negative.

For the EXACT benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, accepted
`b98bb807d514e80d` gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Therefore

    B_n V_chr <=(1+4n^(log_2 3))
           [2048(3/4)^((n+1)/8)+2^36*log_2(n+1)/(n+1)^2]
          ->0, n=2^m-1.                                    (9)

The same estimate holds for the maximal queried-prefix interpolant and
all reduced-feasible terminal values in the precise sense of section8.
This is an upper bound on the strength of the specified character law,
not on the unrestricted W_n(H_chr,m). The original all-schedule minimax
target remains open.

## 2. Original inequalities and finite parameter estimates

The minimizer set is nonempty and closed. In finite dimension, a bounded
distance-minimizing sequence has a convergent subsequence, so a closest
minimizer exists. A positive initial gradient implies R>0. Translate
X_star to0, and subtract F_star from F.

Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the necessary original inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,
    f_i=F(X_i),

including the optimum jet(X_star,g_star,f_star)=(0,0,0). Adding the two
orientations yields

    ||g_i-g_j||^2<=<g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                        (10)

In particular <g_i,X_i>>=||g_i||^2 and ||g_i||<=||X_i||.
An actual step X'=X-hg satisfies

    ||X'||^2<=||X||^2-h(2-h)||g||^2.

It follows that distances do not increase for0<=h<=2; for h>=2 they
increase by a factor at most h-1. The same statements hold for gradient
norms: put g'=grad F(X'), A=||g||, D=||g'|| in(10) to get

    D^2+(h-2)<g',g><=(h-1)A^2.

For h<=2 use <g',g><=AD; for h>=2 use <g',g>>=-AD. Factoring the
resulting quadratic gives

    ||g'||<=max(1,h-1)||g||.                                (11)

If A=0 then X'=X and the conclusion still holds. These inequalities are
valid for the actual terminal gradient without prescribing its direction.

The enclosing parameters obey

    7/5<l<3/2, 199/100<k<2, c_chr<5/2,
    1<delta*c_chr<5/2, a_chr<1/2.                            (12)

For example sqrt(2)<99/70 and m>=1024 give c_chr<49/20, while
delta>99/100 yields a_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5. Also c_chr>2,
so a_chr<1/2. Finally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2) lies between1 and5/2.

Set, for the movement calculation,

    lambda=rho(1-eta), omega=1-lambda, e_mix=omega/lambda,
    d_mass=(l+k)/(l+1+(1-l)e_mix),
    r_0=sqrt(omega)R/lambda.

The powers of2 give omega<=epsilon/2+eta and

    e_mix<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,
    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255, p>999/1000,
    11/8<d_mass<3/2.                                       (13)

For the last pair of bounds, the denominator of d_mass is at mostl+1,
so d_mass>=1+(k-1)/(l+1)>349/250>11/8. Subtract l+2 from
(3/2)[l+1-(l-1)e_mix]; the result is
(l-1)(1/2-3e_mix/2)>0, proving the upper bound. All the mixture bounds
follow already from omega<1/65536, strengthened if necessary by m>=1024.

The EXACT coarse correlations are

    z=-rho beta+d_eps, w=-rho beta-d_eps,
    alpha_ret=1-kz^2+L_leaf(p-zw),
    alpha_0=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2.                               (14)

At an original leaf, the full-law correlation of its endpoints isp.
Writing r for the positive amplitude ratio, (10) becomes

    r^2+(l-2)p r-(l-1)<=0.

The unique positive root is r_leaf in(3). Evaluating this polynomial atp
and1 gives p<=r_leaf<1. Hence l<=L_leaf<=l+3epsilon. For beta in[0,1],
z^2>=beta^2-2epsilon and p-zw<=1-beta^2+epsilon, whence

    alpha_ret<=alpha_0+9epsilon.                            (15)

To check the last constant, the extra terms are bounded by
2k epsilon+3epsilon(1-beta^2)+l epsilon+3epsilon^2<9epsilon.
Direct substitution in(14) gives the complete quadratic

    alpha_ret=1+L_leaf p-(k+L_leaf)rho^2 beta^2
                    +2k rho d_eps beta+(L_leaf-k)d_eps^2.   (16)

Its constant term is positive, its leading coefficient is negative,
and it has exactly one positive zero. Solving alpha_ret=-tau_ang gives
precisely beta_S. Solving alpha_ret=0 gives beta_R. At beta=6/7,
the bounds l<3/2,k>199/100 in(15) give

    alpha_ret<-157/2450+9epsilon<-tau_ang.

For the second strict inequality use epsilon<2^-20 and
9*2^-20+2^-12<1/1000<157/2450. At beta=4/5, z^2,zw<=beta^2,
p-zw>0 and L_leaf>=l give
alpha_ret>=alpha_0-l epsilon>28/125-(3/2)epsilon>0.
This proves the finite bounds in(8) and, throughout(4),

    alpha_ret>=-tau_ang,
    (l+k)beta^2<=l+1+tau_ang+9epsilon,
    -z>=2/3, p-zw>0.                                      (17)

The last two claims follow from beta>=7/10,beta<6/7 and(13).
As m tends to infinity, epsilon->0, r_leaf->1, l->sqrt(2), k->2.
Taking limits in the explicit positive root proves(8).

## 3. Uniform movement BEFORE estimating perturbations

Separate from(1) its common-prefix component with B=1, of masslambda.
Within this component the directions repeat a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, where
||a_0||=||b_0||=1 and <a_0,b_0>=-beta. Consequently the full gradients
have an isometric direct-sum realization

    g_i=Gamma_i(sqrt(lambda)u_i,sqrt(omega)v_i),
    u_i in{a_0,b_0}, ||v_i||=1.                              (18)

This follows by realizing the two mixture Gram matrices in orthogonal
spaces. The isometry on the gradient span can be extended to the initial
displacement, adding orthogonal space when needed; it preservesR. Write
X_0=(x^c,x^r).

At each block start t=4r,0<=t<=N-4, let P_a,P_b denote the total
previous physical step masses h_i Gamma_i on a_0,b_0, and put T=P_a+P_b.
Define

    A_x=<a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta P_b,
    B_x=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta P_a.

Write A=Gamma_t,C=Gamma_(t+1). The first leaf gives A>=C by(11).
The remainder displacement before t has norm at most sqrt(omega)T.
At the QUERIED sample t+1, its optimum inequality in(10) therefore gives

    A_x>=l(1-e_mix)A+(1+e_mix)C-r_0-e_mix T.                 (19)

Indeed the remainder contribution after that leaf, divided bylambda C,
is at most r_0+e_mix(T+lA), and the squared gradient term divided by
lambda C is C/lambda=(1+e_mix)C.

Let D_a=lA+kC be the a-mass added in the first two steps of this block.
For0<=r<=1, the ratio
(l+kr)/[l(1-e_mix)+(1+e_mix)r] increases, because
k(1-e_mix)-(1+e_mix)>0 by(12),(13). Its value at1 is d_mass. Thus

    D_a<=d_mass(A_x+r_0+e_mix T).                            (20)

At the QUERIED sample t+2, the direction is b_0. Its optimum inequality,
with its positive squared gradient term dropped, gives

    B_x+(beta+e_mix)D_a+r_0+e_mix T>=0.                     (21)

Substitute(20) and expand A_x,B_x,T. The resulting inequality is

    c_a P_a+c_b P_b
      <=<b_0+d_mass(beta+e_mix)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                                 +[1+d_mass(beta+e_mix)]r_0,
    c_a=(beta+e_mix)[d_mass(1-e_mix)-1],
    c_b=1-e_mix-d_mass(beta+e_mix)^2.                        (22)

Here c_a>1/4. Since beta+e_mix<1, the right side is<4R by(13).
Put xi=max(0,-c_b). The exact numerator of -c_b, on its positive
denominator D_mass=l+1+(1-l)e_mix>9/4, is

    (l+k)beta^2-(l+1)+[2(l+k)beta+2l]e_mix+(k+1)e_mix^2.

The last two coefficients are<10 and<3. Also epsilon<=2e_mix, because
omega>=epsilon/2. Hence(17) implies

    xi<tau_ang/2+13e_mix,
    c_a-5xi>1/4-(5/2)tau_ang-65/1000>1/8.                  (23)

Rearrange(21), using the definitions rather than substituting(20):

    (1-e_mix)P_b<=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                              +(beta+e_mix)(P_a+D_a)+r_0.

Consequently, whenever a next block start exists,

    P_b<=2R+2P_a(next).                                   (24)

The constants2 follow from
[(1/sqrt(lambda))+sqrt(omega)/lambda]/(1-e_mix)<2 and
(beta+e_mix)/(1-e_mix)<2.

We close the mass estimate at the ACTUAL last block start N-4.
Its preceding block starts at N-8, and their separating bridge has
original level2 and size H_2=1+delta*c_chr<7/2. In that preceding
block put C=Gamma_(N-7). The next two short steps k,l give both b-ray
amplitudes at mostC. The b-mass added is at most(l+H_2)C, while its
a-mass D_a is at leastkC. Since(l+H_2)/k<3, (24) gives

    P_b(last)<2R+5P_a(last).                               (25)

If c_b<0, use(25) in(22):

    (c_a-5xi)P_a(last)<(4+2xi)R<5R.

Thus P_a(last)<40R by(23). If c_b>=0, (22) directly gives
P_a(last)<16R. Nonnegative chronological a-mass is nondecreasing, so
P_a<64R at EVERY block start. By(24), P_b<130R at each start<=N-8.
Therefore T<194R and ||X_t||<195R at these starts. The next three
steps l,k,l are short and keep this distance bound. They cover all
samples through N-5. The final level2 bridge increases distance by
at most H_2-1=delta*c_chr<5/2, and the final short steps cannot increase
it. Hence ||X_i||<512R at every sample, including the terminal.

There are seven physical steps from N-8 through n-1. Their sum is
4l+2k+H_2<14, and their gradients have norm<512R. This proves

    T_path<194R+14*512R<8192R,
    K_path:=R+T_path<2^14R.                                 (26)

This calculation occurs before the eta-error estimate. In particular it
does not divide by c_b, alpha_ret,1-beta,epsilon or eta. The explicit
positive coefficient in(23) replaces any circular boundedness assumption.

## 4. Original return pairs with a negative angular coefficient

Now separate the ENTIRE common-prefix component in(1), of mass1-eta.
Writing V for its common C_j sign, the four chronological unit directions
are

    a_e=1, a_o=B, b_e=V, b_o=BV.

Their correlations are <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,
<a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z and <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w, with the exact
finite-epsilon formulas(14). The full-law correlation differs from its
common-prefix correlation by at most2eta for any pair of directions.

For an original endpoint pair with amplitudes A,D and intervening total
physical mass T_ij, replacing full correlations by common-prefix ones
in(10) creates error of absolute value at most

    2eta(A+D)T_ij+4eta AD<=4eta K_path(A+D).

Set e_0=4eta K_path; here A,D<=K_path follows from(26) or from the
original displacement bound. This estimate does not hide an amplified
path length inside an error symbol.

Take an original queried odd-b return j=4r+3 with j+4<n. Its physical
steps and amplitudes are, in order,

    j: H on b_o with B_0,
    j+1: l on a_e with A_0,
    j+2: k on a_o with C_0,
    j+3: l on b_e with E_0,
    j+4: b_o with D_0.

Put bbar=-z>0, M=B_0+bbar C_0, Delta=D_0-B_0, and in the common
component let Z=-H B_0 b_o-l A_0 a_e and X=<b_o,Z>.
The component of Z in direction a_o-zb_o has scalar product
-l A_0(p-zw). The ORIGINAL pair(j,j+2) therefore yields

    C_0^2+B_0^2-2zB_0C_0
      <=(zC_0-B_0)X-l A_0 C_0(p-zw)+e_0(C_0+B_0).           (27)

When Delta>0, the ORIGINAL pair(j,j+4) gives

    X>=Delta+kC_0 z+lE_0p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.               (28)

Since zC_0-B_0=-M<0, substitute(28) in(27). The actual leaf atj+1
gives A_0>=C_0/r_leaf. Using p-zw>0 obtains

    alpha_ret C_0^2+bbar(2-k)B_0C_0+B_0^2
                           +M Delta+MlE_0p
      <=e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                 (29)

No endpoint here is formal or unqueried. In particular the high-step
size H has not been replaced by a limiting bridge size.

Allow alpha_ret>=-tau_ang. If Delta>=2m e_0, use bbar>=2/3,
C_0+B_0<=(3/2)M and2-k=1/m in(29). Its right side is at most
(5/2)e_0M+B_0M/m. Cancel the term(bbar/m)B_0C_0 and retain the
nonnegative coefficient(1-1/m)B_0^2. Dropping this and MlE_0p gives

    Delta<=tau_ang C_0^2/M+(5/2)e_0
           <=(3/2)tau_ang C_0+(5/2)e_0.

If0<Delta<2m e_0 the following common bound already holds; it also
holds if Delta<=0. Thus

    D_0<=B_0+(3/2)tau_ang C_0+2m e_0.                       (30)

Let q>=2 be the original level of the high bridge H=1+u. Equations
(11),(12) give C_0<=uB_0, since the high step is followed by a short
leaf and u>1. Therefore every such queried return obeys

    D_0<=(1+(3/2)tau_ang u)B_0+d_amp,
    d_amp=2^17*m*eta*R.                                   (31)

Indeed2m e_0=8m eta K_path<d_amp. Since a_chr<1/2,c_chr<5/2, a
convenient level-dependent upper multiplier is

    G_q=1+tau_ang(5/2)^q, q>=3.                            (32)

These multipliers can be greater than1 and grow withq. We do not claim
individual supercritical high returns are nonexpanding.

## 5. Exact full-law contraction at every actual level2 return

For an original levelq bridge and its following leaf, let their three
amplitudes be A,M,D and their steps1+u,l. Their relative character
signs are1,BC_q,C_q, because incrementing the original bridge index
flips exactly the lowerq+1 bits, and the next leaf flips bit0.
The full-law correlations are consequently w,p,z. Equation(10) is
equivalently

    0>=-uA^2+D^2+(u-1)zAD+lM(pD-wA).                       (33)

The leaf is short, so M>=D. Also pD-wA>0. For t=D/A>0, it follows
that

    F_u(t):=(1+lp)t^2+[(u-1)z-lw]t-u<=0.                   (34)

This quadratic has exactly one positive root. Since beta<6/7<gamma,
-w>=beta and -z<=beta. Moreover1+lp>239/100.
At level1, u=delta<1, the middle coefficient exceeds l beta>49/50.
Thus

    F_delta(1/2)>239/400+49/100-1>0.

Every level1 bridge-plus-leaf gain is<1/2. At level2,
1<u=delta*c_chr<5/2 and
[(u-1)z-lw]>=beta(1+l-u)>-1/10. Hence

    F_u(6/5)>239*36/2500-3/25-5/2>0.

Every level2 bridge-plus-leaf gain is<6/5. A complete four-step
level2 return consists of this compound followed by a level1 compound,
so its gain is less than

    theta=3/5.                                             (35)

This bound is for the FULL measure, with no additive perturbation and
with arbitrary adjacent interpolation slack.

## 6. A ruler-product estimate and bounded accumulated forcing

This section is an explicit scalar calculation, proved here as part of
the joint candidate. It is not imported from an unverified finding.
Put t0=tau_ang=2^-12 and define gains

    G_2=theta=3/5, G_q=1+t0(5/2)^q forq>=3.

Let W_s be the finite scalar level word of length2^s-1 with entries
2+nu_2(r),1<=r<=2^s-1. Thus W_1=(2) and

    W_s=(W_(s-1),s+1,W_(s-1)), s>=2.

At level2 apply the scalar map y->theta y. At any higher levelq apply
y->G_q y+d, where d>=0 is fixed. Composing in chronological order over
W_s gives a map

    y -> P_s y+E_s d,
    P_1=theta, E_1=0,
    P_s=G_(s+1)P_(s-1)^2,
    E_s=(1+G_(s+1)P_(s-1))E_(s-1)+P_(s-1).                (36)

We claim, for every integer s>=1,

    P_s<=(3/4)^(2^(s-1)), E_s<256.                         (37)

First expand the multiplicative recurrence:

    log P_s=2^(s-1)[log theta
                         +sum_(q=3)^(s+1)2^(2-q)log G_q].  (38)

For every x>=0, log(1+x)<=sqrt(x): both sides vanish at0, and forx>0
the derivative of their difference is
(sqrt(x)-1)^2/[2sqrt(x)(1+x)]>=0. Put r_geo=sqrt(5/8)<4/5. Hence
the entire infinite positive sum in(38) obeys the exact bound

    sum_(q=3)^infinity 2^(2-q)log G_q
       <=4sqrt(t0) sum_(q=3)^infinity r_geo^q
       =4sqrt(t0) r_geo^3/(1-r_geo)
       <(4/64)(4/5)^3/(1/5)=4/25<1/5.

Also
log(5/4)>1/5, by integrating1/x over[1,5/4]. Therefore the bracket
in(38) is less than log(3/5)+log(5/4)=log(3/4), proving the first
bound in(37).

For the additive coefficient put T_s=G_(s+1)P_(s-1), s>=2, and
r_prod=3/4. Since2^(j-1)>=j forj>=1,

    sum_(j>=1)P_j<=sum_(j>=1)r_prod^j=3.                   (39)

Also

    sum_(s>=2)T_s
       <=3+t0 sum_(s>=2)(5/2)^(s+1)r_prod^(2^(s-2))<4.     (40)

Here is an explicit bound for the last series. Its four terms s=2,3,4,5
are each<256. Write v_s=(5/2)^(s+1)r_prod^(2^(s-2)). For s>=6,

    v_(s+1)/v_s=(5/2)r_prod^(2^(s-2))<1/2,

because r_prod^8<1/9 and hence r_prod^16<1/81. Since v_6<(5/2)^7<1024,
the tail froms=6 is<2048. The whole series is<3072, whose product
witht0 is3/4<1. This proves(40).

Unrolling the additive recurrence in(36) now gives

    E_s=sum_(j=2)^s P_(j-1) product_(t=j+1)^s(1+T_t)
       <=[sum_(j>=1)P_j] exp(sum_(t>=2)T_t)
       <3 exp(4)<243<256.                                 (41)

For completeness exp(1)<3 follows by comparing the exponential series
with a geometric series using j!>=2^(j-1) forj>=1, with strictness for
j>=3.
The scalar estimate controls ALL accumulated forcing; a negative mean
log-gain alone would not have proved(41).

## 7. Original queried indices and the actual free terminal

Let K=N/4 and s=m-2, so K=2^s. The queried odd-b amplitudes are

    B_r=Gamma_(4r+3), 0<=r<=K-2.

For0<=r<=K-3 the return taking B_r to B_(r+1) has original level
2+nu_2(r+1). Thus(31),(35) are majorized by exactly the scalar maps of
section6, with d=d_amp. The first three physical steps l,k,l are short,
so B_0<=R.

The queried returns constitute the first K-2 entries of W_s. Its only
missing final entry is2. Construct a scalar upper sequence for these
K-2 returns, then append the scalar map y->theta y merely to calculate
its bound. This appends NO gradient constraint, queried sample, or
physical step. Since this last scalar map has no additive term, (36)
implies the bound on the pre-final scalar sequence

    Gamma_(n-4)=B_(K-2)<=[P_s R+E_s d_amp]/theta.            (42)

The last FOUR actual physical steps are

    1+delta*c_chr, l, k, l.

The first two have queried endpoints n-4 and n-2, so their compound
gain is<6/5 by(34). The last two short steps obey(11) for the actual
function, with unrestricted terminal gradient. Therefore

    ||grad F(X_n)||<=(6/5)Gamma_(n-4)
       <=2[P_s R+E_s d_amp]
       <=2(3/4)^(N/8)R+512d_amp.                           (43)

Since512*2^17=2^26, this is(6). Convexity atX_n and its TRUE closest
minimizer, with(5), gives

    F(X_n)-F_star<=||grad F(X_n)|| ||X_n-X_star||
                         <512R||grad F(X_n)||.

Multiplying by2/R^2 in(43) yields exactly(7), since
2*512*512*2^17=2^36. Applying the accepted bound
B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3) proves(9): log_2 3<2, so its second term tends to
zero, and the first decays geometrically inn.

For an L-smooth objective f use F=(f-f_star)/L. The normalized payoff
is then exactly2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(LR^2), retaining W=2R in the project's
rate notation. The symbolR in this document denotes a distance, not
the unnormalized worst-case-rate functional.

## 8. Maximal prefix interpolation and scope of the remaining problem

Every character-specific inequality used above has queried endpoints:
the movement optima t+1,t+2 are at mostn-1; return pairs end atj+4<n;
and the final compound ends atn-2. After that compound we apply(11) to
an actual global function. We do not impose an incoming inequality at
a formal unqueried terminal jet.

Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` constructs the pointwise maximal1-smooth
convex interpolant from any legal complete queried prefix and optimum
sample. It has that optimum as a minimizer and realizes the prescribed
GD prefix. Hence it satisfies this proof, with its own true closest
minimizer and radiusR. Every reduced-feasible terminal value is at
most its value atX_n. If the supplied optimum is within unit distance
ofX_0, its actual closest radiusR is at most1, so(7) also bounds twice
every such reduced terminal value. No inverse-Gram norm replacesR.

The full finite common-prefix measure has not been replaced by a
limiting two-ray kernel: epsilon and eta remain exactly2^-m and2^-2m.
The mixture perturbation is charged only after(26), and its accumulated
propagation is explicitly bounded by256 in(41). The factor
m=1/(2-k) in(31) remains visible through(7). No angular inverse margin,
power of1/(1-beta), or other divergent condition number is omitted.

The prior four-ray coefficient vanishes at beta_R; that boundary does
not prevent this complete word estimate because the exact physical
level2 returns supply contraction. The endpoint beta=beta_S in(3) is
an explicit supercritical law, with alpha_ret=-2^-12, for
which EVERY legal positive amplitude history and every anchor/value
choice has vanishing exact-B_n payoff. This is a scoped obstruction,
not a competitive adversarial construction or a solution of the main
all-schedule question.

The unresolved angular region for this investigation has consequently
moved to beta>beta_S. Merely obtaining a locally expanding return there
does not suffice. A candidate must satisfy all original remote and
optimum pairs while producing a terminal payoff after the intervening
level2 contractions and the true-distance cost. This proof identifies
two quantitative interfaces rather than equating a vanishing denominator
with feasibility: the terminal mass coefficient c_a-5max(0,-c_b) in(23),
and the ruler product plus accumulated forcing in(38)--(41).
Their sufficient estimates may be sharpened, but no converse is claimed.

No fact_submit was allocated. This single joint manuscript and its
published evidence are unverified candidates; they do not add a fact
to the graph and do not finalize the original target.

# Independent analytical audit of the complete actual two-family candidate

Worker: max4. One allocated audit round, after completed61, completed
ceiling62 / attempted ceiling63. Status: ANALYTICAL AUDIT ONLY; no
verifier verdict, accepted fact, or original-target completion.

## 1. Verdict, frozen source, and exact scope

**The full stated L=1 candidate survived this independent analytical
attack.** I found no substantive mathematical error or missing estimate
in its claimed two-family theorem. In particular, the new Section9A
discrete turning estimate and its deletion comparison survive changes
in adjacent levels and incoming-child sizes. PASS below means that this
analytical attack found no defect; it does not mean verifier acceptance.

There is one notation clarification outside the fixed-L=1 calculation:
the general-L scaling sentence must use the normalized payoff with L in
the denominator. This is separated from the theorem audit in
`notation_recommendation.md`. It does not alter a function, selection,
parameter domain, or constant in the assigned theorem. There is no
substantive repair recommendation and no iterative repair round.

Frozen source, read in its entirety (all 1223 lines):

    shared finding bfff4aa387a9d818, author max2
    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/bfff4aa387a9d818.md
    53449 bytes
    SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2

No source bytes were changed. Component drafts are provenance, not
accepted premises. The sole mathematical predecessor used by the frozen
candidate is accepted fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark
and its certified saved interval table. I read its relevant statement,
ray proof, table-certificate scope, and the referenced saved row.

The physical word is defined, with one enclosing pair (m,tau), by

    m>=1536, 0<tau<=1/4,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.

Every child keeps these same enclosing parameters. A chronological
selection writes the original word as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),
    s_i=sum block_i.

There is ONE common pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2 for the whole selection.
It may depend arbitrarily on m; different stages do not get separate
pairs. Ordinary blocks retain all physical entries. The candidate's
general geometric construction also permits zeros and arbitrarily large
ordinary steps.

At advertised smoothness L=1, the payoff is

    E=2(f(x_(n_m))-min f)/dist(e_0,argmin f)^2,
    H_base=1/(1+2*S_m).

The audit covers every eligible ordered selection of every count. The
claimed bounds are MAIN nonempty E<(441/500)H_base and SOURCE nonempty
E<(9801/10000)H_base. The main and combined optima include their empty
selection and equal H_base; the source-only optimum is
1/[2(1+S_m)]. The original-B deficits and the fixed-tau limit are checked
below. Stage-dependent pairs, retained-circle components, unrestricted
W_n, and the original minimax question are outside this candidate.

No substantive interface is marked FAIL or GAP. The following details
record the attempted attacks and why they did not expose a failure.

## 3. Geometry, chronology, and true radius

Put theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon) and

    C=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C*exp(-kappa*theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)).

The supporting normal is n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa*t(theta). For
Delta=phi-theta, the derivative of
exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta) is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta. It changes sign in the correct
direction at0 for the entire permitted angle interval. This validates
support against both earlier and later arc points, not merely adjacent
sampled slopes. Support against0 is immediate from its smaller value.

For one local chronological ordinary list, the backward endpoint is
(epsilon,-1). The source's cap test is

    y<=kappa*(u+(alpha-1)*C).

If it holds, mu=u+(alpha-1)C>=y/kappa>0 and

    r_old-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-y).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. The resulting state satisfies
y_old<=kappa*(u_old-C), which proves persistence of every preceding
cap even for alpha=0. If the cap test fails, the root in(6) exists
uniquely: its left side increases strictly with theta, equals1 at the
current projected angle, and is below1-alpha at0. The root is allowed
to equal the current angle for a zero step. Its normal coefficient is
nonnegative because the state's polar angle is no smaller than the
root angle.

The uncapped normal representation is

    r=R(theta)*[(1+nu)e(theta)+kappa*nu*t(theta)], nu>=0.

The exact relation(8) proves the two stated bounds for nu. For
kappa<1 its exponential comparison telescopes; for kappa>=1 the
coefficient A_D is at most1 and the B_D contributions sum to at most
theta_sp/kappa. Thus the proof does not extrapolate the singular-looking
first branch through kappa=1. Its limit at1 is theta_sp, agreeing with
the second branch there.

For every local output P,Q, the pointwise inequalities p>=epsilon*q,
p<=C, p+q>=1 imply

    P>=epsilon*Q, 0<=P<=C*s_loc,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2 >= (1+epsilon+s_loc)^2/2.

The derivative of q locates its maximum at the endpoint when
kappa<=epsilon and at arctan(1/kappa) otherwise, giving the stated
qmax including the equality case. A negative horizontal normal can
occur only when p<kappa*q. Since u>=epsilon, it gives
u-p>=epsilon-kappa*qmax>=-N_sp. Otherwise u>=p. This checks the
correction used by the main threshold for all kappa, including kappa>1.

Both endpoint planes are present in every finite local maximum. Every
other slope satisfies

    (p,-q)=((p-epsilon*q)/C)*(C,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

with nonnegative coefficients. This proves the complete zero wedge,
not just a convenient subset of minimizers. Conditions(11) make the
pre-activation quadrant and the entire post-checkpoint vertical ray
part of this wedge. The proximal subgradient test uses all sampled
planes through the supporting-normal inequality just checked.

For the SOURCE sum of local envelopes, the coefficient1/2 is essential:
the actual h_t update implements alpha=h_t/2. Its next amplitude is
D'_i*sigma'_i*b_i/2. Its shifts use Q_i, and its final hinge has the same
coefficient1/2. The derived exact terminal numerator is
(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)]. All preceding components are at minima and all
future components are at minima along the stated physical trajectory.
Each coordinate belongs to at most two half-weighted 1-smooth terms,
including the terminal hinge. This proves global 1-smoothness. The
resulting global interpolation inequality applies to every ordered
pair, the terminal sample, and every minimizer; physical vectors also
give a positive semidefinite Gram matrix without an added assumption.

The SOURCE minimum set is exactly(19). Let
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...) and w=e_0-z_bar. Its first-row normal
representation is

    w=sigma'_0*(P_0-epsilon*Q_0)*e_0
       +sigma'_0*(1+Q_0)*(epsilon*e_0-e_1).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Every point x in the full minimum
set satisfies <w,x-z_bar><=0 even if z_bar violates later constraints.
Hence

    ||e_0-x||^2
      =||w||^2+||x-z_bar||^2-2<w,x-z_bar> >=||w||^2.

This is exactly the direction needed for an upper payoff bound. It
does not reuse the larger, generally invalid one-source-checkpoint
radius. With the half-step mass, ||w||^2 is at least
(sigma'_0)^2*(s_0+2)^2/8. Cancellation with the exact first transfer
gives precisely(23), including its factor1 rather than an extra2 or1/2.

The exact all-row source identity(20) is also consistent: each
nonnegative independent-row multiplier gives a dual distance lower
bound, and the true projection has a normal generated by active rows.
An independent active subcollection attains the distance. The argument
does not require every maximizing candidate center to be feasible.

For the MAIN envelope, ordinary steps use their full physical values.
The proximal first coordinate is at least U_i>=0 because
epsilon*(b_i-1)>=N_sp. Its proximal second coordinate is between0 and
sigma_i*(1+Q_i). These inequalities respectively preserve the previous
zero wedge and the next pre-activation wedge. Farther terms vanish as
well. The current local slope is therefore an active subgradient of
the maximum defining J. The checkpoint leaves next amplitude
D_i*sigma_i*(b_i-1) and shift D_i*sigma_i*(1+Q_i), as stated.

Here the first center z_bar IS feasible: its second coordinate is
ell_1, so the next local input is(0,0); every subsequent input is in its
pre-activation wedge. The same nonnegative normal proves exact closest
distance, giving(43). This difference between the two realizations is
preserved throughout the proof. The terminal gradients and values follow
from D_k-(u+1)*delta>=0 for every tail prefix mass u<=s_k.

## 4. Actual upper functionals and the high-C/source comparisons

Use r_h=c(c-2), v_h=beta/r_h, d_j=a*c^j, and u_j=d_j/M_j. Direct
substitution in the mass recurrence gives

    M_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,
    0<v_h<1, 1<=M_j<=c^j, a<=u_j<=c-2<21/50.

Every globally minimum selected level j has both full H_j children
unselected. Its adjacent ordinary sums satisfy s,t>=M_j-1, including
first/last selections and ties. Removing the checkpoint reclassifies
its full physical value b=1+d_j as ordinary. No trajectory is preserved
by this operation; only an already proved upper functional is compared.

For SOURCE, the remaining denominators in(23) are exact transfer
denominators C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i. The first deletion reduces to(28).
At s=t=M-1 it has the difference(29) when C>=r_h, with first coefficient
C-u(2+u)>=0 and the other terms positive. The interior difference is
(32); q=2A-C>=3C makes its remaining coefficients positive. If C<r_h,
Section8 proves A>2*C*M_max, and(30),(33) prove these same deletions.
The last deletion has ratio at most5/8 by(34)-(35), with no dichotomy
needed. All monotonicity reductions use positive derivative signs over
the original domain s,t>=M-1, rather than at only one selected word.

The SOURCE dichotomy is valid on the full parameter domain. If C<r_h,
then epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16 and kappa<1/768. In particular kappa<1
has been proved before using its exponential T branch. The exponent
X_sp=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa exceeds m, and

    1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(X_sp)-kappa)/(1-kappa)>=exp(X_sp).

This yields A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max. It does not imply any corresponding
lower bound by A for an actual MAIN transfer denominator.

For MAIN, differentiating p+q/epsilon shows either monotone decrease
or a single interior maximum. Its minimum is therefore the smaller
endpoint C_floor=min(C,A_floor), where A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1).
Consequently the ACTUAL denominator is at least C_floor*s_i+A_floor.
When C>=r_h, C_floor>=r_h and A_floor>=C_floor. The three deletion
calculations in Section9 use those actual lower bounds with the
exceptional first radius bounded by(s_0+1)^2/2. Their first/interior
differences have nonnegative leading coefficient
C_floor-u(2+u), and the last ratio is at mostu^2*(3+2u)<=1.

After either source or main deletion reaches one checkpoint, X=1+s
and Y=1+t are independently at least M. The relevant ratio decreases
in each. SOURCE eligibility gives d>=9, hence

    (d+1)/(M+1)<=10*u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20,

and its ratio is less than 2*(121/50)*(9/20)^2=9801/10000.
MAIN eligibility gives d>=4 and M>200/21>19/2. Its ratio is at most

    2*u^2*[2+u+(1+u)/(2*M-1)] < 2*u^2*(5/2)<441/500.

All strict comparisons hold at the eligibility thresholds themselves.

## 5. Attack of Section9A: discrete angles and caps

This is the central new interface. Assume C<r_h and write
eta_turn=999/1000 and Delta_C=r_h-C. The proven bounds include

    epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16, kappa<1/768,
    C<769/768, 0<Delta_C<2/m<1/4,
    kappa*m*theta_sp<44/25, log c<9/10.

Run the actual backward process on one complete H_j, j<=m. Immediately
before a step alpha, let y_mass=1 plus the actual mass strictly after
it within that child. A step of level ell has its full right H_ell
after it, so

    alpha/y_mass=(1+d_ell)/y_mass
        <=c-2+1/y_mass<3/2.

For x=alpha/y_mass, log(1+x)>=2*x/(2+x). Thus

    x<=(c/2+1/(2*y_mass))*log(1+x).

Moreover

    log(1+x)/y_mass <= x/y_mass
       =(1+x)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha))
       <=(5/2)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)).

Both expressions telescope in the exact reverse order. This proves
Hharm_j=sum alpha/y_mass <= (c/2)*log M_j+5/4 without a flow or an
order-free model. The empty child is included.

Let the pre-update state have radius rho_loc and polar angle phi_pol,
and let theta be the slope angle actually added. Set gamma=phi_pol-theta.
It lies in[0,pi/2) in both branches. The uncapped root equation gives

    sin gamma=kappa*cos gamma
                 +kappa*(alpha-1)*R(theta)/rho_loc.

The cap test gives exactly this inequality with <= instead of equality
and R(theta)=C. Thus there is no omitted sign or branch in(46e).
The crude radius bound rho_loc>=y_mass/sqrt(2), already supplied by
p+q>=1, gives sin gamma<(11/2)*kappa<1/100. Therefore
cos gamma>eta_turn. Projection of the exact vector addition onto the
old radial direction then proves

    rho_new>=rho_loc+eta_turn*alpha,
    rho_loc>=eta_turn*y_mass

by induction starting from the endpoint radius sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1.
This induction is cap-inclusive.

For z=alpha*R(theta)/rho_loc, the exact polar-angle decrement delta is

    tan delta=z*sin gamma/(1+z*cos gamma).

The branch-independent inequality above gives sin gamma<=kappa*(1+z).
Since 1+z*cos gamma>=cos gamma*(1+z),

    delta<=kappa*z/cos gamma
      <=(C/eta_turn^2)*kappa*alpha/y_mass
      <=(101/100)*kappa*alpha/y_mass.

The post-update state has theta>=phi_new-kappa: for an uncapped state
this follows from arctan(kappa*nu/(1+nu))<=kappa; at a cap it follows
from y_new<=kappa*(u_new-C). Thus the inequality remains usable even
at a putative first cap. Summing the actual decrements gives

    theta>=theta_sp-kappa*((101/100)*Hharm_j+1)
      >theta_sp-1221/(625*theta_sp)>1/16.

The quantitative margins check: the bracket divided by m is less
than111/100 using j<=m, log c<9/10, c<121/50 and m>=1536; and
(23/16)*(22/16)=253/128>1221/625. Only now are caps ruled out on a
complete H_j. They are not ruled out on arbitrary preceding blocks.

Every slope processed on H_j therefore has q>1/32 and p+q>=1. Since
epsilon^(-1)-1>15,

    p+q/epsilon>47/32,
    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C*S_j>=S_j/4.

This is a lower estimate on actual discrete outputs. At j=0 the
statement is weak equality, so no positive surplus is invented for
an empty child.

## 6. Attack of Section9A: arbitrary blocks and changing adjacent levels

Let lev_i be the level of selected checkpoint i. For i>=1 set
j_i=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). At position t, the tree level is the exponent
of2 in t+1. Two positions of level at least j_i differ by at least
2^(j_i), and the final 2^(j_i)-1 positions before the later checkpoint
are exactly H_(j_i). The previous selected checkpoint is no later than
the position immediately before that child. Thus the trailing child
actually lies inside block_i, with unchanged enclosing parameters.

The backward construction processes this child first from the same
endpoint(epsilon,-1). Its contribution is exactly the one just bounded.
Earlier steps may have different angles or may cap. For every one of
them p+q/epsilon>=C, because C<A_floor. Hence

    P_i+Q_i/epsilon-C*s_i>=S_(j_i)/4.

The constant reserve A_floor-C>1/4 and Delta_C<1/4 now imply

    M_i^tr>=C*(s_i+1)+Delta_C*M_(j_i)=:D_i^cmp.

This retains the actual max in M_i^tr; it only lowers the denominator.
It does not replace M_i^tr by the much larger fixed A_sp. Inserting
this inequality into the exact payoff(43) gives Psi_low(46k).

For deletion, choose a globally minimum selected level j, and set
M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M, X=1+s>=M, Y=1+t>=M. The merged block mass plus
one is X+Y+d.

For a FIRST removal, the next comparison denominator is C*Y+Delta_C*M.
The required inequality is

    d*(X+Y+d)<=X*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

Its difference increases in X and Y since C>u. At X=Y=M it is
M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0.

For an INTERIOR removal, both old comparison denominators use M_j.
The newly adjacent selected levels can be larger. Their new complete
trailing child is contained in the merged block, so its mass plus one
is at most X+Y+d. Consequently the NEW denominator is at most
r_h*(X+Y+d). It is therefore sufficient to compare

    d*r_h*(X+Y+d)
       <=(C*X+Delta_C*M)*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

The X derivative of the difference is at least
r_h*M*(C-u)>0, and similarly for Y. At X=Y=M the difference is
r_h*M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0. This explicitly pays for every increase of
the new adjacent level. It never preserves the old P,Q or old M_j
for the new block.

For a LAST removal, the local selected-to-deleted ratio is at most

    d^2*(1+2*(s+b+t))/((s+1)^2*(1+2*t)).

It decreases in s,t. At their lower bounds it is
u^2*((4+2*u)*M-1)/(2*M-1), bounded by u^2*(3+2*u)<=1. The first
cross-multiplied slack is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. The last comparison uses
u<=1/2. Ties in the minimum level do not affect any of these arguments.

Every remaining subset has its own recalculated comparison functional.
The deletions terminate at one checkpoint, where the Section9 bound
uses only d>=4, u<21/50 and X,Y>=M. It does not require C>=r_h there.
Thus the low-C proof closes the entire missing band, with the same
441/500 constant. There is no finite-count restriction hidden in it.

## 7. Boundary attacks, benchmark evidence, and final optima

The source's two-checkpoint test epsilon=kappa=1/2, b_0=b_1=10 and zero
ordinary mass correctly has squared true radius5*sigma^2/4 and actual
payoff250*sigma^2. Formula(23) gives625*sigma^2 in the required upper
direction. It does not use the invalid50*sigma^2 estimate from extending
the one-stage radius. Its full polyhedron caps x_2, not x_1.

The reordered local lists(A_*,1) and(1,A_*) in Section10 have the same
mass but respectively Q=q_*>0 and Q=0. Their written cap tests and
actual projections agree. Nothing in the deletion proofs equates these
outputs. A huge immediately capped step likewise has P=C*alpha,Q=0;
all inequalities used outside the complete-child surplus still hold.
These attacks distinguish the permitted arbitrary-prefix caps from the
impossible caps on a whole H_j in the small-C band.

For the benchmark I used fact04219554e41d9750, not a new recurrence
calculation. Its Section8 certifies every row of the table

    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv
    SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.

The table hash matched. Its header identifies the upper-B column and
row1024 contains upper numerator19229705732238461978760749948 on
D80=1208925819614629174706176. The accepted proof explicitly states
the all-split induction and independent certification of every stored
interval, so using this row does not require reopening its computation.

The ray enclosure is for the original B recurrence and normalization
W=2R. At b=1,j=10 it gives Z_1<237/100 exactly as in(49): the saved
row is below15910*D80, lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)>6725,
sqrt(15910)<127, and15918/6725<237/100. No different benchmark,
balanced-only dominant recurrence, or approximate phase profile is used.

The finite physical mass formula is

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      =2*v_h*(1+tau/m)^m+2*(1-v_h)*(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m).

The stated bounds v_h>255/256, lambda^(-m)<1/256 and
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4 give a value greater than5/2 at tau=1/4,
and greater than12/5 throughout[1/5,1/4]. Combined with Z_1<237/100
and B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m<=Z_1, they give respectively237/250 and79/80.
All arithmetic here is a written symbolic comparison of the displayed
constants; no arithmetic program or interval-table replay was executed.

For k=0, the MAIN hinge gives exactly H_base. The SOURCE half-weighted
hinge gives exactly1/[2(1+S_m)], whose ratio to H_base is
1-1/(2*M_m). Since M_m>=2^m>=32, this ratio is at least63/64>9801/10000.
Thus every nonempty source selection lies below its own k=0 member,
not merely below Huber. Every nonempty main selection lies below
Huber by the uniform441/500 factor. These uniform pointwise bounds
establish the stated suprema even when the parameter pair and selection
depend on the horizon. The source and main factors are never mixed.

For each fixed tau, v_h tends1 and the displayed physical mass divided
by lambda^m tends2*exp(tau). The accepted ray tendsZ_1. Exact family
identities have already been established at every m, so their scaled
limits are Z_1/(2*exp(tau)); for the source-only value its ratio to
Huber tends1. No exchange of a parameter supremum with a limit occurs.

## 8. Retrieval, independence, and resource closeout

Skills applied: query-memory and construct-counterexamples. I read
TASK.md, the relevant project problem, own continuation records,
master_guidance964d529482bf05be, and later guidance235f55c47f06b19a,
which explicitly preserves this audit assignment. Shared findings about
earlier radius and ordering failures were awareness only. I reconstructed
the checked interfaces from the frozen joint proof instead of adopting
my earlier authorship of the spiral construction as validation.

One Matlas query about logarithmic-spiral/Moreau-envelope gradient-descent
constructions returned unrelated definable-function and constant-step
results. No external theorem was adopted. A web background search and
the primary [Ye--Liu arXiv v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2) were
consulted for terminology and the performance normalization. They are
not mathematical premises of this audit. The complete target supplies
its geometric arguments internally; the benchmark has the accepted
predecessor identified above.

Some initial broad tool outputs were truncated. All load-bearing target
sections and accepted benchmark passages were recovered by bounded
reads; the source hash, size, and table identity were checked directly.
Empty exact-identifier searches for prior dead ends/obstacles do not
constitute evidence for correctness; targeted recall supplied the
relevant earlier shortcut failures.

Resources used: mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0;
arithmetic replays0; solvers0; executed recurrences0; formal verifier or
fact_submit calls0; installations0; consult calls0; subagents0. Text
reads/writes, JSON bookkeeping, and file hashing are administrative.
No truth store was hand-edited. New artifacts are confined to the
absolute public audit directory; existing own local logs were appended.

This finishes the one assigned analytical attack. No new audit round,
formal gate, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or original
Goal completion follows from it. The outer loop owns runtime counters;
this report is not evidence of a post-return process exit. Original
minimax remains OPEN and bfff4aa387a9d818 remains UNVERIFIED.

Glossary: all family/word symbols are defined in Sections1,3-7 above.
P,Q are actual local backward outputs; source outputs use half physical
steps and main outputs use full physical steps. M_i^tr is the actual
main transfer denominator, while D_i^cmp is solely its proved lower
