# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation

Status: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, first continuation round.
No kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.
The uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal
remain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;
it does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.

## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated

Fix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
 d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).

The extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual
queried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,
s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.

On the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and

 B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),

with a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:
the first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have
already died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and
B_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.

For arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where
v_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by

 sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
 =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)

Each coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by
the step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l
and l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.
The coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry
appears twice in the quadratic form.

## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word

First, 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also

                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)

Indeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity
before subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and

 c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2
        >1+6epsilon.

Consequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.
The expression increases with k, proving (2).

Second, every original physical step satisfies

                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)

Let S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.
The recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and
d_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity

 C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.

The prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later
occurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves
(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.

## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term

For a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth
recurrence is

 v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).

For k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly

 C_kl*v_k*v_l
 +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l
 +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)
 +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)

where

 C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)

To check (4), the first term of (1) contributes
s'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes
s'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes
h(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).

The last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.
If v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.
Otherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives
d_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence

 h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.

It follows from (3) that

 C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)

Thus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl
when 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step
until they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.

For k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,
whose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give

 E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,
 E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.

These calculations and (4)-(6) prove

                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)

In particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and

                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)

For strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)
are positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on
each of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities
are positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller
than d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this
is possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller
than d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is
strictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).

No uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness
argument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been
estimated uniformly.

## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins

For all nonnegative indices define

 A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),
 b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),
 u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i
                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,

with negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the
finite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant
load obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.

Equation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal
sum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is

                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)

In particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating
signs of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation
between the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.
Nonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.

This does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite
decomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square
e_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).
The last diagonal remains

 u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).

Neither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed
constant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.

## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained

For this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but
every state-specific sum is finite. Set

 mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,
 L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,
 M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,
 N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,

and put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is
B_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves
sum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)
and summing by parts gives

 sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.

Starting with a zero predecessor moment shows
sum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean
exit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.

Polarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives

 ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)

For example, the first term in (1) contributes
s'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,
and its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).
Substitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation
identities just proved. The result is

             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)

In detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is
2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is
sum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k
=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.

Formula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load
inequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must
be estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed
quantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.

## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order

Put t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and

 Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),
 A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.

These are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.
They are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.
The affine-geometric node relation gives

 (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),
 Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)

Write the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact
pair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent
of2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the
remaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler
word, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write
P_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then

 P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,
 s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)

All four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual
pairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of
the full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth
steps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.

The Newton factors also transform exactly:

 phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)

where phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only
as a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.
At every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect
-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact

 gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.

Equations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed
moments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in
which those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded
block's parameters by epsilon=1/j.

## 7. Scope and remaining work

The new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for
every required index. The stronger entrywise result (7) and the
identities (9), (11), and (13) have explicit proofs above.

The unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15, u_i>=0, the quantitative
e_i>=1/15 and u_i>=1/16 at the required terminal indices, and the
two-sided constant-load bounds. This document does not prove the full
kernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those
remaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the
specified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.

The existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first
few steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity
alone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.
Thus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order
estimates. The exact pair identity (13), unlike that coarse envelope,
does distinguish the original word.

# Full physical continuation after a legal small-ratio entry

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max4; invocation70.
Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. No new fact is asserted.
ONE invocation after actual ended baseline69, ended ceiling70,
attempted ceiling71. Consult OFF. No mathematical program, numerical or
arithmetic replay, trajectory/recurrence execution, optimizer, DP, formal
gate, installation or subagent was used. Old allocations stay closed.

Three explicit static policies are screened on the SAME full hierarchy.
The first two have complete actual trajectories, terminal objectives,
minimum sets, sharp global smoothness and TRUE radius. The third has an
exact obstruction to its proposed invariant-line continuation at the
FIRST new query, and an explicit actual gradient showing the exit. No
terminal claim about its unscreened off-line continuation is made.

The decisive full-suffix diagnostic is policy2: a maximal completion
fixed BEFORE GD preserves every entry jet and realizes every remaining
original step. Its exact positive terminal payoff is asymptotically
negligible relative to the ORIGINAL benchmark. This is a limitation of
this specific continuation, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

## 1. Recall, accepted inputs, and the original benchmark

Read in full: prior report20618061fa719178, its fixed-plane failure
62e302b04457911c, and the small-ratio example3f56a6deb1e5bd53.
The relevant earlier audit0ebf0c159cc211e6 was recalled as awareness.
The dispatch guidance is90afbd2617b3aa0a. Later guidance
c8160224c76ffea9 was read in full and leaves this allocation unchanged.
TASK.md was read at entry and again before closeout. The earlier Law1 active-weight cap
is not omitted. Its specialized entry is re-proved below; no unverified
Law1 or Law2 is treated as an accepted building block.

Accepted correctness inputs:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, statement and constructive maximal-prefix
  proof. For all original prefix jets and the zero optimum, define
  y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The conjugate of
  H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,v>-delta_i) is a fixed convex C1 globally
  1-smooth interpolant preserving every such jet, and is the pointwise
  maximal completion of THAT prefix. No prior anchor may be deleted.
  The original signed ordered residual is
  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.
* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d, full statement/proof. For every full physical
  word h of length n, K_n(h) is the maximum of the global Huber
  1/(1+2 sum h) and EVERY single-cut term
  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)], with P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t and
  T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t. These are attained lower witnesses. The ORIGINAL
  all-split benchmark below has B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3).
* fact_iddfde2fa326c908bf, full relevant polynomial statement and
  constructive interpolation proof. The full polynomial is
  Poly_t(z)=product_(0<=i<t)(1-h_i z). In particular the real scalar
  quadratic with curvature1 has full-word payoff |Poly_n(1)|^2.
  No universal-complex-lens restriction is substituted for the full
  smooth-convex class in the argument below.

For x,y>=0 define

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

The exact original recurrences are C_0=B_0=1 and

    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every original integer split is retained. W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite dimensions, all
convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient and a chosen
minimizer x_star, using x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The examples
here have the TRUE closest initial radius1, and hence their normalized
values are actual lower witnesses for W_n(h). They give no W_n upper.

All norms/inner products are Euclidean. Local symbols have the suffix
_fc in the attached glossary; unsuffixed forms below are local aliases.
In particular R below is ||r||^2, distinct from the original objective
R_n=W_n/2; its shared name is Rnorm_fc, and local Q is Qnorm_fc.
H_m is H_fc,m, local Y,Z,W of the return diagnostic are Y_ret_fc,
Z_ret_fc,W_ret_fc, and every other introduced symbol is explicitly
defined in its section and glossary.json. All max-affine weights are1.
Every listed offset is explicit.

## 2. The unchanged FULL word and a paid four-query entry

Fix ANY integer m>=7, tau=1/4, and set

    u=1/(4m), lambda=1+sqrt2,
    beta=1-u, c=lambda*(1+u), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    b_l=1+a*c^l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m and tau. No reset, padding,
truncation or permutation occurs. The first four original steps are
exactly (b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2); all n-4 remaining entries are kept.

Write S_l=sum H_l and v_h=a/(c-2). Direct induction on
1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a*c^l gives

    1+S_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l.

Here c-2>1/lambda>a, so 0<v_h<1, and 2<c<3.
Also beta>=27/28, a<1/2. Every first-occurrence bridge ratio
a*c^l/(1+S_l) is smaller than c-2<1. These are the original masses.

Define the literal entry data in R^3, with its standard basis e1,e2,e3:

    M=2+a, s=M-1=1+a, D=beta, E=beta*c,
    Delta=E-M,
    d=a/[2(1+beta)], p=e1,
    q=-d*e1+sqrt(a*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=a*d,
    k=beta/(2M), r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3, R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q,
    y=-a*e1-q,
    z=y-beta*q-M*r=y-(beta/2)*(q+sqrt(Q)*e3),
    tR=z+s*r.

All quantities are fixed from the known full word before GD. The entry
has M0=M1=1, first ratio a<1/2 and second bridge excess beta. Its third
ordinary block is the actual single step b_0, whose mass is M-1. Thus
this is the earlier Law1's j=0 entry, but its proof is fully repeated.

The apparent boundary cancellation is paid exactly:

    Delta=2u/[lambda(1+u)]-lambda*u^2>0,
    0<Delta<1/14,       0<k<1/4.

Indeed positivity is equivalent to lambda^2*u*(1+u)<2, which follows
from lambda^2<6 and u<=1/28. The upper bound follows by dropping the
negative term and using lambda>1 and u<=1/28. In particular E>M.

The identities needed for EVERY anchor are

    y.q=y.r=z.r=0,      z.q=-beta*Q/2,
    q.r=-kQ,           tR.r=sR,
    y_1=-a+d<0,
    z_1=-a+(1+beta/2)d<0,
    (tR)_1=-a+(1+beta-k)d<-a/2.

Set Dict=conv{0,p,q,r} and

    J(w)=max(0,p.w,q.w,r.w),
    F0(x)=max_(g in Dict){x.g-||g||^2/2}.

The four ORIGINAL queried jets from x_0=e1 are exactly

| query t | x_t | g_t | proximal point x_t-g_t | f_t |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | e1 | p | 0 | 1/2 |
| 1 | -a e1=y+q | q | y | Q/2 |
| 2 | y-beta q=tR+r | r | tR | sR+R/2 |
| 3 | z+r | r | z | R/2 |

At proximal0 every homogeneous plane has height0, and p is the unique
projection maximizer since x_0=p lies in Dict. At y, the q,r,zero
heights are0 and the p-height is negative. At tR the r-height is sR>0,
the q-height is (-beta+k)Q<0, and the p-height is negative by the last
display. At z the r and zero heights are0, the q-height is negative,
and the p-height is negative. These exhaustive guards select the stated
gradients; a support-plane tie does not make the envelope gradient
ambiguous. The updates are precisely b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2 and reach

    x_1=-a e1,
    x_2=y-beta q,
    x_3=x_2-(M-1)r=z+r,
    x_4=x_3-(1+E)r=z-E r.

For F0 the gradients are Euclidean projections onto Dict. If g is
this projection and w=x-g, its normal condition says
w.b<=w.g for EVERY b in Dict, not merely neighboring slopes. Then
F0(x)=J(w)+||g||^2/2. For any two actual samples, including optimum
samples and all future repetitions, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE
ordered identity

    Q_ij=<w_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                         (A)

The inequality is exactly the normal condition at j with b=g_i.
Thus every remote and optimum row is covered. Projection optimality at
two points gives <x-x',g-g'> >=||g-g'||^2; this proves global1-smoothness
and the attained maximum gives convexity and C1 differentiability.
Dict has interior because p,q,r are independent, and on that interior
the projection is the identity. Smoothness1 is sharp.

This entry proof uses no earlier unverified claim. In Law1 terminology
M1=1 so its old tQ endpoint duplicates y and its terminal correction
weight is0; there is no unrestricted use of the earlier positive-weight
formula or omission of its theta<=1 requirement.

## 3. Policy1: changing gradient on the OLD edge, then full absorption

Keep the single F0 above for EVERY query of the full H_m. Its offsets
are all0, its weights all1, and x_0=e1. No completion is made.

At the FIRST new query x_4=z-E r define

    theta_edge=k*Delta,       0<theta_edge<1/56.

The actual gradient is the varying edge point g_4=theta_edge*q in
conv{0,q}, not the old full q slope. This is proved by its actual

    q.w_4=0,
    p.w_4=-a+d<0,

The last sign uses Delta<E. Zero and q are tied at height0, with every

The NEXT original step is h_4=b_0=1+a. It gives

For the second sign, 1+a<2 and Delta<E. For the third, a*theta_edge<1

The COMPLETE minimum set is

The inequalities suffice directly in the maximum defining F0. If any
one is violated, a small positive multiple of its slope belongs to Dict
and gives a positive value, proving necessity. Since p=e1, every

Thus x_5 belongs to C0 and every original remaining update is exactly

    x_t=x_5, g_t=0, F0(x_t)=0,       5<=t<=n.

The original full word has not been shortened: ALL h_5,...,h_(n-1)
are executed with zero gradient. Its actual unqueried terminal is

    2F0(x_n)/dist(e1,C0)^2=0.

Identity(A) pays all directed rows including the varying edge query,

## 4. Policy2: maximal completion fixed BEFORE the full run

The original four queried jets of Section2, plus the zero optimum,
give precisely the affine anchor list

    (0,0), (y,0), (z,0), (tR,sR).

The initial query contributes another copy of(0,0); keep that physical
query although its identical affine term need only be printed once.
There is no omitted q-start anchor: its ordinary list is EMPTY, so the
first q anchor is exactly y. The FIRST r query contributes tR with
positive height sR and is retained. The following r query contributes z.

Freeze BEFORE GD the single function

    Hmax(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,y.v,z.v,tR.v-sR),
    Fmax(x)=sup_v{x.v-Hmax(v)}.                       (B)

The max weights are1 and its offsets are0,0,0,-sR. There is no
function change at time4. Accepted1252 proves that(B) is the maximal
completion of THESE FOUR jets and preserves all of them. Directly,
at g=p only the0 anchor is needed, at g=q the y anchor is active,
and at g=r all0,y,z,tR terms are0. The identities
x_0=p+0, x_1=q+y, x_2=r+tR, x_3=r+z therefore give their exact
gradients and the four exact values in the table.

This fixes Fmax before the run from an explicitly declared four-query
reference prefix. Fmax need not preserve the future jets that F0 would
have produced after time4; they are DIFFERENT policies. In the actual
Fmax run EVERY preterminal jet, including all later ones proved below,
is a jet of this ONE frozen function. No later completion is applied.

### 4.1 Global function, minimum, radius and sharp smoothness

Hmax is finite continuous1-strongly convex with quadratic growth. For
each x its conjugate maximizer g(x) exists uniquely and satisfies
x in partial Hmax(g(x)). Strong subgradient inequalities at x,x'
give <x-x',g(x)-g(x')> >=||g(x)-g(x')||^2. Maximizing inequalities
give differentiability with grad Fmax=g, so Fmax is convex C1 globally
1-smooth. This is also the construction proof of accepted1252.

At v=0 the active affine slopes are precisely0,y,z; the tR term has
strictly negative offset-sR. Consequently

    argmin Fmax=conv{0,y,z},         min Fmax=0.       (C)

To justify completeness, Fmax is differentiable, so x minimizes it
iff grad Fmax(x)=0 iff x belongs to partial Hmax(0). Hmax(0)=0 then
gives the minimum value0. Both nonzero vertices in(C) have negative
first coordinate; hence origin is the unique closest minimizer to e1
and the TRUE radius is exactly1. No smaller denominator is assumed.

For small v=t e1, t>0, the y,z terms are strictly negative and the
tR term remains strictly negative. A nonempty open neighborhood has
Hmax(v)=||v||^2/2. Its conjugate therefore has an open Hessian-I
region. The global smoothness bound1 is SHARP.

### 4.2 One exact region containing EVERY physical suffix query

For EVERY real rho<=1 the actual point/gradient/value are

    x=z+rho*r,      grad Fmax(x)=rho*r,
    Fmax(x)=rho^2 R/2.                               (D)

Indeed y.r=z.r=0 and tR.r=sR, so at v=rho*r the four affine values
are0,0,0,sR(rho-1). They are ALL checked, including the first r
anchor. The z term is active and x=v+z belongs to partial Hmax(v).
Uniqueness supplies the actual gradient and conjugate evaluation gives
(D). There is NO lower bound on rho: negative amplitudes are allowed.
The positive-amplitude restriction rho<=1 is crucial and is proved
for EVERY forthcoming original index; it is never silently removed.

At time3, x_3=z+r and rho_3=1. The actual step at index t has level

    ell(t)=nu_2(t+1),     h_t=1+a*c^ell(t),

where nu_2(j) is the largest nonnegative integer l such that 2^l divides
the positive integer j. This exact index formula follows by induction
on H_(l+1)=(H_l,b_l,H_l): its central index has t+1=2^l, and in the
right child nu_2(2^l+j)=nu_2(j) for 0<j<2^l.

Let s_2(t) count the ones in the binary expansion of the nonnegative
integer t. For EVERY original index 3<=t<=n, define

    rho_t=(-1)^(t-3)*beta^(t-3)*c^(2-s_2(t)).         (E)

This is a printed symbolic formula, not an executed trajectory or
recurrence. It proves the complete run by induction. At t=3 it is1.
The identity s_2(t+1)-s_2(t)=1-nu_2(t+1) gives

    rho_(t+1)/rho_t=-beta*c^(nu_2(t+1)-1)=1-h_t.

If t is even, rho_t<0. If t is odd and t>=3, its binary expansion has
at least two ones, so s_2(t)>=2 and
0<rho_t<=beta^(t-3)<=1, with strict inequality for t>3. Thus EVERY
query lies in(D). Each initialized update and each later physical step
is exactly x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*rho_t*r=z+rho_(t+1)r. This simultaneously
pays the large bridge steps, small steps, all suffix support guards and
the actual terminal, without child reset or endpoint-only evaluation.

Equivalently, the identity sum_(j=1)^t nu_2(j)=t-s_2(t) gives
Poly_t(1)=(-1)^t*beta^t*c^(-s_2(t)); this is the accepted full-word
polynomial of factdfde. The gradient formula(E) is its literal ratio
after the paid entry, with the actual first-r anchor still present.

There is also an exact ALL-preterminal closure, rather than merely a
four-query completion argument. EVERY suffix jet at an original query
3<=t<n has x_t-g_t=z and f_t-||g_t||^2/2=0. Thus its conjugate lower
quadratic is EXACTLY ||v||^2/2+z.v. Re-forming the maximal completion
from ALL n original preterminal jets and the optimum gives precisely
the SAME Hmax in(B): all later anchors are literal copies of(z,0),
while the FIRST r anchor(tR,sR) stays present. This proves that Fmax
is already the maximal completion of its ENTIRE preterminal transcript,
with every preterminal jet preserved. No endpoint-only envelope or
after-run change of function is involved.

### 4.3 Actual terminal and every original directed row

At the ACTUAL horizon n=2^m-1, s_2(n)=m and n is odd. Therefore

    rho_n=beta^(n-3)*c^(2-m)>0,
    x_n=z+rho_n*r,
    grad Fmax(x_n)=rho_n*r,
    Vmax=2Fmax(x_n)=R*beta^(2(n-3))*c^(4-2m).         (F)

The actual unqueried terminal is strictly positive and has been
evaluated on the same frozen Fmax. Its TRUE radius is1. All terminal
anchors and future queries have been paid, not appended as a reduced
endpoint model. In full polynomial form,

    Vmax=[R*c^4*beta^(-6)]*|Poly_n(1)|^2.

For completeness, every ordered interpolation row follows globally,
not from adjacent updates. For ANY convex differentiable1-smooth F
and samples x_i,x_j, put K(x)=F(x)-<grad F(x_i),x>. Convexity makes
x_i a global minimizer of K. The descent inequality at x_j, with
step -grad K(x_j), gives
K(x_i)<=K(x_j)-||grad K(x_j)||^2/2. Rearrangement yields Q_ij<=0
in the original orientation. The inequality holds for EVERY pair of
the displayed original queried jets, the ACTUAL terminal jet, and ANY
minimizer in(C), in both directions and with all repetitions retained.
Every Gram is a Gram of the literal vectors in R^3, hence PSD. No
equality between separate formal gradients is imposed in a certificate.

### 4.4 Exact benchmark comparison, with the complete original horizon

All parameter bounds above imply R<1. Since c>2 and m>=7,
c^(4-2m)<1. Also (1-u)^j<=exp(-uj) for nonnegative j. Thus accepted
b98's ORIGINAL benchmark bound gives

    0<B_n*Vmax
      <=(1+4n^p)*exp[-(n-3)/(2m)] ->0,              (G)
    n=2^m-1, p=log_2(3).

To see the limit without a numerical fit, its logarithm is at most
O(m)-(2^m-4)/(2m), which tends to minus infinity. In particular this
specific full continuation is BELOW the exact U_n^ZJ for every
sufficiently large enclosing m, with vanishing relative ratio.

It even loses to the same-word global Huber. From Section2,
1+S_m<c^m<3^m, hence

    Vmax/H_base(H_m)=Vmax*(1+2S_m)
      <2*3^m*exp[-(n-3)/(2m)] ->0,
    H_base(H_m)=1/(1+2S_m)<=K_n(H_m).

Every K cut is still the full original P_j,T_j cut of Section1. No
truncated-tail K, guessed benchmark constant, reversed profile ratio,
or invented recursion is used. This special-policy upper comparison
does not upper-bound W_n(H_m), which is a supremum over all functions.

## 5. Policy3: a preserving curvature mutation and its exact line exit

A different actual mechanism can lower curvature after the entry.
Define, BEFORE GD and on the SAME full H_m,

    eta=4M/Delta>0,
    Heta(v)=Hmax(v)+(eta/2)*min(v_3,0)^2,
    Feta=Heta^*,             x_0=e1.                 (H)

This changes the function chosen at the beginning, never during GD.
The original affine slopes, weights and offsets are EXACTLY those in
(B); the extra one-sided quadratic is explicit. It is convex C1 and
vanishes with zero gradient when v_3>=0. Old p,q have v_3=0 and old
r has v_3=k*sqrt(Q)>0, so the added term and its derivative vanish
at EVERY original entry gradient. It preserves every value and
subgradient condition for all four original entry jets, including both
r queries and the FIRST r anchor tR. Thus the run still reaches x_4.

Heta remains finite1-strongly convex with quadratic growth. The same
strong-subgradient proof as Section4 gives a finite convex C1 globally
1-smooth Feta. At v=0 the extra term has derivative0, so the COMPLETE
minimum set is STILL conv{0,y,z}, minimum0 and TRUE radius1. In an
open small positive-v_3, positive-e1 neighborhood all affine terms
except0 are negative and the extra term is0; hence smoothness1 is
again sharp. Every actual jet of Feta obeys all original directed rows
by the same necessity proof, including the first new jet below.

The proposed continuation was to keep x=z+t r and use slower negative
side gradient delta*t*r, with

    delta=1/(1+eta/2)=Delta/(E+M).

On the proposed line at x_4=z-E r it asks for
v_prop=-delta*E*r. At that v, 0,y,z affine anchors tie at0 and tR
is strictly below0. Because y is nonzero and perpendicular to q,e3,
x_4-v_prop-eta*(v_prop)_3*e3 has y-component EXACTLY y. An active
convex combination of0,y,z must therefore give weight0 to0 and be
(1-omega)y+omega*z, with 0<=omega<=1. Solving its two remaining
components gives

    omega=1-eta*delta*E/(2M)
         =(M-E)/(E+M)=-Delta/(E+M)<0.                (I)

Thus the proposed gradient does NOT satisfy x_4 in partial Heta(v_prop).
This is an original retained-anchor cap obstruction at the first new
query, preceding any unscreened suffix. For a general eta>=0 the same
negative-line expression could be legal here ONLY if

    eta<=2M/(E-M).

Our declared eta is twice that cap. Retuning independent affine
heights is not the attempted repair; (H) changes the actual curvature.
The exact failure is its invariant-line mapping, not existence or
smoothness of the static function, and not all off-line continuations.

The ACTUAL first new gradient is explicit:

    g_eta,4=k*Delta*q
             -k*(M+E)/(1+eta)*sqrt(Q)*e3.            (J)

It has negative third coordinate. Its y and0 affine values are0.
Since eta>2M/Delta,

    z.g_eta,4=-M*k^2 Q[Delta-(M+E)/(1+eta)]<0,
    tR.g_eta,4-sR=z.g_eta,4+s(r.g_eta,4-R)<0,
    r.g_eta,4=-k^2 Q[Delta+(M+E)/(1+eta)]<0.

Consequently y is active and
x_4=y+g_eta,4+eta*(g_eta,4)_3*e3 is its actual strong subgradient
condition. Uniqueness gives(J), with exact actual value

    2Feta(x_4)=k^2 Q[Delta^2+(M+E)^2/(1+eta)].        (K)

The q and e3 coefficients in(J) have unequal absolute values, so(J)
is not parallel to r. The VERY NEXT initialized update on this changed
function is x_5=x_4-b_0*g_eta,4 and leaves the claimed scalar line.

No actual terminal value of Feta on its off-line suffix is claimed.
The paid result is the exact scoped obstruction(I) and actual exit(J),
not a fabricated full-run success or W_n upper. Its remaining off-line
region mapping is a new mathematical task; the current allocation does
not hide it inside the already-refuted scalar formula. Every original
step of H_m remains the declared word, with the same enclosing m.

## 6. Different original checkpoints really alter the named return debt

This is a NECESSARY-guard diagnostic, not an additional fourth function
policy or a claimed full run. The guard from the old report is jointly
re-proved to avoid using an unverified brick.

Suppose fixed named distinct affine slopes q_ret,r_ret, with arbitrary
fixed offsets, are active at proximal endpoints Y,Z,W of a q-r-q
chronology. Let excesses D_ret,C_ret>=0, ordinary masses M2_ret-1,
M3_ret-1, where M2_ret,M3_ret>=1, so the ORIGINAL updates impose

    Z=Y-D_ret*q_ret-M2_ret*r_ret,
    W=Z-C_ret*r_ret-M3_ret*q_ret.

Let alpha_ret=||q_ret||^2-q_ret.r_ret,
gamma_ret=||r_ret||^2-q_ret.r_ret, and let ell_ret be the q-plane
height minus the r-plane height. The actual active-plane comparisons
give ell_ret(Y)>=0, ell_ret(Z)<=0, ell_ret(W)>=0, and

    ell_ret(Z)=ell_ret(Y)-D_ret*alpha_ret+M2_ret*gamma_ret,
    ell_ret(W)=ell_ret(Z)+C_ret*gamma_ret-M3_ret*alpha_ret.

Since alpha_ret+gamma_ret=||q_ret-r_ret||^2>0, alpha_ret<=0 would
make ell_ret(Z)>0, while gamma_ret<=0 would make ell_ret(W)<0.
Both are therefore positive. The guards imply
gamma_ret/alpha_ret<=D_ret/M2_ret and
gamma_ret/alpha_ret>=M3_ret/C_ret; C_ret=0 is directly impossible.
Hence D_ret*C_ret>=M2_ret*M3_ret is necessary. Arbitrary offsets,
positive endpoint heights and other retained planes cannot cancel
these ORIGINAL comparisons.

For any integer j with0<=j<=m-4, the old first-occurrence prefix is
(H_j,b_j,H_j,b_(j+1),H_(j+1),b_(j+2),H_(j+2),b_(j+3)).
It is a prefix of the unchanged full H_m, not a replacement word.
For its q-r-q assignment the actual data are
D_ret=a*c^(j+1), C_ret=a*c^(j+2),
M2_ret=1+S_(j+1), M3_ret=1+S_(j+2). The Section2 mass formula gives

    D_ret*C_ret/(M2_ret*M3_ret)<(c-2)^2<1,

so that precise return fails, even with independently chosen heights.

Now select DIFFERENT actual positions on the SAME H_m:

    i_left=2^(m-1)-2      (LAST leaf of the left root child),
    i_root=2^(m-1)-1      (the root step b_(m-1)),
    i_right=2^(m-1)       (FIRST leaf of the right root child).

These three physical queries are consecutive. If they were respectively
a q-end query, r-end query, returned-q-end query, their data would be

    D_ret=a, C_ret=a*c^(m-1), M2_ret=M3_ret=1,
    D_ret*C_ret=beta^2*c^(m-3)>1.

The last inequality uses beta>1/2, c>2 and m>=7. Thus the EXACT
necessary return debt passes for this different selection. This is not
sufficiency: the long initialized prefix, both other-plane competitions,
all retained remote anchors and the entire later right child are still
unpaid for any full named-return policy built from this selection.
Neither Policy1 nor Policy2 is asserted to realize this named return.

In particular the first-occurrence debt failure cannot be promoted to
an impossibility theorem about every original checkpoint selection,
changing gradients on edges, additional faces, or smooth functions.

## 7. Scaling and the separate theorem-level gap

For either completed full mechanism, and for the exact obstruction
function, take f=L*F with physical steps h_t/L, L>0. Every displayed
position remains the same. All objective values scale by L and the
advertised global smoothness is sharp L. TRUE distances remain1.

The original target remains OPEN. None of the three policy screens
proves that EVERY nonnegative word at EVERY sufficiently large horizon
has a witness meeting (1-o(1))/B_n with a word-independent remainder.
No such all-word decomposition or profile comparison is supplied.

The concrete progress is: legal small-ratio initialization is now
followed by TWO fully paid static full-H_m runs, rather than a stopped
prefix; the homogeneous continuation absorbs; the maximal completion
survives with its exact binary-index product and suffers a benchmark
deficit; and one actual curvature mutation fails its declared line
interface because a retained anchor weight would be negative. A different
checkpoint choice passes the known necessary return debt but is not yet
a full alternative construction. These findings constrain precise
policies and leave general off-line/multi-face continuation open.

## 8. Retrieval and resource record

Applied skills: query-memory; obtain-immediate-conclusions;
direct-proving; construct-counterexamples; search-math-results;
identify-key-failures for the final scoped failure synthesis.
All new derivations are one joint UNVERIFIED candidate, with accepted
facts cited above as the only correctness premises. No fact_submit or
formal service was called because TASK.md/guidance explicitly forbid
the gate in this ONE analytical allocation.

Three Matlas searches were performed: polyhedral projection/edge
continuation; preserving conjugate squared-hinge curvature; exact binary
prefix products. The returned shadow-dynamics/Moreau-composition,
conjugacy/coordinate-smoothness and unrelated zeta/Hurwitz results did
not supply the required full continuation. No returned theorem is
imported as a black box.

One primary-source web search recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods, arXiv1502.05666. The exact OWN previously
downloaded text was read at
downloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt,
including Lemma2's conjugacy proof and Remark2's construction. Their
finite-dimensional class F_(0,L) uses convex differentiable globally
L-smooth functions, and the swapped conjugate jets are
(g_i,x_i,<x_i,g_i>-f_i). At L=1 their construction matches the accepted
1252 maximum-quadratic proof used here. It is conceptual context, not a
new external proof premise. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/1502.05666v6.

The initial broad recall output was truncated; the selected FULL report
was then extracted from shared global memory into own recall_round70
and read in bounded ranges. Only own files and shared global/fact stores
were used, apart from the explicitly provided worker skill symlinks.
A text patch failed with 'Failed to find expected lines'; the exact

Text copying, JSON memory recording and SHA256/readback are

ZERO mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical replay, executed GD

# Core-entry price and the failure of the unpriced amplitude plateau

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation82 after actual ended baseline81; completed ceiling82,
attempted ceiling83, controller cap1. TASK.md/master_guidance
b2c09b6c7d00169a; plan ceb5b545039a0eed.

No fact_submit or formal call was made. Every new claim below belongs to
this one joint draft, and none is an accepted fact. All ingredients needed
by its two mechanisms are proved here. The prior amended report
7b0bb1b9c220b840 is awareness, not a proof premise.

Mechanism1 closes, as a proof draft, the globally core-invariant region
for every start. Mechanism2 refutes the concrete all-amplitude lemma
and quantifies a necessary repair loss. The whole odd-family rate and
the original unrestricted minimax question remain OPEN.

No mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed trajectory
or recurrence, solver, optimizer, benchmark DP, installation, consult,
or subagent was used. Displayed calculations are symbolic arguments.
Reads, text writes, record publication, and hashes are administrative.

## 1. Same word, fixed function, and precise conclusions

Fix an integer m>=1536. At EVERY child use the SAME parameters

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D=D_m=1+2S, n=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.

Write h_i,0<=i<n, for the chronological entries of H_m. Its last physical
entry is ell; d_m is auxiliary and is not an additional physical step.

Fix0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa. Keep one static function throughout:

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r) for r>=0.

The actual updates are x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), x_0=s!=0.
Psi_j(s) is the actual complete H_j output on this fixed function, and
M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|. The objective payoff is
2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2. Its denominator is the TRUE initial distance squared;
an interval input bound R is never substituted for s in an objective claim.

**Mechanism1: complete stated domain.** If

    kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1,                          (1.1)

then EVERY nonzero start s and EVERY B>=kappa satisfy

    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
       <=[1+21/(4m)]/D <17/(16D).                  (1.2)

The error constant21/4 is absolute, independent of kappa,B,start,m.
The statement includes equality in(1.1), arbitrarily small kappa,
all repeated exterior crossings, and final same-side middle-to-core entry.

**Mechanism2: exact counterexample to the proposed stronger lemma.**

    G(M_j(d_j*w))<=a*w for every0<w<=B              (1.3)

is FALSE. For EVERY enclosing m>=1536 choose the fixed allowed law
kappa=B=1, j=m, and w_m=A_(m-1)/d_m. Then

    0<w_m<beta<1,
    G(M_m(d_m*w_m))=theta=a*beta>a*w_m.             (1.4)

These are exact complete-word interval images attained by compatible
actual starts. This is not a local-step comparison failure.

For a possible repair, the necessary coefficient loss on this witness is

    L_m=beta*d_m/A_(m-1),
    1<L_m<1+3/(2m),
    lim_(m->infinity) m*(L_m-1)=(3+2sqrt(2))/4.     (1.5)

Thus a proposed universal coefficient a*(1+C/m) requires
C>=(3+2sqrt(2))/4. This is a NECESSARY loss, not a sufficient upper
bound for all amplitudes or for the general kink law.

The counterexample has first inner reversing level J=0. It refutes
the assertion quantified over arbitrary J, but does not settle positive J.
No third mechanism or asymmetric family is entered.

## 2. Actual function, objective areas, and all interpolation rows

The radial objective is exactly

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                         0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                   1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                       r>=u,
    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa).                       (2.1)

Values and first derivatives agree at both knots. At B=kappa the
middle interval disappears and u=1. At kappa=1 this is the Huber law
with cap B; the family kink radius has not been changed.

The odd derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with piecewise
slopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning an interval at the knots proves
|g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Hence f is finite, convex, differentiable, and
globally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0 and
its minimum is0. The true initial distance is |s|.

All ordered smooth-convex rows of actual samples hold directly. For x<y
put Delta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. On[x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating gives both directed convexity remainders at least Delta^2/2.
Consequently, with g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), for EVERY ordered pair of
physical, terminal, or optimum points,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.   (2.2)

Repeated points have identical values/derivatives. The Gram matrix is
positive semidefinite because each quadratic form is a square of a real
linear combination of the actual scalar vectors. No terminal gradient
or physical leaf is deleted.

At radius r, put p=G(r). Since p<=r and G(t)<=min(t,p) on[0,r],

    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                               (2.3)

Also G(r)<=min(r,B), so F(r)<=J_B(r), where J_B denotes the Huber
function with derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B). This value comparison does
NOT imply a universal comparison of individual kink-function steps.

For advertised smoothness L>0, objective L*f and steps h_i/L give
the same points and normalized payoff. A smaller actual core curvature
never changes the physical schedule.

## 3. Exact same-m masses and uniform elementary bounds

Throughout,

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>99/100,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2, 2/5<a<1/2,
    0<theta<1/2, c*theta=beta^2<1.                 (3.1)

Use7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000 for
12/5<c<(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7.
Then a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5, while a<1/2 since c>2.

Define

    t=c*(c-2), eta=a/(c-2)=beta/t.

Since lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, t>1>beta and0<eta<1.
The literal word recursion and its exact solution are

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j, j>=0.                 (3.2)

Substitution verifies the formula and A_0=1. Hence

    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    A_j>d_j/(c-2), d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2 for j>=0.      (3.3)

The second strict inequality follows from the positive term
(1-eta)*2^j.

Let d=d_(m-1). By(3.1), d>(2/5)*2^(m-1)=2^m/5.
For all integers m>=8, 2^m>20m: it holds at8, and doubling preserves
the inequality because2m>=m+1. Thus

    d>4m=1/epsilon, 1/d<epsilon,
    D=2A_m-1<2c^m=2d_m/a.                         (3.4)

The exact expansion

    t=1+2lambda*(lambda-1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2

and6<2lambda*(lambda-1)=4+2sqrt(2)<7, lambda^2<6 give

    1+6epsilon<t<1+8epsilon.                       (3.5)

Here epsilon<1/6 bounds6epsilon^2 by epsilon.

At every physical level-k bridge, the actual original prefix mass P
satisfies P>=S_k, because its full left H_k child is present.
The actual suffix after that bridge is H_k followed by zero or more
pairs(h_l^lev,H_l) with strictly increasing ancestor levels l>k.
Ascend the binary tree, retaining an ancestor exactly when its bridge
and right child remain ahead. This accounts for every remaining physical
leaf, including the terminal leaf, without resetting m.

## 4. No-crossing objective inequality

For any same-side segment of physical mass T from radius R to r with
no strict sign crossing, a zero hit makes the final objective0.
Otherwise radii decrease, and every queried gradient is at least
p=G(r). Telescoping the actual updates gives R>=r+T*p. By(2.3),

    2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).           (4.1)

The last step completes the square in p. With T=S this proves the
whole-word1/D bound for every no-crossing history, including same-side
terminal middle-to-core entry.

## 5. Complete unit-Huber reference, proved jointly

First set B=1 and use J_1. Its exact one-step interval radius for
h=1+d_h>=1 is

    V_h(R)=max(d_h*min(R,1), R-h).                 (5.1)

The odd continuous step map has extrema at an endpoint or a knot.
It maps[-R,R] ONTO the symmetric interval with this radius.
Let U_j(R) be the exact interval radius for complete H_j. Then

    U_(j+1)=U_j composed with V_(h_j^lev) composed with U_j. (5.2)

All radius maps are nondecreasing. Composition is exact, since a continuous
odd map has a connected symmetric interval image. Thus no incompatible
choice of separate child starts occurs.

First prove by induction

    U_j(A_j)=1,
    U_j(R)=R-S_j for R>=A_j,
    U_j(R)<=max(1,R-S_j) for R>=0.                  (5.3)

The empty word has these properties. At input A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
the first child gives A_j+d_j+1. The bridge has staying output A_j
and crossing output at most d_j<A_j. The second child gives1.
A positive exterior start R>=A_(j+1) stays saturated at every query
and through the terminal, giving R-S_(j+1). Monotonicity bounds smaller
interval inputs by1. This proves(5.3).

Next prove simultaneously, for every integer r>=0,

    U_j(d_j*theta^r)<=theta^(r+1), j>=1.            (5.4)

For j=1, d_1=beta<1 and the output is exactly
a*beta*theta^r=theta^(r+1). At the unscaled parent input c*d_j,
(5.3) bounds the first child by max(1,c*d_j-S_j). The bridge's
crossing term is at most d_j. Its staying term is at most d_j because

    c*d_j-S_j-(1+d_j)<=d_j
    iff (c-2)*d_j<=A_j,

which follows from(3.3). Apply the second child's r=0 bound.
For r>=1, c*theta<1 gives
c*d_j*theta^r<=d_j*theta^(r-1). The first child gives at most theta^r<1,
the bridge at most d_j*theta^r, and the second child at most
theta^(r+1). This proves the full family(5.4).

For0<R<=d_m, choose r>=0 with
d_m*theta^(r+1)<R<=d_m*theta^r. Monotonicity implies

    U_m(R)<=theta^(r+1)<R/d_m.                    (5.5)

For an actual strict crossing at level k, the landing radius is at most
d_k. If k>=1, the following full H_k gives radius<=theta<a by(5.4);
if k=0, the landing itself is<=a. At each later retained ancestor l,
an incoming radius<=a<1 becomes at most a*d_l<=d_l at the bridge,
and the full right H_l gives<=theta. The exact suffix from Section3
therefore proves

    any strict crossing in H_m => terminal radius<=a. (5.6)

All repeated crossings and all remaining leaves are included.

For R<=d_m use2J_1(r)<=r^2 and(5.5) to bound payoff by1/d_m^2.
For R>=d_m with a crossing use(5.6) to bound it by a^2/d_m^2.
Both are less than1/D, since D<2d_m/a and d_m>2/a by(3.1),(3.4).
No-crossing histories obey(4.1), giving1/D.
Thus the complete all-start Huber profile is<=1/D.

The start R=D=1+2S stays saturated through terminal radius1+S
and has payoff[2(1+S)-1]/D^2=1/D. The exact profile is therefore1/D.
Scaling this reference Huber function by B gives the same profile for
every cap B>0 on the same physical word. This reference calculation does
not change the cap or kink of an actual outward-kink trajectory.

## 6. Mechanism1: exhaustive history split in the invariant-core region

Assume(1.1) through Section7. Every physical step has0<kappa*h_i<=1.
From any radius r<=1 the next radius is(1-kappa*h_i)*r<=r, with
the same sign or0. ANY entry into the closed core is absorbing, even
inside an incomplete child. Four cases exhaust the starts.

**I. Initial-core starts.** For0<|s|<=1 every actual update is quadratic,
and the payoff is

    kappa*product_(i=0)^(n-1)(1-kappa*h_i)^2
       <=kappa*exp(-2kappa*S).

The maximum of z*exp(-2Sz) over z>=0 is1/(2exp(1)S), by differentiation.
Since S>1 and exp(1)>2,

    D*kappa*exp(-2kappa*S)
       <=(2+1/S)/(2exp(1))<3/4.                   (6.1)

Zero factors give zero payoff. Every remaining physical factor is retained.

**II. No strict crossing.** Apply(4.1) with T=S to obtain payoff<=1/D.
This includes zero hits and same-side core entry.

**III. A crossing occurs and the trajectory ever enters the core.**
Consider its FIRST strict crossing, with level k, source radius r,
gradient p=G(r), and actual original prefix mass P.
A previous core entry would have been absorbing, so r>1.
For an exterior source,

    r>=p+v.                                       (6.2)

In the middle this is equality; in the tail it follows from r>=B+v,
p=B. The strict crossing condition(1+d_k)*p>r implies p>v/d_k.

Before the first crossing all radii decrease and every queried gradient
is at least p. There was no zero hit. Telescoping the ACTUAL original
prefix and using P>=S_k gives

    |s|>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p
       >v*(1+(1+P)/d_k)
       >=v*(1+A_k/d_k)
       >v*(1+1/(c-2)).                            (6.3)

Here v>0 since(1.1) implies kappa<1. All masses are measured from the
original start. Set Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2).

After any core entry the terminal radius is<=1 and2f(x_n)<=kappa.
This covers a crossing landing in the core, any number of earlier
exterior crossings, and a FINAL SAME-SIDE middle-to-core entry.
Consequently

    2f(x_n)/s^2<kappa/(v^2*Q^2).                  (6.4)

Section7 bounds this using the true largest step and same-m mass.

**IV. Crossings occur but the core is never entered.**
Every queried point AND the actual terminal have radius>1.
A same-side middle update at h=1+d would have radius

    r-(1+d)*(r-v)=1-(1+d)*kappa-d*(r-1)<1,

so it cannot occur. Every update is either a strict crossing or a
same-side stay queried on the saturated tail.

For a strict crossing,

    |x_next|=h*G(r)-r<=d*G(r)<=d*min(r,B).

For a tail stay, |x_next|=r-hB. Both are bounded by the Huber radius
map V_(h,B)(r)=max(d*min(r,B),r-hB). Monotonicity and chronological
composition over ALL physical steps bound the actual terminal radius by
the Huber interval radius from |s|. Use F<=J_B and Section5 to get1/D.
No actual terminal query or same-side core entry is hidden in this case.

After a crossing, either some point enters the core or every point stays
outside it. Cases I-IV are therefore exhaustive. The terminal core-entry
exception to a purely exterior proof belongs to CaseIII and is paid by
the first original crossing.

## 7. Uniform evaluation of the crossing price

Let d=d_(m-1). The function z/(1-z)^2 increases on0<z<1, its derivative
being(1+z)/(1-z)^3. Under(1.1),

    kappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2.

With D<2c^m, d=a*c^(m-1), beta=ac, and(c-1)^2=t+1,

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)
    <=D*(1+d)/d^2 * (c-2)^2/(c-1)^2
    <(2c/a)*(1+1/d)*(c-2)^2/(c-1)^2
    =[2t^2/((1-epsilon)*(t+1))]*(1+1/d).          (7.1)

Since t>1, 2/(t+1)<=1. Equations(3.4),(3.5) imply

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)
       <[(1+8epsilon)^2/(1-epsilon)]*(1+epsilon).

For0<epsilon<1/1000 the exact difference is

    (1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
       =2epsilon-83epsilon^2>0.

Also(1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)=1+20epsilon+19epsilon^2
<=1+21epsilon because epsilon<1/19. Therefore

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)<1+21epsilon=1+21/(4m).       (7.2)

Since m>=1536>84 this is less than17/16. The four cases establish
the complete stated domain(1.2). In particular v and the first exterior
crossing's original radius cost are retained until the final estimate.

## 8. Mechanism2: an exact complete-word counterexample

Set kappa=B=1. Then G(r)=min(r,1), the actual function is J_1,
and M_j=U_j. This is one fixed member of the required family.
The first inner reversing level J is0 because ell>1.

We first prove the exact identity

    U_j(d_j)=theta for EVERY j>=1.                 (8.1)

Its upper bound is(5.4), proved in this joint report.
At j=1 equality holds since d_1=beta<1.
Put Delta_j=d_j-A_(j-1). At j=2,

    Delta_2=beta*c-2-a
           =epsilon*(2/c-lambda*epsilon)>0.       (8.2)

Substitute c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/(lambda*(1+epsilon))
and lambda-1/lambda=2 to check the equality. For the sign,
c<5/2 gives2/c>4/5, while lambda*epsilon<1/400.
For every j>=2 the exact recursion gives

    Delta_(j+1)=2Delta_j+(c-2-1/c)*d_j>0,          (8.3)

because t=c*(c-2)>1. Thus d_j>A_(j-1) for j>=2.

Assume(8.1) at j-1. At interval input A_(j-1), the first H_(j-1)
gives radius1 by(5.3), the bridge gives d_(j-1) by(5.1), and the
second child gives theta by induction. Hence U_j(A_(j-1))=theta.
Monotonicity and d_j>A_(j-1) give the lower bound in(8.1).
In particular,

    U_m(A_(m-1))=theta.                            (8.4)

Set q=2/c<5/6. Since d_m=beta*c^(m-1), the proposed amplitude is

    w_m=A_(m-1)/d_m
       =[eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)]/beta.                (8.5)

From(3.5) and epsilon<1/1000,

    eta=beta/t>1/2,
    0<1-eta=(t-beta)/t<9epsilon,
    beta*t-1>(1-epsilon)*(1+6epsilon)-1
                  =5epsilon-6epsilon^2>4epsilon.

It follows that

    beta^2-eta=eta*(beta*t-1)>2epsilon.             (8.6)

Furthermore(5/6)^4<1/2 and m-1>=12 give

    (1-eta)*q^(m-1)<9epsilon/8<2epsilon.            (8.7)

Thus eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)<beta^2 and0<w_m<beta<1=B.
The actual interval input is d_m*w_m=A_(m-1), and theta<1.
Equation(8.4) yields

    G(M_m(d_m*w_m))=G(theta)=theta=a*beta>a*w_m,

proving(1.4) for every enclosing m in the assignment.

Exact interval composition in Section5 ensures compatible actual starts,
not adversarially reset child inputs. An actual nonzero start in
[-A_(m-1),A_(m-1)] attains terminal magnitude theta. For a precise
selection take the least s in[0,A_(m-1)] with |Psi_m(s)|=theta.
The set is nonempty by(8.4) and oddness, compact by continuity, and
its minimum is positive since Psi_m(0)=0. This specifies a single
complete trajectory on the fixed function. No objective claim replaces
that true s by the interval radius.

## 9. Exact necessary coefficient price; no sufficient repair claimed

The witness's ratio to the asserted right-hand side is

    L_m=theta/(a*w_m)=beta/w_m
       =beta^2/[eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1)].              (9.1)

Section8 gives L_m>1. Since the second denominator term is positive,

    L_m<beta^2/eta=beta*t.

The exact expansion, using lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2), is

    beta*t=1+lambda^2*epsilon-epsilon^2-lambda^2*epsilon^3
           <1+6epsilon=1+3/(2m).                 (9.2)

This proves the finite necessary witness loss in(1.5).
Direct subtraction gives

    0<beta*t-L_m
      =beta^2*(1-eta)*q^(m-1)
         /[eta*(eta+(1-eta)*q^(m-1))]
      <36epsilon*(5/6)^(m-1),                    (9.3)

using eta>1/2 and1-eta<9epsilon. Multiplying this error by m sends it
to0, while

    m*(beta*t-1)=lambda^2/4-1/(16m)-lambda^2/(64m^2).

Therefore lim m*(L_m-1)=lambda^2/4. Any universal amplitude estimate
with coefficient a*(1+C/m) must have L_m<=1+C/m on this witness,
hence C>=lambda^2/4 is necessary.

This does not establish a sufficient paid amplitude theorem or control
accumulated losses through arbitrary inner reversals. It is impermissible
to compound an unproved per-level estimate using this calculation.

The complete objective profile of this same function is exactly1/D by
Section5. Consequently this counterexample does not violate17/(16D)
and is not an improving objective witness.

## 10. Remaining interface, source scope, and bounded completion

Mechanism1 is complete as a joint draft on precisely(1.1), with all
starts, every cap, the actual terminal, and an absolute error constant.
Its original-prefix price also pays the final same-side core-entry case.

Mechanism2 refutes the universally quantified amplitude statement at
j=m,0<w<=B. Its J=0 witness is within an already objective-controlled
subfamily. It rules out adopting the proposed exact lemma for arbitrary J;
it does not settle any positive-J objective region. A sufficient paid
variant remains unproved.

Outside the absorbing-core region and prior scoped regions, later inner
reversals may allow core exits and returns. Any first/last-entry route
must retain the entire remaining quadratic suffix. At a pre-cap gradient
p the true twice-energy is p^2/kappa in the core and
p^2+kappa*(1-kappa) in the middle, by(2.1). Gradient capture alone
does not supply the required normalized objective bound.

The missing theorem is a sufficient fixed-function amplitude or
entry/suffix bound that covers these histories, pays all kink areas,
later exits/returns and final same-side entry, and has total loss uniform
in kappa,B,start,m. It is not assumed or described as routine here.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 provides B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 and a CONDITIONAL
799/800 consequence only when17/(16D_m) bounds the FULL function-class
supremum W. The present result is restricted even within the scalar odd
family, and cannot meet that hypothesis. The assignment's
d8b59c7d92425121 is a full-class affine-height representation, not a rate;
it is not a premise in our proofs. PROBLEM.md and W=2R remain unchanged.

Recall: TASK.md and operative master_guidance were read. The amended
prior report7b0bb1b9c220b840 was fully read in bounded sections; its
predecessor0b0daa4d6847279f stays superseded. Shared obstacles
d4ca57e1a68ff721 and1ce754f83a28808a pinpointed the normalization
gap and why replacing B by w changes the function. Neither is a fact
used as a proof premise.

Fact searches before proof work found no accepted kink-family rate or
amplitude theorem. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201's statement was read directly;
other returned adversary/ray/different-epsilon statements were not used.

The search-math-results skill was used in mature_subproblem/repair mode.
Matlas returned a small constant-step GD summability lemma, a projected-GD
estimate, and a fixed-step congruency bound. Their steps or algorithms do
not match this physical word. A web fallback returned silver-schedule
and Huber background, with no kink normalization repair. No retrieved
theorem is imported. The setting is exactly the previously audited
three-piece scalar law; no new external machinery was introduced or
rejected without reading. The proof uses explicit maps and inequalities.

Skills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples. The allocation forbids fact_submit, so the
verifier-gate discipline requires all new results to remain JOINT
UNVERIFIED. No unverified assertion was promoted as an accepted fact.

Initial oversized recall outputs were truncated; focused reads supplied
the operative task/guidance, complete prior report, relevant accepted
scope, and original problem portions. No unrelated truncated finding
is represented as fully read. The first artifact patch failed with
"invalid hunk ... 'to0, while (9.2) gives'"; the text write was repaired

This completes the TWO permitted mechanisms of the ONE allocation.
No automatic renewal, TASK overwrite, worker/service control, outward
publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or goal completion
