# An actual endpoint-core survivor and a full-interface exclusion prism

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2's one new
round after the recovery wave, under TASK.md and master_guidance
98d603742ebc21e3. No numerical/model evaluation, word scan, fixed replay,
solver, verification, installation, or subordinate agent was used.
Only exact textual algebra is used below. The accepted inputs are
04219554e41d9750 (the full primitive certificate interface) and
1252b8823bb5a290 (the smooth-convex interpolation convention and realization).
No earlier unverified finding is used as a mathematical premise.

There are two concrete decisions, with different scopes.

* The exact projected core in Sections 4--5 of joint draft
  554ec17f7f590bf0 is NONEMPTY. An explicit four-dimensional region passes
  every condition of that core, including the stronger Ulift endpoint,
  all strict boundaries, the product condition, and the last triangle.
* Every full original primitive certificate is impossible when
  193/25 <= C < 87/10, 39/100 <= a <= 41/100, and
  79/100 <= b <= 81/100, with c,d otherwise free and positive.
  This excludes the ENTIRE endpoint fiber over that projected prism,
  not just the constructed point. The proof retains both bc branches
  and all twenty signs and full ambient five-gradient PSD.

The second assertion is proved directly from legal functions; it does
not depend on the earlier global bc>=1 exclusion or its draft compactness
proof. It is a scoped no-seed result, NOT a decision of the whole
four-step seed problem. The original arbitrary-schedule minimax goal
remains unresolved.

## 1. Definitions and the exact core being decided

Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,
    P=2+a, Q=3+a+b, D=2+d, H=3+c+d,
    k=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-k,
    eta_mid=b/H, J=R-k*eta_mid.

The precise endpoint core is the following set of algebraic inequalities:

    193/25 <= C < 87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97, 0<b,c<1552559/1081453,
    bc<1,
    Ca<=C-1-a, Cb<=P*H, Cc<=Q*D, Cd<=C-1-d,
    ab[1+a(C+1)]<=H,
    bc[R+b(P+C)]<=D*J,
    C^2*eta_mid^2<=J,
    Ccd<=Q, Cabcd<=1, Cbcd<=P,
    Cbd<=P(2+c),
    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]<=1,
    C^2*b^2*d^2<=R(2+c)^2-k*b*(2+c).                 (1)

Here Cbcd denotes C*b*c*d, and similarly for other concatenated factors.
On bc<1 these are exactly the complete lower-clipping inequalities,
all four cuts, the stated first and middle triangles, mixed first cut,
suffix and unit-quadratic tests, last triangle, and lifted return.
No claim of sufficiency for the full primitive certificate is made.

For comparison with the endpoint notation, put

    Ksum=C-5-a-b=c+d, y=Cb/P, m=max(1,aP),
    Bprod=min(1/(m*y),Q/C), r_end=(C-1)/(C+1),
    A=b*(R+b*(P+C))/J,
    Lcut=(C*Ksum-2Q)/(C+Q),
    Ltri=(A*Ksum-2)/(A+1).

The exact admissible endpoint interval has

    d>=max(0,Ksum-1/b,Lcut,Ltri),
    d<=min(Ksum,r_end,Ulift),
    0<d<Ksum, d>Ksum-1/b, d(Ksum-d)<=Bprod.            (2)

The last triangle is additionally retained. No endpoint belonging only
to the closure of one of the three strict inequalities is admitted.
When k=0, as will be proved on our survivor region, the actual stronger
return endpoint is exactly

    Ulift=(Ksum+2)/(1+y).                              (3)

Thus using (3) below is a proved inactive-lift branch, not deletion of
an active term. The original lifted inequality in (1) is checked too.

## 2. A four-dimensional region of strict core survivors

Let B_surv be the following explicit region in the four shifts:

    |a-2/5|<=1/1000, |b-4/5|<=1/1000,
    |c-6/5|<=1/1000, |d-8/25|<=1/1000,
    C=5+a+b+c+d>=193/25.                              (4)

It contains the exact point

    (a,b,c,d)=(2/5,4/5,6/5,8/25),
    h=(7/5,9/5,11/5,33/25), C=193/25.                (5)

It also contains a rectangular region with all C,a,b,d independently
free: take

    193/25 <= C <= 193/25+1/4000,
    |a-2/5|<=1/4000, |b-4/5|<=1/4000,
    |d-8/25|<=1/4000,
    c=C-5-a-b-d.                                     (6)

Indeed the induced c differs from 6/5 by at most 1/1000. This is not a
fixed-capacity reduction; capacities strictly above the threshold and
a nonempty open four-dimensional set occur.

Throughout (4),

    399/1000<=a<=401/1000,
    799/1000<=b<=801/1000,
    1199/1000<=c<=1201/1000,
    319/1000<=d<=321/1000,
    193/25<=C<=1931/250<31/4<8.                       (7)

The compact-domain bounds in (1) hold strictly. In particular a,d<1/2,
b<1, and c<5/4<1552559/1081453, since
4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0. Also 1/2<77/97.
Further,

    a^2+2a-1 <= (1401/1000)^2-2 <0,
    aP <= (401/1000)(2401/1000)=962801/1000000<1.

Consequently k=0, R=J=P^2, and m=1 throughout (4).

Every individual core inequality is checked next.

### 2.1. The cuts and their independent first-cut conditions

For either u=a or u=d, u<1/2 and C>3 give

    (C+1)u < (C+1)/2 < C-1.

This proves both endpoint cuts strictly. Since C<8, b<1, P>2 and H>4,
the second cut has Cb<8<PH. The third cut follows from

    Cc < (31/4)(121/100)=3751/400,
    QD > (419/100)(231/100)=96789/10000,
    96789-25*3751=3014>0.

Since k=0 and eta_mid=b/H, the mixed inequality C^2*eta_mid^2<J is
the square of the strict second cut. The first adjacent triangle obeys

    ab[1+a(C+1)] < (1/2)[1+(1/2)*9]=11/4<H.

The independent endpoint condition a<r_end is the first endpoint cut.

### 2.2. The middle triangle, including its lift

With k=0 the lifted middle triangle is exactly

    bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]<=D.

The bounds in (7) give

    bc<=962001/1000000,
    b(P+C)<=8110125/1000000,
    P^2>=5755201/1000000,
    D>=2319/1000.

Therefore its left side is at most

    (962001/1000000)*(13865326/5755201)<2319/1000.

The exact integer difference after clearing positive denominators is

    2319000*5755201 - 962001*13865326
        =7853641674>0.                                (8)

This proves strict survival uniformly, rather than relying on a sampled
value of the middle inequality.

### 2.3. Products, both return tests, and the last triangle

The bc boundary is strict because bc<=962001/1000000<1.
Using bc<963/1000, d<=321/1000 and C<31/4 gives

    Cbcd < (31*963*321)/4000000
          =9582813/4000000
          <2399/1000<=P.                              (9)

The last strict comparison has numerator difference 13187>0.
Because aP<1, (9) also gives Cabcd<aP<1. The suffix inequality follows
from C<8, c<5/4, d<1/3 and Q>4:

    Ccd<10/3<4<Q.

For the return inequality, C<8, b<1, d<1/2 give Cbd<4, whereas
P>2 and c>1 give P(2+c)>6. Since k=0 this proves the stronger lifted
return in (1) strictly as well, and therefore d<Ulift.

Finally Q>4 and C<8 give (Q+C)/Q^2<3/4. Thus

    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]
       <(5/12)[1+(5/4)(3/4)]=155/192<1.               (10)

This proves every inequality in (1), all strictly except the allowed
capacity boundary C=193/25.

### 2.4. Both endpoint components and all their open boundaries

For the projected values C,a,b arising in (4),

    Ksum>151/100, Ksum<153/100,
    Bprod<=P/(Cb)<2/5.

For the second inequality use P<=2401/1000 and
Cb> (193/25)(799/1000); these imply P/(Cb)<2/5 by direct multiplication.
Consequently Delta=Ksum^2-4Bprod>9/4-8/5=13/20>0. Both product roots

    d_minus=(Ksum-sqrt(Delta))/2,
    d_plus=(Ksum+sqrt(Delta))/2

are therefore real and distinct. Since sqrt(Delta)>4/5, d_plus>23/20.
Every admissible endpoint has d<=r_end<1, so the WHOLE upper product
component is empty for these projected values. Our actual d<1/3 lies
below Ksum/2 and satisfies d(Ksum-d)<Bprod by (9) and the suffix test.
It therefore lies strictly below d_minus, in the surviving lower
component. Its positivity, c>0 and strict bc<1 already checked in
(7)--(9) give every open boundary in (2), including d>Ksum-1/b.
The strict third cut and middle triangle give d>Lcut and d>Ltri.

The last-triangle polynomial does not introduce a hidden second
branch on this particular projected region. Writing v=(Q+C)/Q^2<3/4,
for every 0<=d<=Ksum and c=Ksum-d one has

    d*c*(1+v*c)<=Ksum^2/4 + (3/4)*(4*Ksum^3/27)
               <(153/100)^2/4+(153/100)^3/9
               =491589/500000<1.                    (11)

The product maxima used here follow from
dc<=Ksum^2/4 and d*c^2<=4*Ksum^3/27; the latter follows by
differentiating c^2(Ksum-c) on [0,Ksum]. Thus both (15) and (16) of
the preceding endpoint draft are actually decided on this region:
the upper product component is empty, the lower one has genuine strict
survivors, and the last-triangle exclusion is inactive. No closure
point or inadmissible endpoint was substituted for a survivor.

## 3. The full certificate requires nonnegative energy on legal data

This section restates and directly derives the full interface needed
for the exclusion, so no draft compactness theorem is a premise.

Let s=sum h_i=C-1, let e be the five-entry all-ones column, and let

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    Lambda_ij>=0 for all i!=j in {0,...,4}, Lambda_ii=0,
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).

The original certificate in fact04219554e41d9750 requires

    L^T e=omega, M=Dprim+(LT+T^T L^T)/2>=0 on R^5.     (12)

For any formal GD trajectory xi+1=xi-hi*gi, define

    Qij=fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2,
    V=2 sum_(i<4)hi(fi-f4)
       +sum_(i<4)hi(1-hi)||gi||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2.

Writing yi=xi-gi and delta_i=fi-||gi||^2/2 gives
Qij=delta_i-delta_j+<gi,yj-yi>. Since yi=x0-(Tg)i,
the value-flow equality in (12) cancels all delta_i and yields

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Qij=sum_(i,j)Mij<gi,gj>.      (13)

If the data are legal, Qij<=0. Nonnegativity of every original Lambda
and full PSD make the right side nonnegative. Therefore V>=0.
This uses no support deletion and no restriction on the rank of the
formal five-gradient space.

For data with fi=<xi,gi>-||gi||^2/2 and x4=g4, direct telescoping gives

    V=||x0||^2-C^2||g4||^2.                           (14)

Indeed 2hi fi+hi(1-hi)||gi||^2=||xi||^2-||xi+1||^2,
while f4=||g4||^2/2. These identities prove (14) after summation.

Whenever all ordered Qij, including the zero optimum jet, are checked
below, the interpolation construction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290
produces one everywhere-defined convex differentiable 1-smooth function
realizing the whole trajectory, with minimum zero at zero. One explicit
form is F=Hstar, where Hstar denotes the convex conjugate of

    H(v)=max_j {<xj,gj>-fj+<xj,v-gj>+||v-gj||^2/2}.

At gi, the j-th expression minus the i-th is Qij<=0; xi is a
subgradient of H at gi. The maximum is 1-strongly convex, so its
conjugate has 1-Lipschitz gradient and interpolates all jets. The
zero jet ensures F(0)=0 and gradient zero there. This also explains why
checking every original pair, rather than a selected subset, suffices.

## 4. Direct necessary endpoint and clipping inequalities

These short derivations are included for the projected-prism proof.
They apply to a full certificate via (13), independently of any old
draft prerequisite package.

### 4.1. The last endpoint and third cut

For the last cut use the scalar function with derivative
clamp(x,[-d,1]), and start at x0=4+a+b+c=C-1-d.
Its first four query gradients are 1 and its terminal position and
gradient are -d. All samples have saturated values as in (14).
Thus V=(C-1-d)^2-C^2d^2>=0. Both quantities being nonnegative,

    d<=r_end=(C-1)/(C+1)<1.                           (15)

For the third cut let eta=c/(2+d), use derivative
clamp(x,[-eta,1]), and start at x0=Q. Its positions and gradients are

    x=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),
    g=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta).

The updates are exactly the given h, and the lower plateau ends at
x4=g4=-eta. Equation (14) yields

    C*eta<=Q.                                        (16)

The derivative of either scalar function is continuous, nondecreasing,
and 1-Lipschitz; integration with f(0)=0 proves convexity, smoothness
and the stated minimum. Its saturated values are xg-g^2/2 on every
plateau and at each adjoining quadratic endpoint.

### 4.2. Both lower-clipping branches, with the terminal regime checked

The independent triangle proof in Appendix A gives cd<1 before any
clipping result is used. This order matters: alpha=b and cd>1 would
put the terminal point below the lower clip and invalidate (14).

Start at x0=P and use f'(x)=clamp(x,[-alpha,1]). The first two
positions are x1=1 and x2=-b, with g0=g1=1.

If bc<1, choose alpha=b. Then g2=-b, x3=bc is in (0,1), g3=bc,
and x4=-bcd is in [-b,0] because cd<1. Thus g4=x4. The plateau
and quadratic samples all have saturated values, so (14) gives

    C*b*c*d<=P, if bc<1.                              (18)

If bc>=1, choose alpha=(1+b)/(1+c). The inequality bc>=1 gives
alpha<=b, so g2=-alpha and x3=-b+(1+c)alpha=1. Hence g3=1 and
x4=-d. Moreover cd<1 gives d<1/c, and

    alpha-1/c=(bc-1)/(c*(1+c))>=0.

Thus -d belongs to [-alpha,0], g4=x4, and all samples again have
saturated values. Equation (14) yields

    C*d<=P, if bc>=1.                                (19)

The equality bc=1 belongs to the second case. These are explicit
legal functions in both branches; no unproved terminal-clipping
condition is used. Their only extra prerequisite, cd<1, has its
independent proof in Appendix A.

## 5. A complete legal repaired-cut witness

Let a,b,c,d>0 and eta=c/(2+d). Assume b*eta<1. Put

    epsilon=(1+eta)/(a^2*b*(1-b*eta))>0,
    Jquad=[(1-b*eta)*Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2*b)]/(1+b).

Define two scalar arrays, each following the original four steps:

    xB=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),
    gB=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta),
    xA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,-a*b*eta),
    gA=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*eta,-a*b*eta).

In each array define fi=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. Now take the two-coordinate
sum with positions X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i), gradients
G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i), and values F_i=fB_i+epsilon*fA_i.
Include the optimum X_star=G_star=F_star=0.

### 5.1. Every original pair and the energy

For saturated values Qij=<gi-gj,xj-gj>. The B array is the legal
scalar cut from Section 4.1: at targets0,1 the normal is positive
and the gradient1 is maximal; at target3 the normal is -(c-eta)<0
and gradient-eta is minimal; targets2,4 and the optimum have zero
normal. All base pairs, including optimum pairs, are nonpositive.

In array A, every normal is zero except that at target3, which is
-a*b*(c-eta)<0. The source gradients at0,1,2,4 and the optimum are
respectively 1,-a,ab,-ab*eta,0. Only source1 lies below -ab*eta,
because b*eta<1. Thus the ONLY positive auxiliary residual is

    Q_A(1,3)=a^2*b*(1-b*eta)*(c-eta)>0.

Its base residual is

    Q_B(1,3)=-(1+eta)*(c-eta)<0.

The chosen epsilon cancels this pair exactly. Every other original
ordered finite or optimum pair is the sum of nonpositive residuals.
Both arrays follow GD, so the two-coordinate array does too. The
interpolation construction after (14) realizes one actual function.

Both terminal positions equal their gradients. Therefore

    V=Q^2-C^2*eta^2
         +epsilon*(1-C^2*a^2*b^2*eta^2)
      =(1+b)/(1-b*eta) * (Jquad-C^2*eta^2).            (20)

Every full certificate must consequently satisfy

    C^2*eta^2<=Jquad.                                 (21)

This is the unit-curvature j=2 member of C83 and the scalar branch of
the repaired last-pair inequality. Its proof above is independent of
the old draft derivations and uses all ordered pairs.

### 5.2. The repair is applicable throughout the projected prism

Under the projected-prism hypotheses in Section 6 and full
feasibility, the third cut (16) gives

    eta<=Q/C<(17/4)/(193/25)<1.

Since b<=81/100, it follows that b*eta<1. Thus the repaired-cut
witness above applies throughout the prism, with every denominator
strictly positive. This applicability argument uses no clipping
inequality. The prerequisite cd<1 used by the separate clipping
functions is proved independently in Appendix A.

## 6. The entire projected prism is excluded from the full interface

The statement is: no full certificate (12) exists with

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    39/100<=a<=41/100, 79/100<=b<=81/100,
    c>0,d>0, C=5+a+b+c+d.                             (22)

All prerequisites needed in this proof are explicitly rederived in
Sections 3--5 and Appendix A. In particular (15), (16), cd<1, and
both clipping branches (18)--(19) are available. From (22),

    Ksum=c+d>=3/2,
    P<=241/100,
    Q<=211/50<17/4,
    B=P/(Cb)<2/5.                                    (23)

The last comparison follows from
(2/5)*(193/25)*(79/100)>241/100.

If bc>=1, (19) gives d<=P/C<3/8. If bc<1, (18) gives cd<=B<2/5.
Suppose in this second case that 3/8<=d<1, as allowed by (15).
Since Ksum>=3/2,

    cd=d(Ksum-d)>=d(3/2-d).

The last quadratic is concave on [3/8,1], and its endpoint values are
27/64 and 1/2, both greater than 2/5. This contradicts cd<2/5.
Both branches, including the equality bc=1 in the first, thus give

    d<3/8, c>9/8, eta=c/(2+d)>9/19.                  (24)

In endpoint-root language, for the bc<1 branch the lower component
lies strictly below 3/8 and the upper component starts above
Ksum-3/8>=9/8>r_end. Both are accounted for; none is deleted by an
ordering assumption.

The third cut ensures b*eta<1, as checked in Section 5.2. Also
Jquad is strictly decreasing in eta, because
1/(a^2*b)<25/3<b*Q^2. Using eta>9/19 and (22)--(23) gives

    Jquad
      < [(1-(79/100)*(9/19))*(17/4)^2
          +(1+9/19)*(25/3)]/(179/100)
      =2150863/163248 <53/4.                         (25)

For the last comparison,
53*163248-4*2150863=48692>0. On the other hand,

    C^2*eta^2 > (193/25)^2*(9/19)^2
              =3017169/225625 >53/4,                (26)

since 4*3017169-53*225625=110551>0. This contradicts (21).
Equivalently, the actual legal data in Section 5 have negative V.
Thus (22) is excluded from the full original interface with no
assumption on multiplier support, PSD rank, or omitted gradients.

The whole strict-core survivor region (4) lies inside (22). Its
survival in the endpoint core therefore cannot be mistaken for a seed.

## 7. A second actual separator: the omitted C82 triangle

This separator rejects all of (4) independently of the repaired cut.
Fix U=14/5 and z=sqrt(11)/5, so z^2=11/25. Put

    p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), Ktri=conv{p,q,w}.

The origin lies on the base. The function
F(x)=max_(v in Ktri)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2) is convex and 1-smooth, with
gradient equal to projection onto Ktri and a minimizer at zero.
Use the actual positions and gradients

    x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),
    x1=(-1,z),       g1=(-1,z),
    x2=(b,-bz),      g2=(b,0),
    x3=(-bc,-bz),    g3=(-bc/D,0),
    x4=(-bc/D,-bz),  g4=(-bc/D,0).

The GD identities hold by direct subtraction. The complete vertex
normal checks, sufficient for every original interpolation pair, are:

* At0 the comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1); that with w is
  nonpositive when aU-1>=0.
* At1 the normal is zero.
* At2 the normal is vertical downward; g2 is on the base because
  0<b<U.
* At3 the comparison with q is
  b[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]; the base comparison is nonpositive.
* At4 the normal is vertical downward at w.

All these signs hold throughout (4). For the upper-height condition,

    (U+1)(aU-1)>=(19/5)((399/1000)(14/5)-1)
                  =11134/25000>11/25.

For the lower-height condition use c<121/100, 1+d<4/3,
D<7/3, bc>19/20, and D>23/10 to obtain

    c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2
       <(121/100)(4/3)(83/60)/(529/100)
       =10043/23805<11/25.

The last comparison is 251075<261855. Thus the full data are legal,
including every optimum pair. Although x4 is not g4, its vertical
normal is orthogonal to g4, so f4=||g4||^2/2. Telescoping as in (14)
now gives exactly

    V=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2*(bc/D)^2.           (27)

Throughout (4), (1+a)U-1<293/100 and 1-b^2<37/100, so the positive
part of (27) is less than

    (293/100)^2+(37/100)(11/25)=87477/10000<9.

Meanwhile C*bc/D>(193/25)*(19/20)/(7/3)=11001/3500>3.
Thus (27) is strictly negative on the whole region. This is a
specific legal parameter choice in C82, not an assumed formula for
its optimized envelope.

At the central point (5) the exact value is

    V_C82=5428/625 - (193/25)^2*(12/29)^2
          =-798908/525625<0.                         (28)

## 8. The other repaired-last-triangle tests at the survivor region

For completeness, the earlier last-triangle backward extension uses

    Ktail=c+d+2-cd(d+2), rtail=c^2*d/Ktail,
    eta=c/(2+d), Hpre=2+a+b, Apre=1+a+b,
    Z=(c+rtail)/(1+d),
    qlate=(1+(1-b)*eta)/b,
    qearly=[eta+(Apre*eta-1)*Z]/Apre,
    Jpre=1-2Hpre*eta+Hpre^2*max(qlate,qearly).

The previously stated test is C^2*rtail<=min(Jpre,Q^2). This is an
algebraic test being applied here; its necessity is not used in our
full exclusion. On (4), Ktail>13/5: indeed c+d+2>351/100 and
cd(d+2)<(121/100)(321/1000)(7/3)=271887/300000.
Also c^2*d<(121/100)^2/3<49/100. Hence

    rtail<49/260<19/100.

We have 1/2<eta<13/25, 319/100<Hpre<321/100,
and qlate>137/100. Therefore

    Jpre>1-2*(321/100)*(13/25)+(319/100)^2*(137/100)
          =11602857/1000000>23/2.

But C^2*rtail<(31/4)^2*(19/100)=18259/1600<23/2,
and Q^2>16. So this entire backward-extension inequality survives.
The earliest normal has not been erased: if Apre*eta-1<=0 then
qearly<eta/Apre<1; otherwise Apre*eta-1<3/20 and Z<7/5 give
qearly<[(13/25)+(3/20)(7/5)]/2<1. Thus qearly<qlate by proof.

The quadratic-prefix last-triangle test retains
C^2*max(eta^2,rtail)<=Jquad. Here rtail<19/100<1/4<eta^2, so its
active branch is exactly the scalar repaired cut (21), already
strictly violated. This explains concretely which repair rejects
the survivors and why the separate triangle lift alone does not.

## 9. Exact central full-pair data for the C83 rejection

At (5), eta=15/29, b*eta=12/29, epsilon=1375/68. The arrays in
Section 5 are

    xB=(21/5,14/5,1,-6/5,-15/29),
    gB=(1,1,1,-15/29,-15/29),
    xA=(1,-2/5,8/25,-48/125,-24/145),
    gA=(1,-2/5,8/25,-24/145,-24/145).

Use (xB,sqrt(1375/68)*xA), (gB,sqrt(1375/68)*gA), and the saturated
values described in Section 5. This is the explicit original legal
two-coordinate trajectory, including its optimum. Its exact values are

    Jquad=64363/5220,
    C^2*eta^2=335241/21025,
    V_C83=-79345189/7148500<0.                        (29)

Equations (28)--(29) are exact analytical identities; no optimizer,
word evaluation program, or finite curvature scan supplied them.

## 10. Failed implications, coverage, and the remaining global problem

The proposed implication "the endpoint core plus Ulift is empty at
all C>=193/25" is false, by the full region (4). This is an actual
algebraic counterexample with every hypothesis checked, unlike the
earlier hypothetical endpoint-closure example.

The proposed implication "passing that endpoint core supplies a seed"
is also false: the same region has actual legal negative energy in
both C82 and C83. It cannot have all twenty nonnegative multipliers
and full five-gradient PSD. The proof does not infer feasibility from
flow, from the common-gradient kernel, or from an indefinite completion.

The omission of cd<1 from the clipping chronology would be invalid:
at alpha=b with cd>1, x4=-bcd lies below the lower threshold -b,
so g4=-b rather than x4. The saturated terminal identity (14) then
does not apply. Appendix A is retained precisely to prevent this
shortcut. The energy identity (14) must not be applied when the
terminal position and gradient differ.

An exact counterexample to using only bc<1 and d<1 here is
b=1/2, c=7/5, d=3/4, alpha=b. Then bc=7/10<1 and d<1, but
cd=21/20>1. The actual last positions are x3=7/10 and
x4=-21/40, and g4=-1/2 differs from x4. With a=2/5 the capacity
is161/20 and all four shifts are in the earlier compact box; this
still does not make the omitted terminal condition true.

The previous endpoint-closure counterexample is also retained:
Ksum=2, d in(1/2,1], Bprod=3/4. Its product equals Bprod at the
excluded endpoint1/2, while for every admissible d,
d(2-d)-3/4=(d-1/2)(3/2-d)>0. Thus a closed-endpoint equality
cannot substitute for an admissible survivor. In contrast, (4)
checks all actual strict inequalities.

The proved draft coverage is the whole prism (22). It does not cover
all a,b in the original compact domain. Both product components and
the full original matrix/sign interface remain to be decided outside
that prism. No new full seed, global no-seed theorem, accepted fact,
or original exact-profile solution is claimed. C88's eliminated curved
partial-tail parameters were not retuned; the new decisions come from
the omitted original C82/C83 geometry applied to an actual survivor.

Scope check against the new shared draft9bdaf79ff2503afd, requested by
master_guidance1ff21a1c2a3e6f28: that draft excludes both endpoint
steps in[7/5,3/2]. Its complete shared proof was read. Our entire
region(4) has h3<=1321/1000<7/5, so its hypothesis does not hold.
The earlier paired-endpoint restriction does not cover(4) either:
the interior shift c differs from either endpoint shift by at least
(1199-401)/1000=399/500>1/20. Thus neither known scoped exclusion
already decides this concrete survivor region. Those drafts are not
used as mathematical premises in the proof above.

## Appendix A. Independent proof that cd<1 for any full seed

The following proof covers arbitrary a,b>=0 and positive c,d. Put
Q=3+a+b and C=Q+2+c+d. It first proves cd<1 without assuming a
suffix trajectory stays within a clipping threshold. It then derives
the full last-triangle inequality.

For 0<r<1 set u=(1,0), w=(r,sqrt(r(1-r))), and
v=-(c*u+w)/(1+d). The origin belongs to conv{u,v,w}. Use

    x=(Q*u,(2+b)*u,u,-c*u,w), g=(u,u,u,v,w).

The GD identities hold. Set n=-c*u-v. The required triangle normal
comparisons at target3 are

    <w-v,n>=[-c^2*d+Ktail*r]/(1+d)^2,
    <u-v,n>=[-c*d*(c+d+1)+(c+d+2-c*d)*r]/(1+d)^2,

where Ktail=c^2*d+(c+d+2)(1-cd).
At targets0,1 the normals are positive multiples of u, and all
comparisons are nonpositive because <u,v><0 and <u,w>=r<1.
At target2 the normal is zero, as is the terminal normal. To check
the target3 comparisons when cd>=1, take r sufficiently close to1
from below. At r=1 the first numerator is
(c+d+2)(1-cd)<=0, and the second numerator simplifies to
(c+d+2)(1-cd)<=0. If cd>1 both are strictly negative and continuity
gives legal r<1 arbitrarily near1. If cd=1, Ktail=c^2*d>0 and the
first numerator is Ktail(r-1)<0; the second coefficient
c+d+2-cd=c+d+1>0 and its numerator is that coefficient times(r-1),
also negative. All optimum comparisons follow because0 is in the
triangle. Hence the data are legal for r arbitrarily close to1.

Their energy is Q^2-C^2*r by (14). Since C>Q, choosing r sufficiently
near1 makes it negative, contradicting (13). Thus cd<1 is necessary.

For cd<1, Ktail>c^2*d>0 and the choice r=c^2*d/Ktail belongs to(0,1).
The first target3 normal vanishes. Substitution into the second gives
a negative quantity (equivalently -c*d*(1-r)/(1+d) in the usual
triangle normalization). The same checks give a legal function and

    C^2*c^2*d<=Q^2*Ktail.

Since C-Q=c+d+2, expanding the difference gives

    C^2*c^2*d-Q^2*Ktail
      =(c+d+2)*{cd[Q^2+c(Q+C)]-Q^2}.

Therefore every full seed satisfies

    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]<=1,

including the strict consequence cd<1 already established without
assuming it in a trajectory. This appendix completes the prerequisite
chain used in Sections 4--6.

# A complete candidate obstruction for one real Jordan eigenvalue

Status: unverified candidate. No new fact_submit allocation is used. All new
lemmas needed below are proved in this document; no previous unverified
finding is used as a premise. Graph inputs are accepted1252b8823bb5a290
(smooth-convex interpolation and maximal completion) and b98bb807d514e80d
(the exact benchmark recursions).

## 1. Exact class, recursive family, and conclusion

Set c=13/5, a=c^(-18), b_j=a c^j, and define ordered schedules

    H_0=(),       H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m).

Their length is N_m=2^m-1 and their sum is

    S_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

For every m>=1 consider ANY finite real Euclidean space, a unit initial
vector x0, a real number lambda, and a real linear operator

    A=lambda I+N,          N^2 x0=0.

The condition is only on the initial Krylov space; arbitrary other action
of A is allowed. At all N_m+1 query indices use the exact polynomial jets

    x_i=P_i(A)x0,       g_i=A x_i,
    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),       h=H_m.

Suppose these jets and the optimum sample (0,0,0), with SOME values f_i,
are jointly interpolable by a convex differentiable 1-smooth function.
The values are arbitrary. Let F be ANY such interpolant, including the
maximal one from fact1252b8823bb5a290. Write x_end=x_(N_m), g_end=g_(N_m).

With K=3250 and the finite absolute constant

    C_J=1020+(16/7)c^11(1+8K)(1+9K),

the SAME appended step b=1 satisfies

    2F(x_end-g_end) <= C_J/b_m.                            (1)

No linear-operator constraint is imposed at the new endpoint. Dimension,
lambda, N, conditioning and values may all depend on m. The canonical
sum-based Huber channel is also O(1/b_m). Section7 proves directly from
the accepted benchmark recurrence that B_n<=1+4n^(4/3), so the ratio of
these bounds to U_(2^m)^ZJ tends to zero because c^3>16.

This is a bound on the specified Jordan class, not on unrestricted W.
It does NOT include jointly legal mixtures of different Jordan eigenvalues.

## 2. Exact polynomial identities and a necessary eigenvalue interval

Put u=N x0 and U=||u||. Finite binomial expansion, using N^2 x0=0, gives

    x_i=P_i(lambda)x0+P_i'(lambda)u,
    g_i=lambda P_i(lambda)x0+[P_i(lambda)+lambda P_i'(lambda)]u.  (2)

These identities do not divide by P_i and include repeated polynomial roots.
In particular g0=lambda x0+u and

    A g0=2lambda g0-lambda^2 x0.                           (3)

Let d0=<x0,g0> and e0=||g0||^2. The two optimum interpolation inequalities
imply d0>=e0. The first step of H_m is a>0. Adding its two ordered
interpolation inequalities and dividing by a^2 gives

    <A g0,g0> >= ||A g0||^2 >=0.                          (4)

By (3), the left side is 2lambda e0-lambda^2 d0. If e0=0, then g0=0 and
(3) implies lambda=0; all jets are then stationary. If e0>0, lambda<0
is impossible. For lambda>0 the inequalities d0>=e0 and (4) give

    0<=lambda(2-lambda)e0,

so 0<=lambda<=2. We use this closed interval and do not require endpoint
strictness. Neither an operator-norm bound nor individual eigendirection
legality was assumed.

## 3. A short actual prefix controls the nilpotent component

Consider any initial nonnegative prefix of the same jets, with total sum
T>0 satisfying lambda T<=1/20. For its query indices define

    p_i=product_(t<i)(1-lambda h_t),
    t_i=sum_(t<i)h_t/(1-lambda h_t).

All denominators are positive, and

    19/20<=p_i<=1,       0<=lambda t_i<=1/19.

The lower product bound follows inductively from product(1-r_j)>=1-sum r_j
when r_j>=0 and their sum is at most1. Equation(2) now reads

    g_i=p_i[lambda x0+(1-lambda t_i)u],
    A g_i=p_i[lambda^2 x0+lambda(2-lambda t_i)u].            (5)

Suppose U>=4lambda and U>0. Then

    ||g_i|| >=(9/10)U-(19/20)lambda >=U/2,
    ||g_i|| <=lambda+U <=5U/4,
    ||A g_i||<=lambda^2+2lambda U <=(9/4)lambda U.

At an actual step h_i, reverse ordered interpolation gives

    f_i-f_(i+1)
      >= <g_(i+1),h_i g_i>+||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2/2
       =h_i||g_i||^2-h_i^2<A g_i,g_i>+h_i^2||A g_i||^2/2
      >=h_i U^2/4-3lambda h_i^2 U^2
      >=h_i U^2/10.                                     (6)

The last step uses lambda h_i<=1/20; zero steps cause no division. Sum(6)
over the full chosen prefix. Its terminal value is nonnegative, while
optimum interpolation at the initial query gives

    f0<=<g0,x0>-||g0||^2/2<=lambda+U<=5U/4.

Thus U<=25/(2T). Combining with the alternative U<4lambda proves

    U<=max{4lambda,25/(2T)}<=4lambda+13/T.                 (7)

If lambda=U=0 this conclusion is immediate. The estimate comes from the
actual full-prefix function-value budget, not an imposed normality or
bounded-conditioning hypothesis.

Apply this to H_m with tau=1/100. There are two cases.

LOW FREQUENCY: b_m lambda<tau. Then lambda S_m<tau/(c-2)=1/60, and
S_m>=b_m/c for m>=1. Using(7) on the entire prefix gives

    U<=4lambda+13/S_m,
    ||x_end||<=1+(20/19)S_m U<15,
    ||g_end||<=lambda+U<=5lambda+13/S_m<34/b_m.             (8)

The position bound uses x_end=p_end(x0-t_end u), t_end<=20S_m/19,
and lambda S_m<=1/20. The gradient bound follows directly from(5).

SCALE REACHED: b_m lambda>=tau. Since 2a<tau, there is a first integer
r in[1,m] with b_r lambda>=tau, and b_r lambda<c tau. The actual initial
block of H_m of this depth is H_r. Its sum satisfies

    1/260=tau/c<=lambda S_r
            <c tau/(c-2)=13/300<1/20.

Indeed S_r/b_r=(1-(2/c)^r)/(c-2) lies between1/c and1/(c-2).
Apply(7) to this initial block to obtain the horizon-independent bound

    U<=K lambda,       K=3250.                           (9)

## 4. Complete sector polynomial estimate

Define the full-block polynomial

    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).

For EVERY complex z with 0<|z|<=9/4 and Re(z)/|z|>=2/3, we prove

    |z||p_m(z)|^2<=c^11/b_m.                            (10)

This is an algebraic estimate, independent of any interpolation legality.
Choose the unique integer k with b_k|z|<=1<b_(k+1)|z| and set
y=b_k|z| in(5/13,1]. Because a=c^(-18), |z|<=9/4<c, we have k>=17.
Write q=Re(z)/|z| and z/|z|=exp(i theta).

If m<=k, each factor has magnitude at most one: for t=b_j|z|<=1,
1-2qt+t^2<=1 because q>=2/3. Hence |z||p_m(z)|^2<=1/b_m.

Now m>k. A zero factor proves(10) immediately. Otherwise multiplicities give

    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)
      =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1)2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.  (11)

For j<0, log(1+t)<=t applied to the squared magnitude bounds each term
by -q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2. Put d=2/c=10/13. We have d^17<1/40: d^3<1/2
and d^2<4/5 imply d^17=(d^3)^5 d^2<1/40. Thus the sum of negative-index
terms is at most

    -(13/4)q y+(25/119)y^2.

Here sum_(r=1)^k(2/c)^r>=13/4 and
(1/2)sum_(r>=1)(2/c^2)^r=25/119. For j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3 give
|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The nonnegative upper bounds
j log c+log y can be extended to an infinite sum; their weighted sum is
2log c+log y. The j=0 term is at most (1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2).
Consequently(11) is at most

    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2
          +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).

We give exact details for E(y)<-1/128 on[5/13,1]. For5/13<=y<=1/2,
the quadratic term is at most25/476; D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2 is decreasing
and at most322/507; and -(13/6)y+log y<=-1+log(6/13). Hence

    E(y)<=-1+25/476+log A0,
    A0=(78/25)sqrt(322/507),      A0^2=3864/625<25/4.

The first four positive terms of exp(15/16) sum to61791/24576>5/2.
Thus log(5/2)<15/16 and E(y)<-19/1904<-1/128.

For1/2<=y<=1 the derivative has positive denominator and numerator

    E'(y)=B(y)/[2142yD(y)],
    B(y)=900y^4-5841y^3+11372y^2-8925y+2142.

Set t=2y-1 in[0,1]. Direct expansion yields

    16B((1+t)/2)
      =-2422-11870t+15842t^2-8082t^3+900t^4
      <=-2422+t[-11870+15842t-7182t^2]
      <=-2422-3210t<0.

The last inequality uses that 15842t-7182t^2 increases on[0,1] and its
value at1 is8660. Thus E decreases on this interval, proving the claim.
We obtain

    |p_m(z)|<=exp(-2^(m-k-1)/128).

Let r=m-k>=1. Since |z|<=c^r/b_m,

    |z||p_m(z)|^2<=(1/b_m)c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64).

For1<=r<=11 the extra factor is at mostc^11. For r>=12,
2^(r-7)>=r, by induction from12, and log c<1 since exp(1)>8/3>c.
The extra factor is then at most1. This proves(10), including all roots.

## 5. Polynomial derivatives and the free terminal completion

In the scale-reached case lambda>0. Consider the circle

    z=lambda+(lambda/8)exp(i t),       0<=t<=2pi,

and let M0 be the maximum of |p_m| on this circle. Every point satisfies
7lambda/8<=|z|<=9lambda/8<=9/4 and Re(z)/|z|>=7/9>2/3.
Equation(10) therefore implies

    lambda M0^2 <=8c^11/(7b_m).                          (12)

The finite polynomial expansion around lambda gives the exact coefficient
identities

    p_m(lambda)=(1/(2pi))integral_0^(2pi)
                       p_m(lambda+(lambda/8)exp(i t))dt,
    (lambda/8)p_m'(lambda)=(1/(2pi))integral_0^(2pi)
                       p_m(lambda+(lambda/8)exp(i t))exp(-i t)dt.

To verify them, expand the polynomial in powers of (z-lambda) and integrate
each monomial: every nonconstant Fourier mode has integral zero. No external
complex-analysis theorem and no division by p_m(lambda) is needed. Hence

    |p_m(lambda)|<=M0,        lambda|p_m'(lambda)|<=8M0.

Together with(2),(9), this gives

    ||x_end|| <=(1+8K)M0,
    ||g_end|| <=lambda(1+9K)M0.                           (13)

For every interpolant F, optimum interpolation and unit-step descent give

    2F(x_end-g_end)
       <=2<x_end,g_end>-2||g_end||^2
       <=2||x_end||||g_end||.                            (14)

Unit-step descent follows by integrating the1-Lipschitz gradient along the
unit gradient step; it does not assume that the new gradient equals A times
the new point. Apply(8) to(14) in the low-frequency case, and(12),(13) in
the scale-reached case. Their bounds are respectively1020/b_m and
(16/7)c^11(1+8K)(1+9K)/b_m. Their sum C_J/b_m proves(1).

## 6. An explicit legal genuinely nonnormal calibration

This section is not used in the upper bound. It shows that the class has
nontrivial legal prefixes for every supplied nonnegative schedule, with a
single fixed strongly convex function and a uniform final-step lower witness.

For total sum S choose lambda=1/[10(S+1)], eta=lambda/10,
x0=(0,1), and A=[[lambda,eta],[0,lambda]]. Thus N^2=0 and N!=0.
Write p_i=P_i(lambda), t_i=sum_(t<i)h_t/(1-lambda h_t). The exact positions are

    x_i=(s_i,tcoord_i)=(-eta p_i t_i,p_i).

The use of tcoord_i as a coordinate is distinct from the sum t_i above.
We have p_i>=9/10 and lambda t_i<=1/9. Delete only repeated knots caused
by zero steps. Along every positive step,

    (s_(i+1)-s_i)/(tcoord_(i+1)-tcoord_i)
          =(eta/lambda)(1-lambda t_i) in[0,1/10].

Join these query knots and(0,0) by a continuous piecewise affine graph
s=psi(t). The segment from(0,0) to the last query has slope
-eta t_end with magnitude at most1/90. Extend psi constantly outside the
extreme knots. Then psi(0)=0 and psi is globally1/10-Lipschitz.

Define on R^2 the single function

    F0(s,t)=lambda(s^2+t^2)/2+eta s t-eta integral_0^t psi(v)dv.

Its gradient is (lambda s+eta t,lambda t+eta(s-psi(t))). Thus at every query
it equals A x_i exactly. On an affine piece of psi its Hessian is

    [[lambda,eta],[eta,lambda-eta psi'(t)]].

For any real vector(a0,b0), the absolute value of the perturbation quadratic
2eta a0 b0-eta psi'(t)b0^2 is at most(11/10)eta(a0^2+b0^2). Integrating
the piecewise Hessian along line segments proves that F0 is globally
(89lambda/100)-strongly convex and has gradient Lipschitz constant at most
111lambda/100<1. Its gradient at0 is zero, so F0(0)=min F0=0. All query
values are its actual values; therefore all ordered interpolation inequalities
and the unit initial-distance normalization hold for the SAME data.

For clarity the resulting all-final-step lower bound can also be quantified.
Let x=x_end, g=g_end and z be the orthogonal projection of x onto g-perp.
Here g!=0 and

    |det(x,g)|=eta p_end^2,
    ||g||<=lambda+eta,
    ||z||^2>=p_end^4/121>=1/242.

The determinant identity follows by expanding(2); the terms with t_end
cancel. Strong convexity gives, for every real final b and hence for b>=0,

    2F0(x-bg)>= (89lambda/100)||z||^2
               >=89/[242000(S+1)]>1/[3000(S+1)].           (15)

There is also one test vector satisfying every maximal-interpolant anchor:
put mu=89lambda/100 and w=mu z. Then <w,g>=0. Since F0>=mu||x||^2/2,
its conjugate is at most||w||^2/(2mu). Every quadratic conjugate anchor
H_j from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 lies below F0*, because F0 interpolates
the queried data. Thus, with y_j=x_j-g_j and delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,

    2delta_j+2<x-y_j,w>-||w||^2
       =2<x,w>-2H_j(w)>=mu||z||^2

for EVERY query and optimum anchor. The maximal completion inherits(15).
This merely calibrates the sum-scale strength of this class; it is weaker
than existing general sum-based lower bounds and is not a new target claim.

## 7. Exact-profile comparison derived from the accepted recursions

Write C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 as in acceptedb98bb807d514e80d.
Their two increasing join maps become

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

For0<=t<=1,

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                (16)

Indeed put x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Direct factorization is

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
      =x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

Each reciprocal is at least1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3, the latter checked
by cubing. The endpoint cases hold as well. Since Omega is the positive
root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD, inequality(16) yields

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the primitive recurrence gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also
sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A implies G(A,D)<=D+4A. The dominant recurrence therefore
gives, for each split k and by induction,

    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).

The last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^(4/3), obtained by
integrating its increasing derivative. Initial values are C_0=B_0=1.
Consequently

    (C_J/b_m)/U_(2^m)^ZJ
       <=(C_J/a)[c^(-m)+4(2^(4/3)/c)^m] ->0,

because c^3=2197/125>16. The canonical Huber value on(H_m,1) is at most
1/(2S_m)<=c/(2b_m), so adding it does not change this conclusion.

## 8. Retrieval and remaining boundary

Matlas search for Jordan polynomial interpolation returned arXiv:1802.06355
Lemma7, arXiv:2111.06105 LemmaA.4 and arXiv:2209.07235 Proposition2. None
states the result needed here and none is imported. The polynomial derivative
identity, sector bound, short-prefix coercivity and benchmark comparison are
all proved above. No numerical sweep, symbolic computation, or solver is used.

The proof bounds one real eigenvalue with a nilpotent part of index2 on the
initial Krylov space. It does not silently extend(7) to separately illegal
components of a mixed prefix. Several eigenvalues, higher Jordan order, and
changing-generator/nonlinear correlations remain outside this theorem.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001326 -->
# C72: full primitive cone, a lossless optimum lift, and the exact reuse threshold

Status: self-contained research draft, UNVERIFIED. Sixth launch, first round,
cumulative round 14. No verifier call is authorized. All new results are proved
jointly here; shared findings are reference evidence, not predecessors.
Accepted background: b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark definitions),
0be8b54e0a33381c (square-root mass contraction), and 1252b8823bb5a290
(full smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate interpolant).

## 1. The intrinsic property and every original vector

Normalize L=1. For a nonnegative word h of length n let s=sum h_i,
H_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Call h primitive if V_h>=0 on every actual smooth convex run. This is exactly
Zhang--Jiang's primitive property: expansion of their difference of squared
positions cancels x_0 and all mixed gradient terms and leaves V_h. No norm
of x_0 is being substituted for the original objective distance.

Let e denote the n+1 column of ones; define A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i,
and A_it=0 for t>i. The formal gradients g_0,...,g_n are independent.
Choose ALL n(n+1) ordered finite multipliers lambda_ij>=0, i!=j; their
zero diagonal is conventional. Define L=diag(lambda e)-lambda and

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).

Then the full sufficient cone is exactly

 L^T e=omega,                 M=D+(LA+A^T L^T)/2 >=0.            (1)

Indeed value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is the displayed flow.
The position terms have matrix sym(L(A-I)); the squared-difference terms
have matrix sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V's diagonal gives (1), with

                  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.          (2)

Thus (1) implies V_h>=0 because Q_ij<=0. All incoming terminal edges are
present. There is no rank, symmetry, child, sparsity, or fixed-remainder
restriction on lambda or M. In particular accepted terminal reduction
1252b8823bb5a290 is NOT used to delete primitive constraints.

Adding optimum indices to this certificate cannot enlarge its feasible
cone. With x_star=g_star=f_star=0, the x_0 squared coefficient remains
zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is -lambda_(i,star). A PSD matrix with
a zero diagonal entry has that entire row zero (test the corresponding
2x2 principal minors). Therefore all lambda_(i,star)=0. The sum of finite
value-flow equations is then -sum lambda_(star,i)=0, since the V value
coefficients sum to zero. Nonnegativity forces all lambda_(star,i)=0.
Consequently the full optimum/position column is forced to be identically
zero; (1) preserves the entire remaining cone, not a guessed reduction.

A unit-slope Huber run started beyond 1+s has g_i=1, Q_ij=0, and V_h=0.
For any certificate (1) this gives e^T M e=0 and hence M e=0. This is an
EXACT equality kernel, never a positive full-space eigenvalue margin. The
numerical formulation below uses the full matrix without a kernel quotient.

## 2. Lossless intrinsic primal: explicitly restore a minimizer

For arbitrary finite sample jets obeying every Q_ij<=0 and the GD recursion,
there is a legal smooth convex function with a minimizer and the SAME V_h.
This justifies the intrinsic model at the level of actual functions as well
as the preceding full-certificate column argument.

Fix epsilon>0 and append one coordinate. Put

 g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,T-epsilon H_i),
 f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 H_i.

The GD recursion remains exact, and direct substitution shows Q'_ij=Q_ij:
the extra function difference -epsilon^2(H_i-H_j) cancels the extra
position term epsilon^2(H_i-H_j). Choose the finite constants

 c>=max_i{-f_i+epsilon^2 H_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2},
 epsilon T>=max_i{f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2}.

After adding the optimum jet (0,0,0), these are exactly Q'_(star,i)<=0
and Q'_(i,star)<=0. The full conjugate interpolation construction in
accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies an everywhere finite convex
1-smooth interpolant with this optimum and every displayed jet. Thus it
is a genuine same-function GD run with an actual minimizer.

The additional V contribution is epsilon^2 times

 2sum_i h_i(s-H_i)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,

because 2sum_i h_i(s-H_i)=s^2+sum_i h_i^2. Hence V'_h=V_h exactly.
This lift does NOT preserve the original distance or normalized objective,
and is used ONLY for intrinsic primitive validity. Zero steps and n=0
cause no exception; identical original queries stay identical after a zero
step. All formal original gradients remain in the final cone model.

It follows that minimizing V_h over the full Gram G>=0, trace(G)=1, all
ordered Q_ij<=0, and a harmless value gauge f_n=0 detects primitive failure
exactly at the sign level. Every negative feasible V yields a legal failure
by the lift. Conversely every nonzero-gradient legal failure can be divided
by its positive total squared gradient to meet trace(G)=1. Zero-gradient
jets cannot have negative V, since all values then agree. A common-gradient
Huber transcript has V=0, so this minimum is always <=0.

For positive steps the primal also has strict feasibility: use a diagonal
quadratic with n+1 distinct curvatures in (0,1) and nonzero initial
coordinates, then scale to trace(G)=1. The gradient polynomial columns
z product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees 1,...,n+1 and are independent
at n+1 nonzero distinct nodes (divide by z). Every finite distinct-query
residual is strictly negative. This is the same Vandermonde argument as in
accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Values are bounded after fixing f_n because the
two ordered residuals bound every f_i-f_n and trace(G)=1 bounds every Gram
entry. Thus no unbounded objective is concealed by the gauge.

## 3. Composition identities, including arbitrary PSD child remainders

These identities are rederived from the Q definition and coincide with the
proofs of Zhang--Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395v2, Theorems3.1/3.2. Their exact
local paper, definitions, and both proofs were reread. No external result
with unexamined hypotheses is needed for the algebra below.

Primitive join. Let a,b be primitive, A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b. Let d>0 solve

 d(A+C+d)=AC; T=A+C+d, kappa=(T/C)^2.

Form h=(a,1+d,b). Let p be the bridge query and z the terminal query, and
let j range over the nonterminal queries in b. Direct expansion gives

 V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)[sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)
                                  +d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)]. (3)

One way to audit all coefficients is to call the bracket C_aux; its value
part is 2sum_j b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z, and its gradient part is

 sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2
                                          +(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.

Insert these in (3), and use (kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2,
kappa C^2=T^2. The mixed terms cancel. Since C>d and kappa>1, every
bracket coefficient is nonnegative. Child certificates with matrices M_a,
M_b produce the FULL parent remainder M_a+kappa M_b on disjoint original
child-gradient sets. They may have arbitrary PSD rank. Its mass is

 T=Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2.

Dominant join. Define, for weights u_i>=0 of mass D=2sum h+1,

 J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z
                              -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

Dominance means J>=0 on every legal run; it implies W(h)<=1/D by Q_i*<=0.
The empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with
u=(h,s+1), because expanding the last square gives J=V_h exactly.
For a primitive left child of mass A, and a dominant right child of mass D
and weights v, put

 mu=2A/D, r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, gamma=A+1, beta=1+A/r.

The parent word is (a,beta,b). Its weights are u=(a,gamma,r v), all
nonnegative, of total G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2=Dr^2. Its identity is

 J_parent=V_a+r^2 J_right-2mu sum_(i>p) v_i Q_(i,p).             (4)

To verify the equality expand both sides. The value flow uses
mu D=(sum a)+gamma=2A. The gradient coefficients reduce to
(sum a)(sum a+1)+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,
r^2=r+mu, and r^2 beta=mu+gamma r; these cancel every mixed term and
leave the defining parent square. This proves (4), including empty
children. It proves exactly the ConPD rule in accepted benchmark notation.

## 4. A finite seed threshold that compares the ORIGINAL exact B_n

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), q=1/rho. Suppose an actual
nonnegative primitive word a has length r_seed and A_seed=1+sum a. Put
m_seed=r_seed+1. The sufficient target condition is

                       A_seed>m_seed^rho.                      (5)

A gain merely above C_(r_seed) does not imply (5).
Here are the full amplification argument and the parameter losses.
First Omega(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q for x,y>=0. For positive arguments use
x=exp(u-t), y=exp(u+t), t>=0. It becomes J(t)>=0 where

 J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh t).

J(0)=0 and J(t) tends to zero at infinity. Its derivative has the sign
of qt-alpha(t), where alpha=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Direct
differentiation gives alpha'=cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing
from 1/sqrt(2) to1. Also 1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 and lambda^3<16 imply
1>q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Thus alpha-qt starts by decreasing, has strictly
increasing derivative, and tends to infinity; it crosses zero once on
t>0. Therefore J first increases and then decreases to zero, proving the
inequality. Zero arguments follow directly from Omega(x,0)=x.

Induction on each primitive binary tree now gives C_k<= (k+1)^rho.
Unrolling a maximizing dominant tree, its primitive side branches have
leaf counts l_1,...,l_t summing to n. Since G(A,D)<=D+4A,

 B_n<=1+4sum l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.

Balanced primitive trees give C_(2^k-1)=lambda^k, by the preceding upper
bound and Omega(A,A)=lambda A. Hence B_(2^k)>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k.
The number K=limsup B_n/n^rho thus satisfies 2<=K<=4. No phase limit is
assumed and this is the ORIGINAL exact DP sequence.

Take a fully expanded optimizing dominant tree at length n, with smallest
maximizing split as deterministic tie rule. It has exactly n primitive
empty leaves and one final dominant empty leaf: induction on the unique
right dominant chain proves this count. Replace every primitive empty
leaf by a and recompute all physical bridges by (3)-(4). Leave the final
dominant empty leaf unchanged. The actual resulting word H_n has length
m_seed n and is dominant. Homogeneity of Omega makes each primitive mass
exactly A_seed times its former mass.

Let D_n=2sum H_n+1. In an auxiliary scalar evaluation also change the
terminal dominant mass from1 to A_seed. Homogeneity of G makes that
auxiliary root A_seed B_n. Accepted0be8b54e0a33381c proves
sqrt(G(A,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8A))/2 and that this is increasing and
1-Lipschitz as a function of sqrt(d). Propagating the unchanged terminal
leaf discrepancy therefore gives

 sqrt(A_seed B_n)-(sqrt(A_seed)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A_seed B_n). (6)

Choose n_j with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to K. Equation(6) gives
D_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to A_seed K, whereas by the SAME global limsup
limsup B_(m_seed n_j)/(m_seed n_j)^rho<=K. Consequently

 limsup_j B_(m_seed n_j) W_(m_seed n_j)(H_(n_j))
                           <= m_seed^rho/A_seed <1.            (7)

For delta=(1-m_seed^rho/A_seed)/2>0, every sufficiently large output
horizon in this subsequence has ratio <=1-delta. This is a fixed relative
all-function upper separation and suffices to refute the original uniform
asymptotic matching claim, conditional on the actual certified seed.
The unused dominant leaf and its O(sqrt(B_n)) mass loss are explicit.

## 5. Before-solve design, calibration signs, and scope

The new discovery lane searches length15 only, with every coordinate free
and fixed capacity A_target=(1+eta)lambda^4, initially eta=1/1000. The
known Silver word has length15 and exactly lambda^4 capacity, so (5) has
zero baseline threshold gap. Source order, unequal step magnitudes, and the
full all-pair sensitivity guide proposals; no symmetry or tensor class is
imposed. Length4 is used only for two exact model calibrations.

Calibration expected signs BEFORE any solve:
* h=(1,2,1,1) has min V=0. Its nonzero lambda entries are 01:2,10:4,
  21:3,31:3,41:3,14:3,23:8,32:16,34:35,43:48,24:3. Expansion gives
  M=|g0-g1|^2+4|g2-g3|^2+16|g3-g4|^2. Huber attains0.
* h=(3,1,1,1) is nonprimitive. Use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),
  g=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. This is the
  convex1-smooth function with derivative clipped to[-1/2,1]. Direct
  substitution gives V=-45/4 and sum||g_i||^2=2. The trace-one model
  therefore has min V<=-45/8. Every actual query and optimum Q<=0.
* The full exact length15 Silver word has min V=0 by (3), starting with
  empty leaves. Its full nonnegative array is generated by (3), with
  identically zero PSD remainder. Its e kernel is not made strict.

The primal objective is NOT normalized objective error. Only an exactly
reconstructed nonnegative certificate satisfying (1) and (5), followed by
(3)-(7), could settle the original negative route. Solver feasibility,
positive residual tolerances, clipped eigenvalues, or a finite capacity
gain below (5) do not do so. All original statuses and raw primal/dual
arrays must be retained. The allowance is at most72 evaluations,120 measured
build-plus-solve seconds,n<=15,40 distinct nonbaseline words. No old budget
is renewed and no verifier call is made.
