# A sharper original-step angular boundary, with a finite mass bound at equality

Status: complete joint proof candidate, **UNVERIFIED**. Ninth max4 launch,
first round (cumulative20). No numerical evaluation, optimizer, installation,
or fact submission is used. This document gives all new arguments together;
earlier unverified common-prefix drafts are not correctness predecessors.
The accepted inputs are `1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation and maximal
terminal completion) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (exact benchmark definitions and
the bound B_n<=1+4 n^(log_2 3)).

The old 1/sqrt(2) boundary is not the boundary of the original-step argument.
Retaining the physical leaf preceding the level1 bridge changes it. Moreover,
the movement bound need not divide by its vanishing angular coefficient:
one-block look-ahead controls the missing mass at equality. These assertions
are scoped to the explicit measure below. A separate two-ray construction
shows the limiting sharper boundary is sharp for a local four-step return;
that construction is NOT a full-word construction for the positive mixture.

## 1. Word, measure, normalization, and quantitative statement

Let m>=1024 be an integer, and define

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),
    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,
    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2, l=1+a_chr, k=1+delta.

Use the ACTUAL word H_chr,0=() and

    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.

Every subword keeps these enclosing-m parameters. Equivalently the original
step at index i<n is h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent
of2 in a positive integer. Set rho=1-epsilon/2 and gamma=9/10.

For a bit vector s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) define

    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),
    B(s)=(-1)^s_0, C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m,

where i_j are the standard binary digits of the original index i. The map
s -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective. Choose beta in(0,1), and set
mu_+=-beta, mu_-=gamma. Conditional on B=b, define

    A_b(c)=(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)
                +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),
    P_b(c)=product_(j=1)^(m-1) (1+mu_b c_j)/2,
    w_s=(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2
                 *[(1-eta)A_(B(s))(C(s))+eta P_(B(s))(C(s))].       (1)

These are positive weights summing to1. Define the following explicit
finite-m quantities BEFORE choosing beta:

    lambda=rho(1-eta), omega=1-lambda, e=omega/lambda,
    d_mass=(l+k)/(l+1+(1-l)e),
    beta_M=sqrt((1-e)/d_mass)-e,
    p=1-epsilon, d_eps=gamma epsilon/2,
    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2,
    L_leaf=l/r_leaf,
    beta_R=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p)
                                  +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]
                          /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                       (2)

The candidate theorem covers EVERY

    7/10 <= beta <= beta_R,                                      (3)

including the endpoint beta=beta_R. In particular4/5<beta_R<6/7 and
beta_R tends to2^(-1/4), strictly beyond1/sqrt(2). The auxiliary beta_M
is the zero of the simpler mass coefficient; the complete proof crosses
it and reaches beta_R by retaining the actual final level2 bridge.

Prescribe only QUERIED gradients

    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<n,                    (4)

with arbitrary positive amplitudes Gamma_i. Suppose a finite-dimensional
convex differentiable1-smooth function F realizes (4) along its actual GD
trajectory X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, and has a minimizer. The anchor, values,
amplitudes and unqueried terminal gradient are otherwise unrestricted.
All isometric realizations of the Gram matrix in(4) are included. Let

    X_star be a true closest minimizer to X_0,
    R=||X_0-X_star||, F_star=min F,
    T_path=sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i,
    V_chr=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2.

Then the claimed bounds, uniform over(3), are

    R>0,  T_path<8192 R,
    ||X_i-X_star||<512 R for every0<=i<=n,                        (5)
    ||grad F(X_n)|| <= R[2(3/5)^(N/8)+3*2^17*m/N^2],              (6)
    V_chr <=2048(3/5)^(N/8)+2^29*m/N^2.                          (7)

The comparison uses the exact original benchmark and its normalized
objective2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2, preserving the project's factor of2:

    B_n V_chr <=(1+4n^(log_2 3))
                       [2048(3/5)^((n+1)/8)+2^29*m/(n+1)^2]
                   ->0,  n=2^m-1.                              (8)

For L-smooth f apply the theorem to F=(f-f_star)/L, so the normalized
objective is exactly2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2). No surrogate denominator
or inverse-Gram anchor norm replaces the true closest-minimizer distance.

## 2. Original inequalities and elementary finite bounds

A closest minimizer exists because the nonempty minimizer set is closed,
and a distance-minimizing sequence can be chosen bounded in finite dimension.
The positive initial gradient makes R>0. Translate X_star to0.
Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` gives all actual original inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,
    f_i=F(X_i)-F_star.

The optimum sample has position, gradient and value0. Adding orientations
gives, for any two actual samples,

    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                           (9)

In particular <g_i,X_i>>=||g_i||^2, and smoothness gives
||g_i||<=||X_i||. A physical step0<h<=2 therefore satisfies

    ||X-hg||^2<=||X||^2-h(2-h)||g||^2,
    ||grad F(X-hg)||<=||g||.                                    (10)

For the second assertion put d_g=grad F(X-hg)-g in(9):
-h<d_g,g>>=||d_g||^2. Expanding ||g+d_g||^2 gives the assertion.
For h>=2 the same optimum inequality gives instead

    ||X-hg||^2<=||X||^2+h(h-2)||g||^2
                  <=(h-1)^2||X||^2.                            (11)

All applications below distinguish these two regimes.

The enclosing parameters satisfy

    7/5<l<3/2, 199/100<k<2,
    1<delta*c_chr<5/2,
    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,
    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255, 1-epsilon>999/1000.                 (12)

For example sqrt2<99/70, c_chr<49/20 and delta>99/100 give l>7/5;
c_chr>2 gives l<3/2. Also delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<5/2 and is>1.
The powers of2 give omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, which implies the
displayed mixture bounds. These estimates require no numerical evaluation.

Because l>1 and e>=0,

    d_mass>1+(99/100)/(5/2)=349/250>11/8,
    d_mass<3/2.                                                (13)

For the second inequality, subtract l+2 from
(3/2)[l+1-(l-1)e]; the result is (l-1)(1/2-3e/2)>0.
Thus

    beta_M>sqrt(333/500)-1/1000>4/5,
    beta_M<sqrt(8/11)<6/7.                                    (14)

The first last comparison follows from (801/1000)^2<333/500; the second
follows from392<396.

We also need a finite bound for beta_R BEFORE the mass proof. Define,
for a trial beta in[0,1],

    z=-rho beta+d_eps, w=-rho beta-d_eps,
    alpha_ret=1-k z^2+L_leaf(p-zw),
    alpha_0=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2.

The quadratic alpha_ret has negative leading coefficient, positive
constant term, and exactly one positive root, the beta_R in(2).
The polynomial in(25) below, evaluated at p and1, gives
p<=r_leaf<1 and therefore l<=L_leaf<=l+3epsilon. Direct substitution gives
z^2>=beta^2-2epsilon and p-zw<=1-beta^2+epsilon. Consequently

    alpha_ret<=alpha_0+9epsilon for0<=beta<=1.                 (14a)

Indeed the extra upper bound is
2k epsilon+3epsilon(1-beta^2)+l epsilon+3epsilon^2<9epsilon.
At beta=6/7 this gives
alpha_ret< -157/2450+9epsilon<0, using l<3/2,k>199/100.
At beta=4/5, z^2<=beta^2,zw<=beta^2 and p-zw>0 give instead
alpha_ret>=alpha_0-l epsilon>28/125-(3/2)epsilon>0.
Thus4/5<beta_R<6/7. For every beta in(3), alpha_ret>=0, and(14a) implies

    (l+k)beta^2<=l+1+9epsilon.                               (14b)

Finally epsilon,e->0, r_leaf->1,l->sqrt2,k->2. Both beta_M and beta_R
tend to sqrt((1+sqrt2)/(2+sqrt2))=2^(-1/4).

## 3. Movement without division by a vanishing angular margin

Split the SAME probability measure into the component B=1 with all prefix
signs common, of mass lambda, and its remaining component of mass omega.
In the first component the unit directions repeat a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0 and
<a_0,b_0>=-beta. Hence the full gradients admit the direct-sum realization

    g_i=Gamma_i(sqrt(lambda)u_i,sqrt(omega)z_i),
    u_i in{a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                                (15)

An isometry on the gradient span, extended to the initial displacement,
preserves the Gram data and true radius; thus this representation loses
no actual realization. Write X_0=(x^c,x^r).

At a block start t=4r,0<=t<=N-4, let P_a and P_b be the total previous
amplitude-weighted physical step masses on a_0 and b_0, respectively.
Put T=P_a+P_b and

    A_x=<a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta P_b,
    B_x=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta P_a,
    r_0=sqrt(omega)R/lambda.

The remainder displacement before t has norm at most sqrt(omega)T.
Let A=Gamma_t,C=Gamma_(t+1). The first physical leaf gives A>=C by(10).
At the QUERIED sample t+1, its optimum inequality yields

    A_x >= l A+C/lambda-r_0-e(T+l A)
         = l(1-e)A+(1+e)C-r_0-eT.                            (16)

Indeed the remainder contribution to <g_(t+1),X_(t+1)>/C is at most
sqrt(omega)[R+sqrt(omega)(T+l A)]. Division by lambda proves(16).

The mass of the first two physical steps is D_a=l A+k C. For0<=C/A<=1,
the function (l+k r)/[l(1-e)+(1+e)r] is increasing: its derivative has
the sign of k(1-e)-(1+e)>0 by(12). Its value at1 is d_mass. Consequently

    D_a<=d_mass(A_x+r_0+eT).                                 (17)

At the QUERIED sample t+2, the coarse direction is b_0. Dropping its
positive norm term from the optimum inequality gives

    B_x+(beta+e)D_a+r_0+eT>=0.                               (18)

Substituting(17), then expanding A_x,B_x,T, gives EXACTLY

    c_a P_a+c_b P_b
       <=<b_0+d_mass(beta+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                         +[1+d_mass(beta+e)]r_0,
    c_a=(beta+e)[d_mass(1-e)-1],
    c_b=1-e-d_mass(beta+e)^2.                                (19)

Throughout(3),

    c_a>(7/10)[(11/8)(999/1000)-1]>1/4.

Since beta+e<1, the right side of(19) is<4R by(12),(13).
When c_b>=0 this already gives P_a<16R at every block start, including
equality c_b=0. To cover the entire interval(3), let
xi=max(0,-c_b). Equation(14b), epsilon<=2e, and the denominator
D_mass=l+1+(1-l)e>9/4 imply

    xi <=[9epsilon+10e+3e^2]/D_mass<13e<13/1000.              (19a)

For this calculation, the exact numerator of
d_mass(beta+e)^2-(1-e) is
(l+k)beta^2-(l+1)+[2(l+k)beta+2l]e+(k+1)e^2;
the last two coefficients are<10 and<3, respectively.

We do not divide by c_b. First rearrange(18):

    (1-e)P_b <=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                            +(beta+e)(P_a+D_a)+r_0.           (21)

Write A_rad=(1/sqrt(lambda)+sqrt(omega)/lambda)/(1-e)<2 and
v_mass=(beta+e)/(1-e)<2. If the next block start exists, its a-mass is
exactly P_a+D_a. Thus(21) reads P_b<=A_rad R+v_mass P_a(next).

For the LAST block start t_last=N-4, use its preceding block t_prev=N-8.
The bridge between them has ORIGINAL level2, H_2=1+delta*c_chr<7/2.
Writing C=Gamma_(t_prev+1), the two following short steps k,l imply
Gamma_(t_prev+2),Gamma_(t_prev+3)<=C. The b-mass added between these
block starts is therefore at most(l+H_2)C. Since the a-mass added there
is D_a=l Gamma_(t_prev)+k C, the added b-mass is at most
[(l+H_2)/k]D_a<3D_a. It follows from(21) at t_prev that

    P_b(last)<=2R+5P_a(last).                               (20a)

If c_b<0, apply(19) only at this last start and substitute(20a):

    [c_a-5xi]P_a(last)<=(4+2xi)R<5R.

By(19a), c_a-5xi>1/4-65/1000>1/8. Hence P_a(last)<40R.
If c_b>=0, the earlier bound16R applies instead. In either case,
the nonnegative chronological a-mass is nondecreasing, so

    P_a<64R at EVERY block start.                            (20)

For each t<=N-8, (21) and the bound at its next block start now give
P_b<2R+2*64R=130R. Hence

    T<194R and ||X_t||<195R at every block start t<=N-8.       (22)

The three following steps are l,k,l<2, so their samples also obey195R.
This covers every sample before the final block. From t=N-8 the next
fourth step, at N-5, has ORIGINAL level2 and size1+delta*c_chr.
Equation(11) bounds the norm after it by (delta*c_chr)195R<512R.
The final three short steps keep this bound. Thus all samples obey(5).

There are just seven physical steps left from N-8 to n. Their total
stepsize is4l+2k+(1+delta*c_chr)<14. Every gradient there has norm<512R.
Therefore

    T_path<194R+14*512R<8192R,
    K_path:=R+T_path<2^14 R.                                (23)

This mass bound is obtained BEFORE estimating any eta perturbation.
It remains uniform as c_b decreases through zero to the range(19a).
No factors1/c_b,
1/(1-2beta^2),1/(1-beta),1/epsilon, or1/eta are concealed in(23).

## 4. Exact finite-epsilon correlations and the physical-leaf threshold

For the return argument split(1) differently: the entire common-prefix
component has mass1-eta. Write its common prefix sign as V. Its four unit
directions, in the original order modulo4, are

    a_e=1,a_o=B,b_e=V,b_o=BV.

Set

    p=E B=1-epsilon,
    z=E V=-rho beta+gamma epsilon/2,
    w=E(BV)=-rho beta-gamma epsilon/2.                        (24)

The correlations are <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,
<a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z, and <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.
All finite-epsilon terms are retained. The full measure has these same
three correlations whenever only B and one individual prefix sign occur.

At ANY original leaf with amplitude ratio r>0, (9) is exactly

    r^2+(l-2)p r-(l-1)<=0.

Let r_leaf be its unique positive root. Evaluation at p and1 gives

    p<=r_leaf<1,
    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2.                (25)

The relevant original-step return coefficient is

    alpha_ret=1-k z^2+(l/r_leaf)(p-zw).                       (26)

To state its finite-m zero exactly, put L_leaf=l/r_leaf and
d_eps=gamma epsilon/2. Substitution in(26) gives

    alpha_ret=1+L_leaf p-(k+L_leaf)rho^2 beta^2
                   +2k rho d_eps beta+(L_leaf-k)d_eps^2.

Its positive zero is

    beta_R=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p)
                                  +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]
                          /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                  (27)

In contrast, the old coefficient1-kz^2 has positive zero
(1/sqrt(k)+d_eps)/rho. Replacing either by1/sqrt2 at finite m is
incorrect. The limit of(27) is2^(-1/4), while the old zero tends1/sqrt2.

For(3), alpha_ret>=0 by its exact positive root(27). Also -z>=2/3, p>0, and
p-zw>=p-beta^2>0 by(12),(14). Also z^2<=beta^2, zw<=beta^2 and
L_leaf>=l. To compare the auxiliary mass boundary with this return
boundary, specialize temporarily to beta<=beta_M. Then

    alpha_ret >=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2-l epsilon
       >=2l e-(l-1)e^2-l epsilon.                            (28)

The second inequality uses beta^2<=(1-e)/d_mass and the exact denominator
in(2). Since omega>=epsilon/2, epsilon<=2e/(1+e); the last expression in
(28) is at least e^2[2l/(1+e)-(l-1)]>0. In particular beta_M<=beta_R.
The two explicit finite thresholds need not coincide. The strengthened
mass proof in section3 covers their gap, including beta=beta_R. Neither
threshold is asserted to be the full-word feasibility boundary beyond
the obstruction interval for positive epsilon and eta.

## 5. Original return pairs, with no inverse-alpha loss

For any original i,j the full direction correlation differs from its
common-prefix correlation by at most2eta. If endpoint amplitudes are A,B
and the intervening total physical mass is T_ij, the resulting difference
in the cocoercivity expression is at most

    2eta(A+B)T_ij+4eta AB <=4eta K_path(A+B).                  (29)

Here T_ij<=T_path<K_path and A,B<=K_path, which were proved first.
Let e_0=4eta K_path.

Take an original queried odd-b return j=4r+3 with j+4<n. Write its
physical steps and gradient amplitudes as

    j: H on b_o with B_0,
    j+1: l on a_e with A_0,
    j+2: k on a_o with C_0,
    j+3: l on b_e with E_0,
    j+4: b_o with D_0.

The original high step H is arbitrary here. Put tau=z, bbar=-z>0,
M=B_0+bbar C_0 and Delta=D_0-B_0. In the common component set

    Z=-H B_0 b_o-l A_0 a_e,
    X=<b_o,Z>, Y=<a_o-tau b_o,Z>=-l A_0(p-zw).

The ORIGINAL pair(j,j+2), with(29), gives

    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0
       <=(tau C_0-B_0)X-l A_0 C_0(p-zw)+e_0(C_0+B_0).       (30)

If Delta>0, the ORIGINAL pair(j,j+4) gives

    X>=Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.            (31)

The coefficient tau C_0-B_0=-M is negative. Substitution in(30), followed
by A_0>=C_0/r_leaf from the ACTUAL leaf at j+1, gives

    alpha_ret C_0^2+bbar(2-k)B_0 C_0+B_0^2
                      +M Delta+M l E_0 p
       <=e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                 (32)

This is where the old proof discarded the nonnegative term
l A_0 C_0(p-zw), losing the sharper coefficient.

Because bbar>=2/3, C_0+B_0<=(3/2)M. Suppose Delta>=2m e_0.
The right side of(32) is at most

    (5/2)e_0 M+(1/m)B_0 M
      <=(5/2)e_0 M+B_0^2+(bbar/m)B_0 C_0.

The left side is at least B_0^2+(bbar/m)B_0 C_0+M Delta, since
2-k=1/m and alpha_ret>=0. Cancellation forces Delta<=(5/2)e_0,
contradicting m>=1024. Thus EVERY such original return satisfies

    Gamma_(j+4)<=Gamma_j+d_amp,
    d_amp=2^17*m*eta*R,                                     (33)

by(23). This proof also works with alpha_ret=0. It retains the explicit
factor m=1/(2-k), rather than hiding it in a perturbation symbol.
There is no1/alpha_ret loss. For eta=0 the same calculation directly
forbids Delta>0 whenever alpha_ret>=0 and the stated signs hold.

## 6. Uniform contractions at actual low bridges

For any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, let their
three amplitudes be A,M,D and steps1+u,l. The relative character signs are
1,B C_q,C_q. Therefore the FULL law gives correlations w,p,z, respectively,
and the original endpoint-pair sum is

    0>=-u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                     (34)

The leaf is short, so M>=D. Also pD-wA>0. The positive ratio t=D/A
therefore satisfies

    F_u(t):=(1+lp)t^2+[(u-1)z-lw]t-u<=0.                   (35)

This polynomial has one positive root. On(3), -w>=beta and -z<=beta,
because beta<6/7<gamma. Also1+lp>239/100.
At level1, u=delta<1, so [(u-1)z-lw]>=l beta>49/50. Hence

    F_delta(1/2)>239/400+49/100-1=35/400>0.

Every level1 compound gain is consequently<1/2. At level2,
1<u=delta*c_chr<5/2, and

    (u-1)z-lw >=beta(1+l-u)>-1/10.

It follows that F_u(6/5)>2054/2500>0, so every level2 compound gain
is<6/5. A four-step return at level2 is exactly the concatenation of
these two compounds; its gain is therefore<theta=3/5. All these estimates
are for the full measure and allow arbitrary adjacent slack.

## 7. Actual last steps, free terminal, and the exact benchmark

Let K=N/4. The queried odd-b amplitudes are

    B_r=Gamma_(4r+3),0<=r<=K-2.

The omitted next odd-b sample is n, whose actual gradient is free.
For0<=r<=K-3 the return bridge has level2+nu_2(r+1); it is level2
exactly when r is even. Thus(33),(35) give

    B_(2s+1)<=theta B_(2s),
    B_(2s+2)<=B_(2s+1)+d_amp

whenever all the displayed queried indices exist. The first three steps
are l,k,l<2, so B_0<=R. Iterating for N/8-1 pairs yields

    Gamma_(n-4)<=theta^(N/8-1)R+(5/2)d_amp.                    (36)

The last FOUR actual steps are, in order,

    1+delta*c_chr, l, k, l.

The first two have queried endpoints n-4 and n-2, so their compound gain
is<6/5. The last two short steps satisfy(10) for the realizing function,
without any prescribed terminal direction. Thus

    ||grad F(X_n)||<=(6/5)Gamma_(n-4)
                      <=2theta^(N/8)R+3d_amp.                (37)

Convexity, the true minimizer, and(5) give
F(X_n)-F_star<=512R||grad F(X_n)||. This proves(6),(7), since
3072*2^17=3*2^27<2^29.
Accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` gives the exact-B_n comparison(8).
The second term tends to zero because m=log_2(n+1) and log_2 3<2;
the first decays geometrically in n.

The same conclusion applies to the maximal prefix interpolant in accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`: apply the proof to that actual global function and its
own true closest minimizer. Every reduced-feasible terminal value is at
most its value at X_n. If the supplied optimum has distance at most1 from
X_0, the true R is at most1 and(7) bounds twice the reduced terminal value
as well. No incoming formal terminal inequality is imposed.

Every character-specific endpoint used above is queried: the movement
optima are t+1,t+2<=n-1; return pairs end at j+4<=n-4; and the last
compound ends at n-2. The terminal norm step concerns the actual function.

## 8. Nonvacuity for these same positive weights

This calibration is not a competitive lower bound. For the same actual
word and weights, additionally prescribe the terminal character gradient.
Let H_max=max_i h_i, kappa=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0 and

    q_decay=kappa/[8(H_max+kappa+1)], Gamma_i=q_decay^i,
    f_i=(kappa+1/2)Gamma_i^2, 0<=i<=n.

The full gradient Gram G is positive definite: the full character matrix
is invertible, as summing chi_s(i)chi_s(j) over s is N for i=j and0
otherwise, and every weight and amplitude is positive. Define

    b_i=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t G_ti,
    X_0=sum_i(G^(-1)b)_i g_i,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.

Then <X_i,g_i>=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j, direct substitution gives

    Q_ij=(kappa+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa Gamma_j^2
                            -sum_(i<t<j)h_t G_it-G_ij,
    Q_ji=(kappa+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa Gamma_i^2
                            +(h_i-1)G_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t G_jt.

Using |G_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t,q_decay<1/2,h_i-1>=4kappa,
the first divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most
-3kappa+2(H_max+1)q_decay<=-11kappa/4. The second is at most
-kappa+(2H_max+kappa+1)q_decay<=-3kappa/4.
The optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-kappa Gamma_i^2.
This checks ALL ordered pairs with the optimum and terminal.

An explicit global function is

    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly)[<X,v>-||v||^2/2].

Its maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_(C_poly)(X). The projection
normal condition is <X-P(X),v-P(X)><=0 for v in C_poly. Adding this
condition at two positions shows ||P(X)-P(Y)||<=||X-Y||. Comparing the
two maximizing expressions bounds the first-order remainder between0
and||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is convex differentiable1-smooth with gradient P.
At X_i the displayed Q inequalities are precisely the projection normal
conditions at each vertex (and the optimum). Thus the function realizes
every prescribed jet and the entire original recursion.
Its minimizer set is the polar cone {Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}. The true
distance to that set is positive and is the R used in the theorem; no
identity with||X_0|| or with an inverse-Gram expression is asserted.

## 9. Sharp local scope test beyond the limiting boundary

The following exact construction tests a proposed extension of return
nonexpansion to all beta<1. It is for the two-ray boundary epsilon=eta=0,
and uses a whole global function on its own four-step segment.
It is not a realization of the finite positive mixture over H_chr,m.

Fix1<l<2,1<k<2, unit vectors a,b with<a,b>=-beta,0<beta<1, and set

    t=l+1, s=l+k, E_ang=s beta^2-t.

Suppose E_ang>0. For a positive number J with J E_ang>t^2, set

    H=1+J, A=beta J/t, D=J E_ang/t^2>1,
    C_triangle=conv(0,Aa,Db),
    F_triangle(X)=max_(v in C_triangle)[<X,v>-||v||^2/2].       (38)

By the argument in section8 this is one convex differentiable1-smooth
function. Start at X_0=b and take the ACTUAL four steps H,l,k,l. The
claimed gradients and positions are

    g_0=b, g_1=Aa, g_2=Aa, g_3=Db, g_4=Db,
    X_1=-Jb, X_2=-Jb-lAa,
    X_3=-Jb-sAa, X_4=-(J+lD)b-sAa.                            (39)

Here are all required vertex normal checks, which imply every original
ordered interpolation inequality through the same global function.
At X_0, b belongs to the segment[0,Db], so its projection is itself.
At X_1 the residual X_1-Aa has a-projection lA>0 and b-projection
-J+beta A<0. Its products with -Aa and Db-Aa are therefore nonpositive.
At X_2 the residual X_2-Aa has a-projection0 and b-projection
-J(1-beta^2)<0; both vertex normals again hold.
At X_3 the residual X_3-Db has b-projection lD>0 and a-projection
beta D-(k-1)A<0. The latter follows from

    D/A=E_ang/(beta t)<(k-1)beta/t.

Both products with -Db and Aa-Db are negative. At X_4 the residual
X_4-Db has b-projection0 and a-projection

    t beta D-(k-1)A=-s(1-beta^2)A<0.

The normals hold at all vertices and their convex hull, proving(39).

The minimizers form the polar cone of C_triangle. For any minimizer Z,
<b,Z><=0; hence||b-Z||^2>=1, with equality at0. Thus the TRUE initial
distance is1. Since <X_4,b>=D, the terminal value is D^2/2 and the
normalized objective is D^2>1. In particular the b-return amplitude
strictly increases from1 toD.

For the actual enclosing-m values l,k, the local condition is exactly

    beta^2>(l+1)/(l+k).                                     (40)

An original level q>=2 bridge has J=a_chr*c_chr^q and is followed by
l,k,l. Thus the four physical steps coincide with such an actual segment
whenever q<m and a_chr*c_chr^q*E_ang>(l+1)^2. This exposes the entire
angular-gap cost J>(l+1)^2/E_ang. It does not assume that the segment can
be spliced into the preceding or following H_chr,m trajectory.

Conversely at epsilon=eta=0, (25) has r_leaf=1 and (26) becomes
alpha_ret=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2. Equation(32), with zero error, forbids ANY
positive return growth when alpha_ret>=0. Therefore(40) is the exact
boundary for the existence of an expanding two-ray four-step return
with arbitrarily selectable high step H. This establishes local
sharpness, not a finite-mixture or full-word threshold.

### The explicit expanding triangle cannot pass the next actual leaf

There is a concrete compatibility failure for the SAME function(38),
not merely an absent proof of concatenation. Suppose its expanding
segment starts at an actual bridge of level q>=3. The next bridge,
four indices later, necessarily has level2; write H_2=1+delta*c_chr.
Starting from X_4 in(39), its next position is

    X_5=X_4-H_2 Db=-(J+(l+H_2)D)b-sAa,
    <X_5,a>/A=-(k-1)+(l+H_2)E_ang/t.

Since beta<1, E_ang<s-t=k-1=delta. Hence

    <X_5,a>/A <delta(H_2-1)/t
                       <(5/2)/(12/5)=25/24<l.               (41)

For the original prescribed ray pattern, the next two gradients must
both lie on the positive a ray. If g_5=C a with0<C<A, projection onto
C_triangle and the two vertices0,Aa force <X_5,a>=C. The next actual
leaf gives <X_6,a>=(1-l)C<0. If g_5=Aa instead, (41) gives
<X_6,a>=<X_5,a>-lA<0. No positive a-ray projection is possible at X_6:
its normal with the zero vertex would require <X_6,a>>=||g_6||>0.
If g_5 is not on that ray, the prescribed pattern has already failed.

An original level2 choice cannot satisfy J E_ang>t^2 in(38), since
J=delta*c_chr<5/2,E_ang<delta<1 and t^2>(12/5)^2. Thus ANY expanding
segment of(38) embedded at an actual bridge has q>=3, and the above
two queried samples actually exist in the following block. Therefore
this explicit sharp local constructor cannot continue through the next
level2 bridge and leaf for ANY beta<1. It is not a candidate full-word
adversary. A different function, changing its same-ray gradient/value
geometry, would have to repair precisely this next-leaf compatibility.

## 10. Remaining compatibility condition and budget scope

The interval[7/10,beta_R] includes a fixed supercritical neighborhood of
the OLD1/sqrt2 threshold, and the explicit law beta_m=beta_R is the exact
finite-m zero of the original-step return coefficient. It tends
to the sharper2^(-1/4) boundary. Equations(5)--(8) exclude all its positive
amplitude histories, all anchors and values, and all terminal completions.

There is no full-word counterexample at beta>beta_R here. The concrete
remaining issue is to reconcile expanding high returns beyond(26) with
one function's earlier and later ray data, the alternating actual level2
returns, and a true-distance payoff surviving the exact B_n comparison.
The triangle in(38) uses one fixed cap on each ray and fails the explicit
next-leaf compatibility(41). The finite gap between beta_M and beta_R has been included
in the obstruction by the actual final-bridge mass calculation(20a).

No claim is made that failure of(19) or(26) proves whole-word feasibility.
The original all-schedule minimax target remains open. The task allocates
no verifier call, so this document and its shared copy are candidates,
not facts. All external retrieval hits required short steps or a different
flow regime; no external theorem is used. The only new machinery is
algebra in the already audited original finite-interpolation regime.

# Reflected central-unit five-step words above the new threshold

Status: COMPLETE JOINT **UNVERIFIED** analytic draft, max2 round40.
No fact_submit allocation; no new fact is claimed. The original Goal is OPEN.
Guidance d611bba957eb910a; plan f86bdad9851bf565.

## 1. Statement, novelty and scope

The candidate assertion proved jointly below is:

For every a40,b40>=0 with

 h=(a40,b40,1,b40,a40),
 P40=a40+b40, A40=1+sum h=2P40+2>=197/20,

there is an actual convex differentiable 1-smooth function, with a unique
minimizer0 and an actual five-step GD trajectory from a nonzero initial
point, on which the original primitive energy V_h is strictly negative.
Consequently the word has no original full nonnegative interpolation
certificate with any positive-semidefinite remainder on all six formal
gradients. All30 ordered multipliers remain unrestricted.

The fresh starting slice was

 (a40,79/20-a40,1,79/20-a40,a40), A40=99/10.

It is disjoint from the earlier capacity10 examples and their radius10^-8
coordinate boxes. The proof in fact covers the whole two-parameter
reflected family from197/20 upward, including every capacity above the
accepted Gamma5_c107: indeed
Gamma5_c107-197/20=38507/806000>0. The old rejected
capacity10 word is not reused as a prospective seed. Reflection is only
a choice of candidate family; no reflection closure, lossless reduction,
or symmetry of the unknown multipliers is asserted.

Only accepted facts04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290 are mathematical
inputs. The former supplies the exact original certificate identity,
and the latter the interpolation orientation. Every additional screen
and construction used here is proved jointly below. Accepted
4c92d3fc4eb6153e motivates the sufficient capacity99/10; it is not a
seed-existence theorem. No external theorem is imported.

## 2. Original identity and actual projection functions

Normalize smoothness to1. For a word of length n, retain every original
sample and write

 s=sum_(i<n)h_i, A=1+s,
 x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

The optimum is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Every actual smooth convex
trajectory satisfies all original Q_ij<=0, including both orientations
of the terminal and optimum comparisons, by1252b8823bb5a290.

Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, gives the following identity under
the full original flow equations:

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
     =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.                         (1)

Here Lambda has zero diagonal and nonnegative off-diagonal entries,
L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, e is the all-ones column,
L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T, and

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

Thus an actual negative V_h contradicts every such M_prim>=0 on the
whole R^(n+1). This implication needs no primitive dual-attainment or
completeness assertion. No formal coordinate is identified or removed.

For a compact convex set K containing0, define

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                    (2)

Completing the square gives the unique maximizer g=P_K(x), the Euclidean
projection onto K. The projection normal condition is

 <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for all v in K.

Adding this condition at two points gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>; hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing inequalities give

 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>
      <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.

Therefore F_K is convex and differentiable, with 1-Lipschitz gradient
P_K. If0 is interior to K, F_K(0)=0 and F_K(x)>0 for x!=0: substitute
a sufficiently small positive multiple of x in(2). Thus0 is its unique
minimizer.

At any such projection transcript put y_i=x_i-g_i and
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                    (3)

Checking the complete normal cone at each query therefore checks EVERY
ordered original Q, not just neighboring pairs. Since0 belongs to K,
the optimum comparisons are included, with Q_(i,star)=0.

The GD norm telescope is

 ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
     =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

Consequently every trajectory of(2) has

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                         (4)

This formula is valid for nonnegative steps including zero steps.

## 3. Global cuts applied before any matching

For any selected h_j>1, put

 P_cut=1+sum_(i<j)h_i,
 D_cut=1+sum_(i>j)h_i,
 eta_cut=(h_j-1)/D_cut>0.

Take the interval K=[-eta_cut,1] and x_0=P_cut. Through query j every
gradient is1, query j equals1, and its step lands at
-(h_j-1)=-D_cut eta_cut. All later gradients are -eta_cut: the later
nonnegative steps move monotonically along that plateau and the final
point is -eta_cut. Every intermediate query remains in the appropriate
projection normal cone, also when a physical step is zero. Equation(4)
gives

 V_h=P_cut^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D_cut^2.

Hence a necessary full-certificate test at every index is

 A(h_j-1)_+<=P_cut D_cut.                               (5)

A failed test already gives an actual negative-energy function with
unique minimizer0 and nonzero initial point. We may therefore assume
all five tests hold when analyzing the reflected family.

Set

 P40=a40+b40, R40=1+a40, T40=A40-R40,
 d40=b40-1=P40-R40,
 delta40=R40(3P40+4)-2P40(P40+1).

The first endpoint test gives a40<2. Indeed a40>=2 violates
A40(a40-1)<=A40-a40; if a40>1 the precise bound is
a40<=2A40/(A40+1)<2, and a40<=1 needs no further argument.
Since P40>=157/40, we have b40=P40-a40>77/40>1. Thus the test at index1
is applicable and reads

 delta40=R40(P40+2)-A40(b40-1)>=0.                      (6)

The same constraint occurs at index3; no new condition is deduced by
claiming a general reflection theorem. Direct substitution suffices.
The central unit step has zero left side in(5).

These surviving words also have A40<12. In fact R40<3 and(6) imply
A40(A40-2)<=R40(3A40+2)<3(3A40+2), whence
A40^2-11A40-6<0. The polynomial is positive and increasing for A40>=12.

## 4. The larger-endpoint region a40>=8/5

Keep all the preceding cut constraints and suppose a40>=8/5. Define

 B40=a40-1 in[3/5,1), C40=b40-1>0,
 D40=1+(1+b40+a40)=P40+2=A40/2+1,
 s40=A40-1.

The remaining physical steps after the adjacent pair(a40,b40) are
(1,b40,a40), of sum D40-1. We now prove and then violate the appropriate
adjacent-pair necessary test, retaining every one of those queries.

First suppose D40<=B40 C40. Use the one-dimensional function

 F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1, F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.

Its derivative is min(x,1), so it is convex and1-smooth with unique
minimizer0. Starting at x_0=1, the first two steps give

 x_1=-B40, g_1=-B40, x_2=B40 C40.

Every remaining query stays at least1, including
x_5=B40 C40-D40+1>=1, and every remaining gradient is1. The function
satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 at all these points, so the same telescope
as(4) applies. Since x_5+s40=1+a40 b40,

 V_h=1-(1+a40 b40)^2<0.                                (7)

Now suppose D40>B40 C40. Put

 Kappa40=B40^2 C40+s40(D40-B40 C40)>0,
 t40=B40^2 C40/Kappa40 in(0,1),
 u40=(1,0), w40=(t40,sqrt(t40(1-t40))),
 v40=-(B40 u40+D40 w40)/b40,
 K40=conv{u40,v40,w40}.

The vectors u40,w40 are independent. The positive relation
B40 u40+b40 v40+D40 w40=0 proves0 is interior to the triangle K40.
Use F_K40 from(2), starting at x_0=u40. The actual proposed gradients
are

 (g_0,...,g_5)=(u40,v40,w40,w40,w40,w40),

and the proposed positions are

 x_0=u40, x_1=-B40 u40, x_2=D40 w40,
 x_3=(D40-1)w40, x_4=(a40+1)w40, x_5=w40.             (8)

Every physical GD update holds, by the defining relation for v40.
It remains to check all projection normals. We have

 ||u40||^2=1, <u40,w40>=||w40||^2=t40,
 <u40,v40>=-(B40+D40 t40)/b40,
 <v40,w40>=-(B40+D40)t40/b40.

At x_0 the normal is zero. At x_1 the normal y_1=-B40 u40-v40 obeys

 <w40-v40,y_1>
     =[-B40^2 C40+Kappa40 t40]/b40^2=0,
 <u40-v40,y_1>=-B40(1-t40)C40/b40<0.                  (9)

To check the coefficient in the first equality without an omitted
identity, substitute v40 and the displayed inner products. Its t40
coefficient is

 D40(2B40+D40)+b40(D40-B40 D40+B40)-B40 b40^2
  =B40^2(b40-1)
      +(B40+b40+D40)(D40-B40(b40-1))
  =Kappa40,

where B40+b40+D40=s40. At every later query the normal is a
nonnegative multiple of w40, whose pairing with u40-w40 is zero and
whose pairing with v40-w40 is
-t40[1+(B40+D40)/b40]<0. This proves every normal in(8), not only the
two-step prefix. Formula(3) gives all ordered interpolation inequalities
including the optimum and the terminal sample. The initial distance to
the unique minimizer is exactly1.

By(4), the exact energy is

 V_h=1-A40^2 t40
     =-s40 Delta40/Kappa40,
 Delta40=B40 C40[1+(A40+1)B40]-D40.                    (10)

The last equality follows by expanding
A40^2 B40^2 C40-Kappa40=s40 Delta40, using A40=s40+1.
Thus Delta40<=0 is necessary in the triangle regime. We next show the
opposite sign throughout the entire larger-endpoint region.

Write

 Phi40(A,B)=B(A/2-B-3)[1+(A+1)B]-(A/2+1),

so Delta40=Phi40(A40,B40). For A>=197/20 and3/5<=B<=1,

 partial_A Phi40=(B-1)/2+B^2(A-B-5/2)
                  >=-1/5+(9/25)(127/20)=1043/500>0.

At A=197/20,

 Phi40(197/20,B)=B(77/40-B)(1+217B/20)-237/40,
 partial_B Phi40(197/20,B)
       =77/40+(15909/400)B-(651/20)B^2
       >=77/40+(2889/400)B>0.

Consequently

 Delta40>=Phi40(197/20,3/5)=909/20000>0.                (11)

This proves V_h<0 in all cases. There is also a convenient uniform
margin in the triangle regime: A40<12 gives s40>8, B40<1, C40<3,
D40<7 and0<Kappa40<80. Hence(10)--(11) give

 V_h<-909/200000<-1/250.                               (12)

No numerical test, sign-unchecked array, or restriction on the PSD
remainder has been used.

## 5. The smaller-endpoint region a40<8/5

Assume all global cuts pass and a40<8/5. Then

 157/40<=P40<25/6, 49/20<R40<13/5,
 29/4<T40<473/60<8.                                   (13)

Here is a direct derivation. From(6),
R40>=2P40(P40+1)/(3P40+4). The right side is strictly increasing,
since its derivative numerator is6P40^2+16P40+8>0. At P40=157/40 it
is30929/12620>49/20. Also R40<13/5. Therefore

 0<=delta40<=(13/5)(3P40+4)-2P40(P40+1).

The last polynomial decreases for P40>=157/40 and equals -7/45 at
P40=25/6, proving the upper bound on P40. The bounds on T40 follow
from T40=2P40+2-R40.

Define

 eta40=P40/T40, mu40=d40/P40, nu40=(T40-R40)/T40.

The rectangular bounds(13) imply

 1/2<eta40<3/5, 1/3<mu40<5/12, 3/5<nu40<7/10.          (14)

For eta40, use R40>2 and
T40-(5/3)P40=P40/3+2-R40>=17/24>0.
For mu40=1-R40/P40, the more precise bounds are
mu40>=53/157>1/3 and mu40<103/250<5/12.
For nu40, use R40/T40<=52/145<2/5 and
R40/T40>147/473>3/10.

Choose planar vectors

 u40=(1,0),
 v40=(-2eta40/3,sqrt(5)eta40/3),
 w40=(omega40,(2omega40-3d40/T40)/sqrt(5)),
 omega40=-d40/T40+(2/3)nu40 eta40,
 p40=w40+u40+v40, q40=w40+v40,
 K40=conv{p40,q40,w40}.

Thus

 ||u40||^2=1, ||v40||^2=eta40^2,
 z40=<u40,v40>=-2eta40/3,
 <w40,u40>=-d40/T40-nu40 z40,
 <w40,v40>=-mu40 eta40^2.                              (15)

There is no numerical parameter selection: the angle and all coefficients
are fixed exact expressions throughout the entire surviving region.

### 5.1 The triangle contains the true minimizer

Solving w40=-alpha40 u40-beta40 v40 in(15) gives

 beta40=3mu40-(4/5)nu40,
 alpha40=eta40[3mu40-(6/5)nu40].

Bounds(14) give

 11/25<beta40<77/100,
 0<alpha40<159/500<11/25<beta40.

In detail,3mu40-(6/5)nu40>1-21/25=4/25, and
alpha40<(3/5)(5/4-18/25)=159/500. Consequently

 0=alpha40 p40+(beta40-alpha40)q40+(1-beta40)w40

is a strictly positive convex combination. The vectors u40,v40 are
independent, so K40 is a nondegenerate triangle with0 in its interior.
Function F_K40 has the unique minimizer0 by Section2.

### 5.2 All six queries and all normal cones

Start at

 x_0=A40 w40+P40 u40+T40 v40                           (16)

and prescribe the gradients

 (g_0,...,g_5)=(p40,p40,q40,q40,w40,w40).               (17)

Their step-weighted sum is
P40 p40+(1+b40)q40+a40 w40, so the GD updates end exactly at x_5=w40.
The six normals y_i=x_i-g_i obtained from these updates are

 y_1=T40 w40+d40 u40+(T40-R40)v40, y_0=y_1+a40 p40,
 y_3=P40 w40+d40 v40,                  y_2=y_3+q40,
 y_4=a40 w40,                         y_5=0.           (18)

The unit step between the two q40 queries is retained exactly. There is
no deletion or equality assumption on any formal gradient coordinate.

A normal at p40 must pair nonnegatively with u40 and u40+v40; at q40 it
must pair nonpositively with u40 and nonnegatively with v40; at w40 it
must pair nonpositively with v40 and u40+v40. These two vertex tests at
each vertex are equivalent to the complete triangle normal cone.

Introduce the positive scalar quantities

 X40=d40 T40, Y40=R40(T40-P40),
 H1_40=Y40-(2/3)X40,
 H2_40=X40-(2/3)Y40.

Their exact signs follow from(13). As functions of P40,R40,

 H1_40=(-4P40^2-4P40+9P40 R40+10R40-5R40^2)/3.

Its P40 derivative is(-8P40-4+9R40)/3<0 and its R40 derivative is
(9P40+10-10R40)/3>0. Thus

 H1_40>=H1_40(25/6,49/20)=181/2160>0.                  (19)

The P40 derivative of H2_40 is4P40+2-(11/3)R40>0 and its R40 derivative
is-(11/3)P40-10/3+(10/3)R40<0. Hence

 H2_40>=H2_40(157/40,13/5)=9223/2400>0.                 (20)

Equations(15) and(18) now give

 <y_1,u40>=0, <y_1,v40>=(P40/T40^2)H1_40>0,
 <y_3,v40>=0, <y_3,u40>=-(P40/T40^2)H2_40<0.

For the additional p40-query normal, the required increments satisfy

 <p40,u40>
   =1-eta40[mu40+(2/3)(1-nu40)]>59/100>0,
 <p40,u40+v40>
   =1+eta40[-4/3-mu40+(2/3)nu40]+(1-mu40)eta40^2
   >1-(27/20)(3/5)=19/100>0.

For the additional q40-query normal,

 <q40,u40>=-eta40[mu40+(2/3)(1-nu40)]<0,
 <q40,v40>=(1-mu40)eta40^2>0.

For the two w40 queries,

 <w40,v40>=-mu40 eta40^2<0,
 <w40,u40+v40>
   =eta40[(2/3)nu40-mu40(1+eta40)]
   <eta40(7/15-1/2)<0.

These inequalities prove every normal in(18), so(17) is the ACTUAL
projection-gradient sequence of one static convex1-smooth function.
Every ordered interpolation row follows from(3), including all remote,
terminal and optimum rows. Set the actual values to
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2; no independent value repricing is involved.

The initial point is nonzero: p40 is a vertex of a nondegenerate triangle
having0 strictly inside, so p40!=0, while P_K40(0)=0. The just-proved
identity P_K40(x_0)=p40 would be impossible if x_0=0. Its true distance
to the unique minimizer is precisely the norm of the vector in(16).

### 5.3 Strict energy throughout the region

Since x_5=g_5=w40, equations(4),(15),(16) yield

 V_h=||A40 w40+P40 u40+T40 v40||^2-A40^2||w40||^2
     =(2P40/T40^2)E40,
 E40=delta40 T40-(2/3)P40 R40^2
     =4(P40+1)^2(2R40-P40)-(R40^2/3)(11P40+12).        (21)

For an explicit coefficient audit, the coefficients of ||u40||^2 and
||v40||^2 before substitution are(P40/T40)delta40 and
(T40/P40)delta40. The mixed contribution is
2P40(T40-A40 nu40)z40=2P40 R40^2 z40/T40. Substituting
||v40||^2=P40^2/T40^2 and z40=-2P40/(3T40) gives(21).

On the rectangle(13), E40 decreases with P40 and increases with R40:

 partial_P E40=-4(P40+1)(3P40+1-4R40)-(11/3)R40^2<0,
 partial_R E40=8(P40+1)^2-2R40((11/3)P40+4)>0.

For the first sign,3P40+1-4R40>=19/8>0. For the second,
P40>3, R40<3 and((11/3)P40+4)<20 bound the positive term below by128
and the subtracted term above by120. Thus

 E40<=E40(157/40,13/5)=-29951/48000<0.                  (22)

In fact2P40/T40^2>157/1280, and consequently

 V_h<-4702307/61440000<-3/40.                           (23)

This completes the remaining region, including every possible equality
in a global cut. It uses no zero-remainder condition, no terminal-square
restriction, and no symmetry or support assumption on Lambda.

## 6. One concrete fresh word with every global cut strict

Take

 h40_test=(3/2,49/20,1,49/20,3/2), A40=99/10.

The five global-cut slacks in(5), in chronological order, are

 (69/20,13/25,9801/400,13/25,69/20),

all strictly positive. The full-curvature product is0 because the middle
step is1. Neither check establishes a seed.

For this word the second triangle construction has

 P40=79/20, R40=5/2, T40=37/5,
 eta40=79/148, mu40=29/79, nu40=49/74,
 u40=(1,0), v40=(-79/222,79sqrt(5)/444),
 w40=(163/4107,-8353/(16428sqrt(5))),
 p40=w40+u40+v40, q40=w40+v40,
 x_0=(99/10)w40+(79/20)u40+(37/5)v40.

The explicit barycentric coefficients are
alpha40=2241/13690 and beta40=8353/14615. All queries, values, normals,
the unique minimum, and all original Q are supplied by(17)--(20), with
these rational/radical substitutions. Its exact negative energy is

 E40=-37831/3000,
 V_h=-(395*37831)/(2738*3000)
     =-2988649/1642800<0.                               (24)

It lies outside each old capacity10 radius10^-8 coordinate box: its
capacity differs by1/10, whereas five coordinate changes of that size
alter capacity by at most5*10^-8. It is a new counterexample at the
lower accepted sufficient capacity, not a candidate seed to recycle.

## 7. Scope, literature and continuation

This is a whole-family obstruction in the FULL original cone, not merely
failure of a selected multiplier factorization. Every contradiction is
an actual smooth-convex trajectory and therefore survives arbitrary
nonzero PSD remainders and all30 free multipliers. Every physical query
and every gradient coordinate is retained. Zero-step boundary words are
either rejected by(5) or are impossible under the surviving bounds.

The new result does NOT exclude asymmetric central-unit words, unit steps
at indices1 or3, any other five-step family, or general six-step words.
It does not provide
an improving seed, invoke the conditional lift, establish the original
minimax target, or give a general reflection theorem.

Two targeted Matlas searches returned unrelated results. Web search
recovered the known composability literature. The exact local source
Chikhi, arXiv2609.13765v2, Definition1 and the history/certificate proof
in Section4, was read. Its product rate identity and zero-remainder
constant-step certificate do not furnish an internal-unit seed. No
theorem from that source is imported here. The exact adjacent-pair and
two-switch projection mechanisms were recalled from shared awareness
and rederived in full, with no unverified predecessor used as truth.

Resources: zero numerical evaluations, solver calls, machine algebra,
DP/replay, fact_submit, installation, consult or native subagents.
This is the FIRST of the TWO analytic rounds allocated after completed39.
The next round must re-read TASK.md and current shared guidance; if the
lane is unchanged, use a genuinely asymmetric word or a different unit
position, apply original screens first, and keep the full R6 PSD cone.
Do not retune the reflected family just excluded. No renewal, service
shutdown, publication, finalization, or observed runtime-exit claim is made.

# A coupled gap between two original cut energies

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4 round30,
the second of the two analytical rounds selected by TASK.md and guidance
f7608aa358751108. No numerical evaluation, solver, replay, installation,
subordinate agent, consult or fact_submit is used. The only accepted
mathematical input is the original full certificate identity and full
primitive join in fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11. Every additional claim used below is
proved here. Earlier drafts are context, not correctness premises.

The new interface is quantitative: a negative two-step prefix cut cannot
be supported by an arbitrarily small repaired last-cut energy. The
original cross rows of M and their actual coefficient signs impose an
explicit gap. Keeping the earlier first-cut budget gives a closed,
piecewise solution of the resulting two-energy optimization, including
zero and saturated energies. This is not a sufficiency test for the
entire primitive cone.

Section6.1 strengthens this with an exact sum-of-squares identity and
classifies its ENTIRE equality face in the original twenty-sign cone.
That face has explicit full certificates, but all its capacities are
strictly below77/10<193/25. The threshold equality boundary is therefore
decided, including its remaining endpoint regimes, without reversal.

The original threshold seed and exact-profile target remain open. In
particular, this draft does NOT claim that the excluded energy strip
contains a point passing every previously published scalar/triangle
test. That additional independence question is separated from the
full-cone exclusion actually proved here.

## 1. Original space and notation

Initially assume

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, v>=0,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, Apre=1+a+b,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),
    Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2.                 (1)

The repaired-cut results assume b*t<=1, so ell>=0. No division by ell
will appear in the final test. When comparing with the earlier positive
excess regime, write d=v-1; that comparison explicitly assumes d>0.

Let P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j, s=P4=C-1, e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, and let e_i be the
five original standard coordinate columns. Set

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=D+sym(LT).

Full feasibility means Lambda_ii=0, all twenty Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j,
L^T e=omega, and M positive semidefinite on ALL of R^5. Here
sym(Z)=(Z+Z^T)/2. The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                   -s(s+1)||g4||^2.
                                                               (2)

This is a polynomial identity on every formal gradient and value after
